### 18.62 Step 59: high frame rates scoped (render-side interpolation; nothing implemented). Draws match between game frames by call chain, shader, textures and mesh (96-99% a frame in a level, the hub and a boss); every 3D draw factors into a world matrix times the game's camera, so a camera-only pass needs no matching; the intro's cut shows as a camera jump. The limit is xbox_d3d8's ~7.5 us a draw: four replays a tick don't fit until indexed draws stop creating two D3D11 buffers each.

Report: `step59_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\1bef32bb-...\scratchpad`), records, logs and analysis scripts there (`runs/`, `tiload.py`, `analyze.py`, `check2.py`, `dyn.py`). Two temporary builds, never committed (`step59_interp_records.patch`: a record per host draw with its guest call chain, shader, textures, target, mesh, vertex hash, camera, fixed-function matrices and the 192 program constants; `step59_draw_repeat.patch`: the host draw's time split and each draw issued N times). Source and the user's exe restored. Nothing in `xboxrecomp/` changed.

- **Task:** scoping only: up to 240 fps on the user's 240 Hz display by interpolating in the D3D bridge, the game's logic kept at its 60 Hz tick (references: the CTR Turbocharged decomp's scaled timing plus interpolated animation; RT64 in the Zelda recomps, renderer-side). 1. matching draws between frames and its reliability in the hub, a level and a boss; 2. what to interpolate and what not, cuts and teleports; 3. pacing, the game's timing and latency; 4. a staged plan with a setting, and the risks.
- **Recorded** (host-only, 640x480): the Arctic Antics attract demo (swaps 2000-2500, 388 draws a frame), the intro cutscene (1591-1830, 113), the hub walking and turning (2341-2640, 423), the hub's upstairs room (7801-8100, 876), Drain Damage (3826-4125, 587).
- **1. Matching.** Key: the draw entry and its call chain (return address + three callers, the motion log's walk), program or FVF, pixel shader, the four texture headers, the target, and the mesh (index pointer, stream 0 buffer, offset, base vertex, count). Matched a frame: demo 97.4% (worst 72.6%), boss 98.2% (91.3%), hub 96.0% (67.7%), upstairs 98.9% (89.9%), intro 93.5% (3.9% at the cut); matched pairs keep the draw order 98.7-100%.
  - The worst frames are draw-path switches: the same objects drawn through another function and program (demo 2324: 64 draws of `8E116565` via `0xA5ED2` out, 115 via `0xA614F`/`0xA8233` in; hub 2523: 101 out, 138 in, Crash standing). *(18.72: not detail levels, which the game doesn't have: Crash's spin is a rigid model, and the hub floor's reflections come and go as he moves.)*
  - Inside a key group, pairing by draw order picks a neighbour in 0.9% (demo) and 1.2% (hub) of pairs (a ring of identical objects whose order shifts); by nearest world position: 0.03% and 0.13%, 0 in the boss.
  - Animated vertex data, 14-68 matched draws a frame: mostly the particle system (immediate mode, `0x385DE` <- `0x39538`/`0x39504` <- `0x34FEA`, FVF 0x142, 128-vertex batches). Slots are reused (vertex 0 jumps 4.6-8.6 units a tick in the boss's rain), so it can't be interpolated per vertex.
- **2. What moves.** `0x242620` is the camera's world matrix and every fixed-function view its inverse; `0x242398` the projection. Program constants c96-c99 (XDK c0-c3) are (World View Proj) transposed, and World = M^T P^-1 Cam factored for every 3D draw in the five windows. The camera's position is also in c116 (level and hub programs) or c103 (Drain Damage's). Bones from c124 (XDK c28), three registers each, world-space 3x4 rows. The constant shadow keeps whatever earlier draws set, so only the registers a program reads may be compared (its microcode's read mask). Not interpolated: the 2D quads (`0x39901`), the render-target passes with their own projection, particles (redrawn at the new frame's positions under the interpolated camera); CopyRects and clears are replayed in order. Camera per tick: at most 0.38 units and 9.4 deg outside the cut; the intro's cut 11.3 units and 150 deg. Rule: over ~2 units or ~30 deg, or under half the draws matched, holds the new frame; an object stepping over ~1.5 units snaps.
- **3. Pacing and timing.** The game ticks trunc(elapsed x 60 - counted) a frame, never below 1 (18.48), so the game thread must still leave Swap at 59.94 Hz. Plan: inside the game's Swap, four sub-frames at DXGI interval 1 (t = 1/4 .. 1), each after the frame-latency wait and the mid-refresh hold; 2 at 120 Hz; other rates later. Latency: the first sub-frame shows a quarter of the new frame when the new frame shows today, the whole of it 12.5 ms later: about 6 ms (3/8 of a tick) on average; the 2-deep present queue drops from up to 33 ms to 8 ms.
- **Cost.** The host draw call is 7.3-7.5 us a draw inside xbox_d3d8 (twoc's state calls 0.47 us): 3.4 ms a frame in the demo, 4.3 in the boss, 6.4 upstairs. Indexed draws (~90%) median 5.6-8.6 us, non-indexed 1.4-3.7: the two immutable buffers xbox_d3d8 creates per indexed UP draw are ~4.4 us. Each draw issued N times, the demo at native 3840x2160 16:9: 1x 60 fps 31.8 W; 2x 57-60 fps (1.35 ms spare) 45 W; 4x 36-49 fps 64 W, the GPU ~39% busy. CPU-bound, not the GPU.
- **Plan** (setting `framerate = 60 | display`, `RECOMP_FRAMERATE`, Display tab; off = today's path): 0. the cost: indexed UP draws through a ring buffer and a sampler cache in xbox_d3d8 (perf only, identical output; **needs the user's decision**: 18.32 limits xbox_d3d8 changes to translator bugs), and each program's constant read mask; 1. record the frame's host draws and replay them at Swap (one sub-frame = identical); 2. camera-only interpolation, 2x/4x, cut detection; 3. objects: matching, world matrices, bones, fixed-function worlds, teleport snaps; 4. if needed: draws tagged with the guest object pointer at the model-draw functions (what the Zelda recomps' patches give RT64), particle identity, a render thread, other refresh rates.
- **Risks:** the CPU time; wrong or missing matches (an object sliding between instances, or moving at 60 Hz for a tick); camera-tied effects through constants not identified yet; CPU-built geometry such as Crash's stencil shadow staying at 60 Hz beside an interpolated Crash (not checked); the HUD and menus (3D with their own camera) weren't on screen in these windows; ~6 ms more latency; the post filters and MSAA resolves run per sub-frame.
- **A user ini:** `twoc\build\Release\crash_twoc.ini` (Oct 8 00:57: fullscreen, widescreen, native, pause off) left alone; the runs used exe copies in scratch.
- **Files changed:** this entry and "Start here tomorrow".

### 18.63 Step 60: frame interpolation, stages 0 and 1. xbox_d3d8's indexed draws go through ring buffers and its samplers are cached (local, perf only): the host draw call in the heaviest room goes from ~7.1 to 1.4 us. The mirror can record each frame and replay it at the present (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY`); an in-run check found no differing pixel in 33,000 frames. Off: identical.

Report: `step60_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\1bef32bb-...\scratchpad`), runs in `runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: `xboxrecomp` `258cb8d` (local, never pushed; the 18.63 rule refinement); repo `1fd7d62` (stage 0), `906708a` (stage 1), plus the notes commit.

- **Decision before the step:** perf-only changes in xbox_d3d8 with identical output approved, local and never pushed (the 18.63 rule refinement). Task: stages 0 and 1 of the 18.62 plan: 0. indexed UP draws through a ring buffer (vertex and index) and a sampler cache in xbox_d3d8, aiming at ~2 us a draw, and each vertex program's constant read mask from its microcode, the per-draw time and the upstairs room's frame time measured before and after; 1. record and replay behind a setting, one sub-frame identical to today, off untouched. Bridge off and the default run identical; a commit per stage.
- **Use:** `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY=1` records and replays (`=N`: N replays a frame, a cost check). `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY_CHECK=1` draws each frame at once and again from its recording and compares them (`[D3DB] host replay check: N frames compared, D differ`; with `-DumpDir` the first differing frames as `hostchkSWAP_once.bmp`/`_replayed.bmp`). Both are switches for the next stages; stage 2 adds the player's setting (`framerate`).
- **The upstairs room**, reproducible now: new game WOMBAT, skip the intro, steer Crash onto the hub's central lift (0, 0): he rides up to y ~37 in ~5 s (scratch `upstairs2.py`; 18.62's route there was luck). ~957 draws a frame.
- **Stage 0 (`258cb8d`, `1fd7d62`).**
  - xbox_d3d8: DrawIndexedPrimitiveUP's vertices into the UP ring (4 -> 16 MB) and its indices into a new 2 MB index ring, no buffer created per draw; sampler objects kept by description (up to 64), not made and released for each stage of each draw; a program's microcode hash taken when its slot is filled, not at every draw.
  - twoc: `[D3DB] host draw calls this interval:` (draws, time, per draw, per frame); `d3dh_vs_consts` and a `[D3DB] host program` line per program: the constant registers it reads directly and the lowest base it reads through a0.x. Upstairs: `8D225D6D` reads 19 registers and from c124 relative (the bones), `A98754DF` from c66, `8C42BA36` and `1C743958` from c25: 18.62's c25-c56 "changed on every draw" is a table read through a0.x.
  - Upstairs: the host draw call ~7.1 us (6.6-6.7 in xbox_d3d8 + 0.43 state) -> **1.40 us**; the mirror's draw time 9.9 -> 4.3 ms a frame; the limiter's wait (spare) 1.1 -> 7.0 ms a frame.
- **Stage 1 (`906708a`).** With the switch, `d3dh_draw_up`, `d3dh_clear`, `d3dh_copy_back` and `d3dh_memory_freed` record into the frame's command list (a chunked arena, reused) and `d3dh_present` runs it first. A draw owns copies of its vertices, indices, states, fixed-function matrices and lights, pixel shader, program and constants, texture descriptions; its textures are fetched through the cache when recorded (the same sequence of fetches as before) and held to the replay, except a texture whose Data is a host render target or is drawn, cleared or copied into earlier in the frame: that is fetched at the replay, where the target is drawn. Off: the same functions (`draw_exec`, `clear_exec`, `copy_exec`, `freed_exec`) run at once, as before. Upstairs: recording 0.31 us a draw (0.30 ms a frame, ~963 commands, ~8.7 MB), one replay 1.28 ms a frame (spare 6.80 ms against 6.95); **four replays 4.93 ms a frame, 60 fps with 3.2 ms spare**.
- **The replay check needs a snapshot.** Drawn twice in a row, a frame that reads a render target it doesn't clear started its second drawing from the first's result: 244 of 3,900 frames of the demo differed in 1-42 pixels (up to 7 levels) on one moving object. The check now keeps the back buffer's and every target's colour at each frame's start and puts it back before the replay (a target made during the frame cleared black, as its creation leaves it); depth and stencil aren't kept (cleared before use in every frame seen), nor MSAA surfaces (check without MSAA).
- **Checks.**

Stage 1 alone: 7,887, 0 differ.

### 18.64 Step 61: frame interpolation, stage 2. "Frame rate: Display (interpolated)" in the Esc overlay draws each game frame 4 times at 239.98 Hz with the camera moved partway from the frame before (fixed-function views and program c96-c99 recomposed from the camera at `0x242620`), cuts shown as they are; 59.9 fps with every sub-frame one refresh apart in the heaviest room. Objects still move at 60 Hz. Off: identical.

Report: `step61_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\c8fde94e-...\scratchpad`), runs and sub-frame dumps in `runs/`, identity runs in `id/`, exe copies `base/` (step 60) and `s2/`. Commit: repo `53e8ed9`, plus the notes commit. Nothing in `xboxrecomp/` changed.

- **Task:** stage 2 of the 18.62 plan: two recorded frames, sub-frames at t = 1/k .. 1 (k = 2 at 120 Hz, 4 at 240 Hz) with the camera at `0x242620` interpolated (position lerp, rotation slerp, projection lerp), fixed-function views from it, program draws recomposed from their factored world (c96-c99), the camera-position registers the read masks find; cut detection (over ~2 units or ~30 deg a tick, or under half the draws matched); 2D, targets, copies and particles as recorded; the setting "Frame rate: game (60) / display (interpolated)" (`framerate = 60 | display`), live, sub-frames at DXGI interval 1 inside the game's Swap. Verify: off identical; sub-frame dumps in the hub turning, a level, the intro's cut, H2 Oh No, the pause screen; frame times at 240 Hz upstairs.
- **Use:** Esc, Display, "Frame rate" (help line: "Now: 4 frames each game frame, 239.98 fps", or why not); `RECOMP_FRAMERATE=display`. Works when the pacing is the display's vsync (auto or vsync) at 2, 3 or 4 x 59.94 Hz; with the limiter or another rate, one frame each game frame as before. Applied at the end of the present it was changed in.
- **How** (`d3d_host.c`, "Step 61: frame interpolation"): d3d_bridge.c's `host_draw` gives every host draw the camera as it was made (`0x242620` and the projection `0x242398`, 32 floats). At record time each draw is classed: fixed-function 3D into the screen with V Cam = I (its view becomes V Cam Cam(t)^-1, its projection P(t) where it was P); program draws into the screen that read c96-c99 and factor with the camera (W = WVP P^-1 Cam affine: c96-c99 become (WVP P^-1 Cam Cam(t)^-1 P(t))^T, and registers equal to the camera position, read directly or in the a0.x-relative range, Cam(t)'s); the rest as recorded. Cameras are paired between frames (identical first, then nearest, most used first, only those 3D draws used); the main camera decides cuts; draws are matched by kind (program or FVF, pixel shader, textures, target, primitive, counts). The last sub-frame is the recorded frame. While interpolating the visible swap chain has 4 buffers and 4 frames of latency, and sub-frames go when the queue has room.
- **Found on the way:**
  - Each frame starts with the frame before's camera still at `0x242620` (the render-target passes and 2D draw under it; the 3D draws come after the update). Paired by value, it took the real camera's partner and sub-frames spanned two ticks; the t = 0 check showed it. Only cameras 3D draws used are paired now.
  - The 18.48 mid-refresh hold on every sub-frame kept the game in its Swap 3+ refreshes after its own ~8 ms: 56 fps upstairs (11% of sub-frames shown twice); a deeper queue alone 53.5 fps. Without the hold, queue 4/4: 598-600 frames/10 s, 2,392 presents/10 s, none shown twice (repeat 596-599). Queues 2/2 and 3/3 were less steady (492-600, 549-600).
  - Drain Damage's Wa-Wa program `A4AE6206` has the camera position in c103 inside its a0.x-relative range (not in its direct read mask); the recorded hub, castle and H2 Oh No frames had none among the registers read directly (18.62 found c116 by value, not by read mask).
  - H2 Oh No's underwater layer (144 draws into the 640x480 target, program `DE6E4FA2`) reads only c112, c113, c191: screen-space vertices, so it stays at the game frame's camera.
- **Checks:**

Verify: off identical; the t = 0 check extended to objects; sub-frame strips of Crash running and turning in the hub and a level; upstairs still 600 frames/10 s at 240 Hz.
- **How** (`d3d_host.c`, "Step 62: objects"; `d3d_bridge.c` `host_draw`): every host draw carries a guest mesh id (`d3dh_draw_t.mesh`: index address, stream-0 address with the base vertex, count; UP: vertex pointer and count; immediate: 0). A draw stage 2 interpolates (IC_VP, IC_FF*) with a mesh is an object: key = stage 2's kind + mesh + ZWRITE, blend, CULL, stencil, COLORWRITE; its world W (the factored (WVP) P^-1 Cam, or an affine fixed-function world); for a program reading relative to a0.x from c124, bones c124-c189 (22 x 3 registers, rows of a world-space 3x4). At the present each object takes the frame before's nearest unpaired object of its key (W's origin + bone 0's); over 1.5 units it snaps, none: camera-only. Sub-frame t: W(t) and B(t) slerped / lerped (element by element when not rotation and scale), c96-c99 = (W(t) W^-1 WVP C)^T, the fixed-function world W(t). Bones with a register the program reads directly (c172-c181, lights) are left alone, and so is one stepping over 6 units (4 x the snap; unread registers hold other draws' leftovers). `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_OBJECTS=units` (0: camera only); `[D3DB] host interpolation objects:` line; interp records carry the mesh (field 11).

### 18.69 Step 66: Coco's arm with interpolation on, and the warp portal's lightning. Coco's arms stretched for the in-between sub-frames of a jump's first frame, and her pigtails and Crash's limbs in spins: each bone was slerped on its own about the model's origin, so two bones stopped agreeing on the joint they share once they turned far in one tick (the jump animation taking over: 98 degrees; spins: up to 179). A skinned object's bones are now blended in a frame that moves with its skeleton, which keeps every joint together. The portal's lightning was misplaced at 16:9 and 21:9 only: part of it is 2D the game projected with the widened projection, and the bridge pillarboxed it as 4:3 art. The game's own projections now stretch with the 3D. Off: identical.

Report: `step66_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\2824b898-...\scratchpad`), images in `report66/`, runs and sub-frame dumps in `runs/`, identity runs in `id/`, exe copies `base/` (step 65), `c1/` (fix 1), `c2/` (both). - **Task (the user's playtest):** 1. Coco's model glitching with frame interpolation on: her arm for ~3 frames at a jump's start (`twoc/reference/playtest/coco glitched arm when jumping.png`), and something similar running and spinning; find the draws and bones (an animation change, bones moving relative to each other under the snap limit, or a wrong partner) and fix it; check Crash and the enemies. 2. The warp portal's lightning bolts misaligned when entering or finishing a level (`misaligned lightning on warp portal.png`), also with interpolation off: compare 4:3 with the wide ratios, render sizes, filters off and xemu, and fix it. Sub-frame dumps of Coco jumping, running and spinning, portal grabs, off identical, a commit per fix.
- **1. The skeletons (`53732d7`).**
  - The screenshot is `garden` (level table index 12; scratch `lt.py` prints the table), not the hub. Reproduced at 240 Hz (scratch `lv.sh`: a level, then pad lines, `INTERP_REC` and sub-frame dump triggers): at the jump's first frame sub-frames 1-3 show both arms long and wavy, the 4th (recorded) is normal; spinning, the pigtails splay.
  - The draws: Coco's body meshes (program `8D225D6D`; `7FB99240`, `E58D95DE`, `5A467477` and the arms' and hair's), each paired with its right partner (not the mirror or the shadow). At the jump the animation switches and her bones turn 64-98 degrees in one tick, their origins stepping 0.2-0.8 units: under both snaps (1.5 units an object, 6 a bone).
  - The cause: a bone slerped and lerped on its own turns about the point it carries the model's origin to, not about its joint, so neighbouring bones place the joint they share apart in between. `tools/diag/interp_joints.py` finds the joints on recorded frames (the bind point two bones map to one place in both frames) and measures it: idle and walking 0.002-0.03 units; Coco's jump start 0.196; running 0.1-0.17 on some frames; spins 0.29-6.2 (near 180 degrees each bone took its own way round); Crash in Arctic Antics 0.1-0.57 and 4.9 in a spin; the attract demo (Crash and enemies) up to 0.087.
  - Tried offline first: bones relative to one reference bone, each slerped (worse on ordinary frames: 0.19-1.1); a skeleton tree from the shared joints, each bone turned about its joint (unread registers hold earlier draws' bones, 116 values for one character, and the tree joined wrong bones).
  - The fix: skinned objects holding one identical moving, non-flat bone form a skeleton; its frame G is the bone most of them hold, slerped and lerped as before; each bone B(t) = G(t) ((1 - t) Ga^-1 Ba + t Gb^-1 Bb). Linear in the bones, every shared point stays shared whatever G is (gaps 0.001-0.003 on every frame above, the solver's noise); G takes the body's turn, so only a limb's swing relative to it goes straight, a little short halfway (95th percentile 0-0.1 units, up to 0.5 on an animation switch). Flat bones (the stencil shadow's) are left one by one. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_SKELETONS=0` is the old way; the objects line adds `skeletons N (bones blended in them N a sub-frame)`.
  - Checked by sub-frame dumps: Coco's jump start (the arms in between, not stretched), her spins (pigtails attached in every sub-frame), Crash running, jumping and spinning in Arctic Antics. Cost: replays 3.56 against 3.53 ms a frame, recording 0.46 against 0.42.

  - The fix: pre-transformed draws in a display-list flush other than the overlay pass (`d3dh_draw_t.world_list`) stretch with the 3D. ### 18.70 Step 67: enemies shaking in Banzai Bonsai with interpolation on. Step 66 made one skeleton of every character in the level: a skinned draw uploads only its own bones before it, and the rest of its bone registers hold earlier draws' (another character's); two draws holding the same leftover joined their characters, so the enemies' bones were blended in one other character's frame and swung away and back between recorded frames (a gong enemy turned edge-on as Coco spun beside it). A draw's bones are now only those it uploaded: one skeleton a character. Off: identical.

Report: `step67_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\3a2f09eb-...\scratchpad`), images in `report67/`, runs and sub-frame dumps in `runs/`, identity runs in `id/`, exe copies `base/` (step 66) and `c1/` (the fix). Commits: repo `fb60e1a`, plus the notes commit. Nothing in `xboxrecomp/` changed.

- **Task (the user's playtest):** with Frame rate: Display, in Banzai Bonsai (`garden`) the enemies jitter and shake while Coco runs around and while she stands and spins (`twoc/reference/playtest/coco spinning character model jitter shake.png`). Reproduce with sub-frame dumps (running past enemies, spinning beside them), compare `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_SKELETONS=0` and `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_OBJECTS=0`, fix it, check Coco, Crash and the enemies in Arctic Antics and the hub. Off identical, commit.
- **Reproduced** (scratch `lv.sh`, level 12 via pad 1:1): Coco running from the start into the gong enemy and the stork, and standing at the start spinning twice with the gong enemy behind her. In the spin's sub-frames 1-3 the gong turns edge-on and the stork's beak disappears, back on the recorded 4th (`report67/spin_base.png`).
- **Narrowed:** skeletons off, the gong holds still (`spin_skeletons_off.png`); camera only, nothing moves in between. `tools/diag/interp_shake.py` (sub-frame path less the direct change, per tile; camera still) for the spin: base 4777, skeletons off 3831, the fix 3754, camera only 813. So step 66.
- **The cause:** scratch `grp.py` replays step 66's grouping on `INTERP_REC` frames. In every frame of the run one skeleton held 80 of 97 skinned draws, 40 meshes, 26 units apart (Coco, the stork, the gong enemies, two of one kind 15 units apart); G was one character's bone, and the others' bones sat 0.1-0.58 units off their own per-bone blend at t = 1/2. Arctic Antics: 58 draws, 29 units. The link: step 66 took every bone register the program reads (`bmask`: c124 + 3b read relatively, 22 bones). A temporary log of `SetVertexShaderConstant` (`0x102AA0`, from `0x3AD82`) beside the draw log shows each skinned draw uploading its own palette just before it (c124, c127, ..., one to twelve bones, plus c169 which the program reads directly), so its other registers are leftovers, and two draws after the same character held the same leftover values.
- **The fix (`fb60e1a`):** `d3d_bridge.c` marks the constant slots `SetVertexShaderConstant` writes (reg + 96) and hands them to the next program draw (`d3dh_vprog_t.fresh`), then clears them. `ic_obj` keeps in `bmask` only the bones the draw uploaded (`own`), so skeletons and partners go by a draw's own bones; a skinned draw that uploaded none keeps the old mask and joins no skeleton (none seen: 11,207 skinned draws in the Banzai Bonsai run, all with their own bones; ~11 leftover bones a draw now ignored). `INTERP_REC` records the uploaded-bone mask in bits 8-29 of the flags word.
- **After:** one skeleton a character: Coco 27 draws (19 meshes, spread 0.2-0.4 units), each enemy its own (9 or 15 draws, deviation from the per-bone blend 0.000-0.003); Crash in Arctic Antics 47 draws (spread 1.0, his body's meshes), alone. Sub-frame dumps: the gong enemy still and smooth through both spins, Coco's pigtails attached (`spin_fix.png`); Crash running, breaking a crate and spinning in Arctic Antics and spinning in the hub, with the hub's Coco steady (`arctic_crash_spin_fix.png`, `hub_crash_spin_fix.png`).
- **Checks:** off identical (`drive.py` 150 s, bridge off and host-only, exe copies, no ini; step 66's build against the fix): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]` lines, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, the host device lines (the dump path aside), the first 8 host dumps byte-identical.
- **Not checked:** bosses; enemies running alongside Coco by eye (the running run's numbers only: the same grouping as the spin); other levels' enemies.

### 18.72 Step 69: model detail and draw distance. The game has no level-of-detail system: one version of each model, and the draws step 59 saw switching path are Crash's spin model and the hub floor's reflections. The draw distance is the level's far plane, with fog that ends inside it; "Draw distance" in the Esc overlay multiplies the far plane, the fog and the sky (1x, 1.5x, 2x, 3x), and open levels show more of themselves. Crates, fruit and enemies keep the game's own distances. Off: identical. **Removed on request in 18.94 (step 91):** the setting, its key and variable, and the fog and sky wrappers (sub_000956B0's back to its widescreen-only form).

### 18.73 Step 70: every controller, through SDL3 next to XInput. SDL 3.4.18 (the 2026 Steam Controller's own driver) reads every pad that isn't an Xbox pad, all of them joined to the game's one controller by position; Xbox pads still go through XInput, bit for bit as before. A hand-written XGetDeviceChanges gives hot-plug: a pad plugged in arms the slot, the last one unplugged disarms it, and the game shows its own "please reconnect the controller" screen. The Controls tab chooses SDL or XInput only, lists the pads with a live test, names inputs as the pad does, and binds the extra buttons (Steam Controller grips, trackpad clicks). No controller, no ini: identical.

Report: `step70_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\3ed62a78-...\scratchpad`), grabs in `report70/`, runs in `runs/` and `lvl/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`, exe copies `base/` (step 69), `ca/` (commit A alone), `cb/` (A + B), `c1/` (final). Commits: repo `5966159` (items 1-2), `cd76778` (item 3), `5eece89` (item 4), plus the notes commit (item 5's tool). Nothing in `xboxrecomp/` changed.

- **Task:** SDL3 (a version with the 2026 Steam Controller outside Steam; its version logged) for Xbox, DualShock/DualSense, Switch Pro, Steam Controller and Steam Deck, hot-plugged, rumble through SDL; SDL pads joined to the poll with XInput and the keyboard, the Xbox default exactly today's, PlayStation and Switch by position with their names in the Controls tab, the Steam Controller's extra inputs bindable; "connected" consulting SDL, a pad plugged in later arming the slot and one removed disarming it; a setting "SDL (all pads) / XInput only", saved, live, with the pads' names and types and a controller test; a pad seen twice read once, with `[PAD]` lines; tests with SDL virtual joysticks and with no controller. A commit per item.
- **1. SDL3 (`5966159`).**
  - 3.4.18 (Oct 2 2026, `SDL-release-3.4.18-0-g829a65d76`) is the newest stable. The 2026 controller ("Triton", 28DE:1302-1305) has its own HIDAPI driver (`SDL_hidapi_steam_triton.c`, on by default; rumble fixed in 3.4.10), and 3.4.18 added `SDL_GAMEPAD_TYPE_STEAM` (the Steam Deck reports it too, PID 1205).
  - Fetched, not vendored: `twoc/CMakeLists.txt` FetchContent from the release tarball (URL and SHA-256 pinned, into `twoc/build/_deps`; offline `-DFETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_SDL3=`), built static with joystick, haptic, HIDAPI and sensor only (audio, video, GPU, render, camera, power, dialog, tray off): no DLL. The source is 53 MB, and the prebuilt VC package is a DLL plus an import library, both gitignored types here.
  - `twoc/src/pads.c` runs SDL on a thread of its own: `SDL_Init(SDL_INIT_GAMEPAD)` with `SDL_HINT_TIMER_RESOLUTION=0` (SDL asks Windows for 1 ms timers by default, which would change every Sleep in the game), XInput on and HIDAPI-Xbox, RawInput, WGI and GameInput off, so every Xbox pad is an SDL "XInput#N" device; those are skipped (read through XInput, below). Motion and button events are switched off (state is read; the queue carries plug and unplug). Each gamepad is opened on `SDL_EVENT_GAMEPAD_ADDED` and closed on removal; its buttons, triggers (0-32767 to 0-255, rounded) and sticks (y negated) are read every 2 ms into a snapshot the game thread copies without waiting on SDL; 50 ms with no pad. XInput users are looked at every second and on SDL's joystick events (`XInputGetCapabilities`, VID/PID from `XInputGetCapabilitiesEx`, ordinal 108). `[PAD]` lines: `SDL 3.4.18 (...) started (built with 3.4.18)`, each pad with its type, ids and how it is read.
  - Rumble: the vibration override (`sub_0015431A`, called by the game every frame) still sets XInput user 0 and now also every other pad read: SDL pads with `SDL_RumbleGamepad` (1 s, sent again every 0.5 s while it lasts), XInput users 1-3 directly. `[XPP] port 0 vibration on / off (peak)` per rumble.
- **2. The poll (`5966159`).**
  - Port 0 = XInput user 0 as before, plus every SDL pad and (SDL mode) XInput users 1-3, joined in `controls_apply_pad(g, more)`: buttons at their highest, each stick from the pad pushing it furthest, then the map. No other pad and the default map: the state is left as it is (the step 57 rule), so an Xbox pad alone is exactly today's.
  - By position (SDL's gamepad API is positional): south = A, east = B, west = X, north = Y, shoulders Black/White, triggers, Start/Back, stick clicks, d-pad. A Switch pad's B (bottom) is A.
  - Extra PC inputs (`controls.h` SRC_: guide, four paddles, touchpad click, misc 1-6), each pressing one Xbox button (`extra_<input>` in the ini). Defaults: the paddles (SDL's right 1, left 1, right 2, left 2: a Steam Controller's or Deck's R4 L4 R5 L5) as X, B, A, Y, so the grips spin, crouch, jump; the rest unbound. A Steam pad's stick and grip touch sensors (misc 3-6) are hidden.
  - With no XInput user 0 seen by the pads thread the poll no longer asks XInput for it every frame.
  - Test only: `RECOMP_DEV_PAD_FILE=<file>` plugs SDL virtual pads into the game's own process from a one-line file (`ps5 a=1`, `steam paddle1=1`, `none`; kinds xbox ps4 ps5 switch steam deck generic; the virtual pad's rumble logged), `RECOMP_DEV_FAKE_XINPUT=U:VID:PID` fakes an XInput user's ids.
- **3. Hot-plug (`cd76778`).**
  - The game's pump `sub_000EB6C0` (every frame) asks `XGetDeviceChanges` (`sub_00154AAD`, stdcall, `type, *insertions, *removals`) for the gamepad type `0x1534AC` and the memory units `0x153430`, closes and clears a removed port's slot (`sub_0015429F`, slot +0 = 0) and calls the insertion handler `sub_000EB5A0` for an inserted one. The type object's +4 "changed" flag is written only by the USB enumeration this recomp never runs (18.16), so it always said nothing changed.
  - Hand-written: port 0 for the gamepad type, from the open's own test (XInput user 0, any other pad, the keyboard, the dev switches) against what the game was last told; nothing for every other type and port (the lifted answer). Silent until `sub_000EB270`'s one-shot prime has run, so a run starts as before. A port gone is reported after 1 s gone (a drop-out, or SDL restarting on a setting change, keeps the slot). Unarmed, XInput user 0 is looked for through the pads thread, or every 2 s in XInput-only mode.
  - The open (`dev_port_connected`) counts any other pad as connected: a Steam Controller alone arms port 0 at the prime or when plugged in later.
  - Unplugged while playing, the game shows its own "PLEASE RECONNECT THE CONTROLLER AND PRESS START TO CONTINUE" over the pause menu (never reachable before: a slot could never disarm); replugged, Start clears it and leaves the pause menu.
- **4. The Controls tab (`5eece89`).**
  - "Controller input: SDL (all pads) / XInput only" (`controller_input = sdl | xinput`, `RECOMP_CONTROLLER_INPUT`; sdl, the default, unwritten), live: SDL's thread started or stopped. XInput only is the old path: SDL never starts, port 0 is XInput user 0 and the keyboard.
  - The pads there: name, type, how read (`XInput #N`, `SDL`), ids, or why left out; under each a live view (every input's name lit while pressed, triggers and sticks as numbers); SDL's version.
  - The PC column is named as the pad last pressed names its inputs (Xbox, PlayStation, Nintendo, Steam Controller, Steam Deck, other), e.g. "Circle", "B (bottom)", "R4 (upper right grip)". The "press the new button" wait takes every pad, and an extra button pressed there binds it to that Xbox button; "Extra buttons" lists the ones the pads there have with a drop-down each; "Reset controller to defaults" resets them too.
  - Double pads: SDL leaves Steam Input's virtual pad (28DE:11FF) out of its own list; XInput doesn't, so an XInput user that is one is left out while any other pad is read and read again when none is (`[PAD] XInput #0 (Steam Input's virtual pad): left out, another pad is read`).
- **5. Checks** (SDL virtual pads; `twoc/tools/diag/vpads.py`; keyboard off unless said):
  - No controller, no ini, identical (`id.sh base c1`, `drive.py` 150 s, bridge off and host-only): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]` lines, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, host lines the same (dump path aside), the first 8 host dumps byte-identical.
  - A DualSense from the start: armed at the prime, Cross started the game, Start skipped the intro. Plugged in at the menu: armed then; unplugged in the hub's cutscene: closed, the game ran on; a Switch Pro plugged in: armed, its bottom button skipped the cutscene. 0.5 s unplugged: kept; 3 s: closed; a Steam Controller: armed.
  - Arctic Antics with a Steam Controller: R4 spins, R5 jumps, L4 crouches, the trackpad click does nothing; the game's rumble (a left-motor ramp from ~27,000 to 0 over ~0.5 s) reached the pad.

  - Commit A alone (no hot-plug): an SDL pad's input reaches the game when the keyboard arms port 0, but alone it doesn't arm (item 3's problem).
  - Cost: with a pad the pads thread takes ~1.2% of a core (11.7 ms/s at 500 Hz, `perfmon.py --cpu`); without one next to nothing.

### 18.74 Step 71: reverb, scoped (nothing implemented). Crash uses one effect, the I3DL2 reverb, through five presets of its own chosen by level and by stretch of level; every 3D voice feeds it, nothing else does; preset 0 (menus, intro, attract demos, about half the levels) is silent. The image's XTalk also runs on the HRTF bins, and the game's HEADPHONES option unplugs it. xemu (use_dsp) gives no measurable reverb under any preset. xemu's DSP core is GPL, but the MIT dsp56300 JIT now under it is not: option A (run the image) is recommended, staged from an offline harness, with a native I3DL2 reverb as the fallback.

No code commit (scoping only); tools in the notes commit. Nothing in `xboxrecomp/` changed (a temporary tap was built into an exe copy and reverted).

- **Task:** scoping only: 1. what Crash sends the DSP (the image download, SetEffectData/I3DL2 parameters per level and room, which voices feed the reverb send, other effects), per scene; 2. xemu (use_dsp, a config copy) against ours on the same scenes: the reverb's level, decay, which sounds feed it; 3. options with size, risk, fidelity: A emulate the DSP56300 and run `dsstdfx.bin` (with the GPL question and the licence of the APU code already in xboxrecomp), B a native I3DL2 reverb from the game's parameters, C in between; 4. a recommendation with stages, an Audio-tab on/off and amount, and its effect on the 3D-bin fits of 18.58/18.59 and the music/effects balance.
- **1. The image.** `Crashdat\sfx\dsstdfx.bin` (25,256 bytes, the XDK's standard image, 10 effects, descriptor at 0x6110; `tools/diag/fximg.py`), loaded once by the sound manager's init `sub_000B99D0` (`0xB99EF`) through `IDirectSound_DownloadEffectsImage` (`0x136605`, image location I3DL2 0, crosstalk 1). DSOUND keeps it in contiguous memory at `0x80BC8000`, descrambles the code in place (plain 24-bit DSP56300 words afterwards), writes its 355-word loader into the first 0x800 bytes, relocates the states and posts GP command 3 at image + 0x810 = `0x80BC8810`, the doorbell `sub_001391DE` acknowledges (18.25). The GP window (P and X memory, which the descriptor names) stays all zero: on hardware the loader DMAs them in. fx0 I3DL2 reverb (bin 10 in, four buffers X:0xCC0-0xD20 out; 1,211 code words; 380 KB of delay lines in system RAM), fx1 XTalk (bins 6-9 to FL FR BL BR), fx2-fx7 effects on FX sends 12-23, fx8 a second reverb on FXSEND0, fx9 the speaker bins' copy. The `DOLBY` section (0x945760) is the EP's AC-3 encoder, unused.
- **1. What the game calls** (every call from `.text` into DSOUND identified): `sub_000B9700` (the manager `0x427A58`) sets a preset when it differs from `[+0x18]`: a DSI3DL2LISTENER to `IDirectSound_SetI3DL2Listener` (`0x13753A`), a listener factor (`0x137516`: 1.0, 1.5 for preset 2, 0.05 for 5) and its own distance `[+0x14]` (2500, 900, 40000). `sub_000AA330` picks it by level `[0x19C068]` and, in some levels, by Crash's path section and point (`0x8F5120` +0x48/+0x4A; `tools/diag/revtab.py`), at a level's start (`0x88130`) and every 31 frames (`0x88406`), not while `[0x427A50]` is set (a stream call; probably speech). Menus, title and intro never run it. `sub_000B9460`, the pause menu's HEADPHONES option, calls `IDirectSound_EnableHeadphones` (`0x136CED`), which zeroes XTalk's header and I/O pointers. Never called: SetEffectData, SetI3DL2Source, FX-send mixbins.
- **1. The presets** (lRoom, lRoomHF, DecayTime, HFRatio, lReflections, ReflectionsDelay, lReverb, ReverbDelay; all RoomRolloff 0, Diffusion/Density 100, HFReference 5000): 0 (default) 0, 0, 1.0, 0.5, **-10000**, 0.02, **-10000**, 0.04 (off); 1: 0, -3000, 1.0, 0.5, 0, 0.3, 0, 0.03; 2: -1000, 0, 0.2, 2.0, -10000, 0.02, 0, 0.01; 3: 0, -3000, 1.0, 0.5, 0, 0.2, -1000, 0.03; 4: 0, -4000, 1.0, 0.5, -10000, 0.02, 0, 0.03; 5: as 0 (factor 0.05, distance 40000). What DSOUND writes (the image copy, `tools/diag/imgcmp.py`): reflection taps at the reflections delay (960/14,400/960/9,600/960 samples), the short-reverb input delay at reflections + reverb delay, reflection gains only in 1 and 3 (~0.0045), short and long reverb output gains 0/0.0078/0.0025/0.0025/0.0078 (1/128 is 0 dB; -1000 mB is exactly -10 dB on it), damping/feedback pairs by decay time. Preset 0's output gains are 0: silent, not quiet.
- **1. Per scene** (`tools/diag/fxlog.py` live, the rest from the table): menu, title, intro 0; **hub 2**; Arctic Antics 0 (3 for point 0x68-0x92; its demo stops at 83); **Sea Shell 4**; **H2 Oh No 4** at the start (point < 0x3D), then 2; **Drain Damage 5** (off); castle levels 3; earth_r 0/1/2 by point and 1 in section 7 (live, the point held by `ptpoke.py`); coral 4; caves in volcano, jungle_r, western, west_b 3; fire_r 1 then 2; droid, weather, fireball, space_r 2; farm, fire_fly, the bosses, space_a 5; west_a, avalanche, snow_b, jungle_a, garden, tsunami, castle_a, cold_b, s_balls, airship, cold_a 0. **The four attract demos: 0, 0, 0, 5.**
- **1. What feeds it** (`tools/diag/step71_fxtap.patch`, step 46's tap ported): every 3D buffer voice, at the same volume as its nearer HRTF bin (387 of 387 voice intervals: the send follows distance), so bin 10 sits 6 dB under the 3D bins (headroom 1 against 0); in the hub that includes the 3D ambience HUB_BG. Music, speech, 2D ambience never; bins 8/9 empty in stereo; bins 11-31 never. HUB_BG plays at 22,008 Hz (0.19% slow).
- **2. xemu.** A config copy in scratch (keyboard on port 1, HDD/EEPROM copies, `use_dsp = true`; the user's `xemu.toml` md5 unchanged). Its window grabs came back black this time (Vulkan and OpenGL; WGC and PrintWindow), so scratch `xdrive.py` drives it by posted keys, each stage confirmed by matching loopback to the game's files; `-s` (QEMU's gdb stub) plus `tools/diag/gdbmem.py` read and write guest memory: writing the picker's hub case (`0xAA360`, the immediate of `mov ecx, 2`) switched the hub's preset, read back at `[0x427A70]`. Results: the attract demos can't show reverb (all off), so step 46's -11 dB and step 56's -2.6 dB were fitted on reverb-free sound. In the hub with the ambience alone (the ~33 s before its music) an A/B of presets 0, 4, 1, 3, 2 (scratch `abtest.py`: per window, short-FIR least squares of HUB_BG and the music below 4 kHz; the residual holds any reverb) gives the same floor for every preset (-12.5 to -15 dB re HUB_BG's dry part with the ambience alone, -18.5 to -22.8 with the music; ours -27), and a 60 ms model doesn't shrink it, so it is timing wobble and the compressor, not a room: **xemu's reverb is at least ~20 dB under the dry 3D sound in every preset**; decay not measurable. Spin whooshes fall to -20 dB within 50 ms in xemu as in ours (`spinenv.py`). The state words ask for clearly audible rooms (presets 1, 3, 4), so either xemu's GP reverb doesn't work (0.8.136; its delay lines go through the GP's scatter-gather DMA) or the program's 1/128 makes it that quiet on hardware: only the real program run faithfully, or a hardware capture, settles it.
- **3. Licences.** The APU in xboxrecomp (`apu_core.c`, `apu_dsp.c`, `apu_vp.c`, `apu_regs.h`, `apu_state.h`, `apu_debug.h`, `fpconv.h`, and `apu_shim.h` by choice) is LGPL-2.1+ from xemu (NOTICE, `LICENSES/LGPL-2.1.txt`); the toolkit is MIT. xemu's DSP core (`hw/xbox/mcpx/apu/dsp/dsp.c`, `interp/dsp_cpu.c`, `interp/dsp_emu.c.inc`, `dsp_jit.c`) is **GPL-2.0+** (Hatari's DSP56001 emulation): linked into crash_twoc.exe it would make the whole program GPL when distributed, which the lifted game code can't be. Its `dsp_dma.c` and `gp_ep.c` are LGPL-2.1+. xemu now runs its DSPs on **dsp56300** (Matt Borgerson, 2026, **MIT**; Rust, Cranelift JIT, a C API with X/Y/P memory maps of buffers or callback regions; differential-tested against silicon).
- **3. Options.** **A**, the GP on dsp56300 with our own glue (memory map: the plain GP window as the DSP's buffers; peripherals as callback regions; DMA and FIFOs from xemu's LGPL `dsp_dma.c`; the frame loop after `gp_ep.c`; output from GP FIFO 0): ~1,000-1,500 lines in `xboxrecomp/src/apu/` plus a Rust toolchain (none here) or a vendored static library; the handshake hand-writes `sub_001391DE`/`sub_0013937A` stand down when it runs; the 3D-bin gains retire; HEADPHONES starts working. Large, medium-high risk (toolchain, DSOUND-GP protocol, timing, and xemu's near-silent result), exact by construction. **B**, a native I3DL2 reverb (reflection taps plus an 8-line FDN, from the DSI3DL2LISTENER or the preset, fed bin 10 in the mixdown): ~400-600 own lines, low risk (off = today, bit for bit), plausible rooms with no reference for the level; XTalk and HEADPHONES unmodelled. **C1**, fx0/fx1 in native code reading DSOUND's computed coefficients, the algorithm read from the 1,211-word program (needs a disassembler: dsp56300's): medium-large, near-exact. **C2**, A offline only.
- **4. Recommendation: A on dsp56300, staged; B the fallback.** Stage 0 (no game change): build dsp56300, a harness runs DSOUND's loader and the game's image as DSOUND left it over our bin dumps (`runs/*.bins`: bins 0, 1, 6-11 a frame), for each preset's state: the real wet level and decay, XTalk's gain against the -2.6/-11 dB fits, files to listen to; audible and right, go on; near-silent on the real program too, keep the dry mix and offer B only as a labelled enhancement. Stage 1: the GP in the APU behind `RECOMP_APU_GP=1`, off by default (off identical), checked against xemu's music-to-rest in the attract demo (preset 0) and stage 0's renders. Stage 2: Esc, Audio, "Room reverb: Off / On" (`reverb`, `RECOMP_REVERB`) and "Reverb amount" 0-200% (`reverb_amount`; it scales bin 10 before the effect, the same for A, B or C, leaving XTalk alone), live, saved; on by default once verified. Stage 3: with A the 3D-bin gains (`96d780b`) and the GP-style mixdown (`0a6f40b`) apply only with the GP off (they were fitted without reverb, never wrong, only a stand-in); with B/C1 they stay and the reverb adds on top in reverb areas only. Effects get louder there (3D voices only), the Effects slider already scales the send (the VP's per-kind gain comes before the bins), the music is never sent, preset-0 scenes don't change, and our HRTF's left-right placement is unaffected.

### 18.75 Step 72: reverb stage 0, the real program offline. Run on the MIT dsp56300 emulator the way the hardware boots it, the game's image gives rooms of the right shape (presets 1, 3, 4 decay in 1.0 s; the hub's preset 2 is a 30 ms echo) at levels nobody hears: 24-41 dB under the mix A-weighted, never within 11 dB of it in any 50 ms window, which agrees with xemu. XTalk is the identity (0 dB). Stage 1 isn't worth it: the dry mix stays, and B only as a labelled enhancement. On the way: our DSOUND computes every preset with the reverb's late lines off, because it reads the state back from the empty GP window.

Report: `step72_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\ade84b01-...\scratchpad`); harness work in `s0/` (fixed preset images `imgw/`, runs `out/`, listening WAVs `wav/`, the loader and fx0 disassembled `loader.dis`/`pcode.dis`), game runs `runs/{sea_w,cas_w,ear_w}.*`. Only diagnostics committed; nothing in `twoc/src` or `xboxrecomp/` changed.

- **Task:** stage 0 only (the user approved a Rust toolchain for their account and dsp56300 built in scratch): a harness running DSOUND's loader and the image as DSOUND left them over the step-71 bin dumps; per preset 1-4 the real wet level (what 1/128 means), the decay, XTalk's real gain against the -2.6/-11 dB fits; dry/wet WAVs for the hub, Sea Shell, the castle, H2 Oh No; audible or not, stage 1 or only B, stage 1's size. No newer xemu.
- **Toolchain.** This session's permission check refused the user-account `rustup` install (it counted it as persistence), so Rust 1.99.0 (minimal, MSVC) lives in `%TEMP%\s0rust`, with nothing on PATH or in the profile. Under the scratch folder the linker hit Windows' 260-character path limit. dsp56300 `2f99039` (2026-10-12) is in scratch (`s0/ext`), not vendored: `cargo build --release -p dsp56300-emu-ffi --no-default-features` gives a 35 MB static `.lib` (links with `legacy_stdio_definitions kernel32 ntdll userenv ws2_32 dbghelp advapi32 bcrypt`), plus its disassembler. xemu `36334d9`'s `hw/xbox/mcpx` was read for the hardware facts only.
- **The harness** (`tools/diag/gpfx.c`, `gpfx_build.bat`):
  - Memory: X 0-0xFFF with the mixbins at X:$1400 aliasing X:$C00-$FFF; Y 0-0x7FF; P 0-0xFFF.
  - Peripherals as callbacks: $FFFFC4 bit 0 means done; $FFFFC5 bit 1 start of frame, bit 7 DMA end of list; $FFFFD4-D7 the DMA engine (7-word blocks; scratch linear F and circular E with offset write-back; 16/24-bit). Scratch is a flat buffer.
  - Boot: scratch words 0-0x7FF to P (the boot ROM), then frames. DSOUND's 355-word loader (P:$29 is its frame loop) reads the 6-word command block at scratch 0x800, which the game file already holds (x0 0, x1 0xC44 code words to P:$171, x2 0x3928, x3 0x9FA state words to X:$80, x4 = 3). It acks by writing x4 = 0 back, then each frame runs fx0-fx9 and DMAs bins 0-5 to rings at scratch 0x8000.
  - A preset change goes in as DSOUND posts it: 132 words from 0x3938, command 2 (`--post`).
  - Checks: 35,400 cycles a frame (max 48,653) against the ~106,000 the loader allows; the reverb runs at 24 kHz (16 words a frame per line, 14 lines in scratch 0xC000-0x5CC60); 7.8x real time.
- **Our DSOUND writes the late reverb off.** The step-71 images gave only a 50-120 ms burst. fx0 multiplies the input of its four late lines by state words 119-129, and those were 0 in every preset (the file's defaults aren't). The chain:
  - `IDirectSound_SetI3DL2Listener` → `sub_00139A36` reads the reverb's first 0x118 bytes of state back through `sub_0013958D` from the descriptor's state pointer `0xFE830200`, the GP's X memory. Then `sub_0013E6D9` recomputes and writes words 70-135 to the image (`sub_001394E4`) and posts command 2 (`sub_0013937A`).
  - The late lines' sizes (read-back words 59-65) go through `sub_0013E1ED` ((x-1)>>2)+1 to samples, then `sub_0013E3F1` (mB = -6000·delay/(24000·DecayTime)) and `sub_0013E224` (gains 0 at or below -10000 mB).
  - Our window is plain RAM nobody fills: 0 becomes 0x40000000 samples, and all four lines are off. The lifted maths is right: the non-zero pairs match the formula (mB -706 → 0.5521/0.1987 against the stored 0.5522/0.1989).
  - Offline fix, with the game's own code: `tools/diag/gpwin.py` holds the harness's post-boot X/Y/P in a running exe copy's GP window. DSOUND then writes presets 1-4 as the hardware gets them: only the flags word (5) and words 119-129 change, e.g. preset 4's first line 0.3501/0.4354, which is 1,392 samples at 1.0 s.
  - `[0x152BAC]` (a second conversion at `0x139A81`) has no writer and stays 0. Today's sound is unaffected (nothing runs the image). In stage 1 the window would be the DSP's X memory.
- **Per preset**, white-noise power gain from bin 10 to FL/FR/BL/BR, first arrival, T20/T30, front octave bands at 125/500/1k/4k (`tools/diag/gpfxan.py`):
  - **1:** -20.0/-20.1/-20.2/-20.3 dB, 50 ms, 1.0 s, -1/-10/-17/-29 dB; 67-80% of the energy in the first 80 ms.
  - **2:** -13.0 dB each (impulse -10: its broadband taps lose the top octave in the 24 kHz decimation), 30 ms, two opposite-sign echoes at 30 and 32.5 ms, tail -27 dB under them, nearly flat.
  - **3:** -25.3/-25.8/-26.1/-25.5 dB, 61 ms, 1.0 s, all late, -8/-16/-23/-34 dB.
  - **4:** -27.6/-26.9/-25.0/-25.7 dB, 50 ms, 1.0 s, -8/-21/-27/-40 dB.
  - **0/5:** silent.
  - **What 1/128 means:** with lReverb 0 mB the late reverb is about -27 dB of power per speaker against the send (the program keeps ~39 dB of headroom at the delay-line input). Reflections at 0 mB add about -21 dB. Against a 3D voice's own dry level (the send is 6 dB under it) the tail sits about 26-33 dB down.
- **XTalk (fx1):** bins 6/7/8/9 → FL/FR/BL/BR at exactly 0.0 dB, crossfeed -68 dB at +11 samples. The -11 dB (18.49) and -2.6 dB (18.59) fits are not the GP's gain. They hold the difference between our VP and mixdown and xemu's VP/HRTF/EP (xemu sends the left ear to all four submixes and plays through the EP). They stay. HEADPHONES only zeroes this identity.
- **The scenes** (step-71 dumps through the fixed presets; reverb against the dry 3D sound, then A-weighted against the whole mix in 50 ms windows: overall, median, loudest window):
  - hub (2): -17.7 dB; -24.4, -24.3, -15.8.
  - Sea Shell (4): -32.4 dB; -37.4, -40.0, -18.0.
  - castle (3): -22.8 dB; -31.5, -40.0, -11.4.
  - H2 Oh No (4): -25.3 dB; -29.1, -29.3, -18.7.
  - earth_r cave (1, 2.7 s): -14.6 dB; -41.2, -40.9, -34.2.
  - In stereo the rear pair carries only reverb. Folding it into L/R at -3 dB (the EP is unmodelled) adds at most 1.8 dB to the reverb and nothing to the mix.
  - Listening files `s0/wav/{hub,seashell,castle,h2ohno}_{dry,wet,reverb_x4,ours}.wav`: dry and wet differ only by the reverb; `ours` is today's mixdown.
- **Verdict:** not audible in normal play (a faint low room in the hub and after loud 3D sounds in the castle, on A/B). That matches xemu's "at least ~20 dB under" (18.74), so xemu was most likely right. Stage 1 would buy that, a unity XTalk (one constant) and a HEADPHONES switch with nothing to switch, for ~600-800 lines in `xboxrecomp/src/apu/` (+150 with the EP's DOLBY program, which DSOUND also starts in stereo: `sub_0013EBEA`, `sub_0013FFC0`), a Rust build or a vendored 35 MB library, ~13% of a core, and real waits in `sub_001391DE`/`sub_0013937A`: not worth it. Keep the dry mix. If an audible room is wanted, B (or a convolution with the measured responses), labelled an enhancement, with "Reverb amount" where 100% = the level measured here.
- **Checks.**
  - The harness against itself: `--post` booted presets give RT60 = DecayTime and first arrivals at the presets' delays; a rebuilt exe gives bit-identical output.
  - The three game runs used exe copies (`tap/`); the user's `crash_twoc.ini` and `xemu.toml` were untouched; no xemu run.
- **Files changed:** `twoc/tools/diag/{gpfx.c,gpfx_build.bat,gpfxan.py,gpwin.py,README.md}` (`546e82f`), this entry and "Start here tomorrow" (notes commit).

- **Saves.**
  - `game_files\UDATA\56550003\4D5C926D6357\crash.sav` (12,548 bytes, every slot) + `SaveMeta.xbx` (38, "Saved games"), beside XAPI's title files.
  - `\??\U:` is linked to partition 1, which the runtime puts in the game dir: relative to the working directory, not `%LOCALAPPDATA%\xboxrecomp`. No autosave seen.
  - The round trip works: save at the hub console → kill → LOAD GAME → the 106% slot loads with all powers.
  - Missing `UDATA`: XAPI makes it again, saving works.
  - Read-only `crash.sav`: the game's own "UNABLE TO SAVE GAME".
  - Save times show UTC: no guest time zone. Not fixed.
- **The fix (`495abf5`).** With `UDATA` not writable, XAPI booted to the dashboard (`XLD_ERROR_INVALID_HARD_DISK`) and the process quit silently in about 1 s. A HalReturnToFirmware tap (thunk `0x15AAD8`, launch page via `0x15AACC`) now writes a `[SAVE]` line and shows a message box naming the folder, on that launch data only; then the exit as before. Default runs identical.
- **Endurance: 53 minutes, one process.**
  - 17 levels at pass-1 settings: 120 s of play each, pause → WARP ROOM, 30 s in the hub.
  - It stopped when random input chose Quit at a game over (title, still 60 fps).
  - Per minute (`procmon.py`), all flat:
    - handles 638, threads 46, GDI/USER 6/18;
    - private bytes 331-377 MB following the level;
    - `[HEAPSTAT]` 84.1%, real live back to 21.9 MB in every hub, VM largest free ≥ 9.7 MB;
    - contiguous high-water 33.5 of 64 MB from minute 11;
    - pool 200 KiB;
    - kernel calls ~2.5 M a minute (134 M at 53 min);
    - 60.0 fps, swap gaps over 25 ms only in minutes with a load (longest 324 ms).
- **Story cutscenes (level 40) not reached unattended.**
  - `sub_0002CC30` chooses them at each level change from the seen-mask `0x5629A8` (bits per id), the room's progress (`0x5625B1` + 4 per room) and the target level. It then sends the game to level 40 with the id in `0x17BED4` and the return level in `0x17BED8`.
  - WOMBAT has every bit, and a cleared bit came back set across the pad's transition (`FFFFFFFF` after), so no cutscene played.
  - A poked id is reset by the same routine, and level 40 with id -1 hangs: an artefact of the poke, not reachable in play.
- **Files changed:** `twoc/src/recomp_manual.c` (`495abf5`), `twoc/tools/diag/{README.md,sweep/*}` (`6cd2a67`, `78d109f`), this entry and "Start here tomorrow" (notes commit).

### 18.77 Step 74: fewer sub-frames when game frames run late, a cheaper combiner lookup, and save times in local time. With Frame rate: Display, a frame now gets 4, 2 or 1 sub-frames by how the frames before ran: cast_bug drops to 1 and avalanch to 2 within a second of loading and hold 60 fps (they ran 37-42 and 50-56), and light levels stay at 4 (21 of 25 throughout; 3 more drop to 2 for a heavy second or two). xbox_d3d8's pixel-shader lookup hashed 1.4 KB a draw byte by byte: 12% of the game thread, now gone (local, perf only). Save times show the PC's local time, daylight saving included. Off: identical.

Report: `step74_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\ff4a96b1-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: repo `d1d205c` (adaptive sub-frames), `e595f95` (time zone); `xboxrecomp` `8137e77` (local, never pushed; the 18.63 rule refinement); plus the notes commit.

- **Task:** 1. interpolation adaptive on heavy levels (4 -> 2 -> 1 when game frames run late, back up after headroom, no flicker or judder at the changes), each change logged, the rate in the overlay's Frame rate line, an "always 4" test switch; check cast_bug, avalanch and the upstairs room at native 4K 16:9 and that light levels stay at 4; per-draw cost if cheap. 2. Save times in UTC: answer `XC_TIMEZONE_*` from Windows' time zone and daylight saving; a save made and loaded, an existing save still loading. Setting off identical except the displayed save time; a commit per fix. The rest of the step-73 sweep's pass 2 is dropped.
- **Where the time went** (cast_bug at 4 sub-frames, HEAD): 39.7 fps; 1,577 commands a frame, recording 1.8 ms (1.16 us a draw, 17.7 MB a frame), four replays 12.8 ms, pairing 0.8 ms, the game and the mirror ~9.6 ms. With interpolation off at the same spot 1.5 ms of each frame is spare: cast_bug is near the limit even off.
- **How it adapts (`d1d205c`, `d3d_host.c` "Step 74: adaptive sub-frames"):**
  - Each present measures the frame before: the time since the game's last present (dt), the part outside `pace()` (busy: the game, recording, replays, presents), and the replay passes' cost a command, plain and interpolated, kept apart (an interpolated pass costs ~2x a plain one).
  - Down to the next divisor of the display's count (4 -> 2 -> 1; 3 -> 1): 30 measured frames, mostly interpolated, mean dt over 1.03 of a frame (16.67 ms here), mean busy over 0.85 of it (the work, not a wait elsewhere: the title and menu ran "late" with 3-4 ms busy), and the passes dropped saving at least half the overrun (not a load).
  - Up: 120 frames on time at a count held 2 s or more, whose busy time plus the next count's extra passes (x1.15, and the pairing from 1) fits in 0.85 of a frame. A drop within 5 s of a rise doubles the hold (to 32 s); a rise kept 20 s sets it back. The first 15 frames after a change aren't judged.
  - Presents: the k sub-frames at interval 4/k; at 1 it is the cut path (one present at 4 with the mid-refresh hold). Every game frame keeps its 4 refreshes, so a change shows nothing on screen.
  - Logged: `[D3DB] host interpolation: 2 frames each game frame from guest swap N (was 4): game frames ran late, 48.6 fps over the last 30 (... ms busy; 1303 commands, an interpolated pass 1.97 ms)` and `... (was 2): room for them, ...`; every interval `[D3DB] host interpolation sub-frames: game frames allowed 4: a, 3: b, 2: c, 1: d; changes n; measured frames m, x ms apart, y ms of it busy`.
  - Overlay: "Now: 2 of 4 frames each game frame, 119.99 fps (fewer while game frames run late)." Switches: `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_ADAPT=0` always the display's count ("always 4"), `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_FIXED=K` always K (both still measure).
  - The "frames shown" line now also counts presents shown at another gap than their own interval (the display log compared each with the present being made, so changes looked like misses).
- **Found on the way:**
  - The swap chain's queue counts presents, not time. At 1 sub-frame (one present at 4) a CPU that keeps up fills it to ~5 presents, 5 game frames (~83 ms) against ~1.5 at 4 sub-frames; at cast_bug's 1 it sits at 1.2-2.5. After a rise the game waits while that drains (~40 ms), which looked like late frames and dropped the count again (avalanch toggled 1 <-> 2): hence the 15 unjudged frames.
  - `SetMaximumFrameLatency` lowered at run time (2 at 1 sub-frame) didn't take: still 5 queued.
  - Presenting each sub-frame 4/k times at interval 1 instead (the same queue time at every count) costs: back-to-back presents wait for the GPU, 0.72 ms each; at 1 in cast_bug 54.6 fps against 60 with one present (`FIXED=1`, standing). The device's render-ahead raised to 16 makes them cheap (0.08 ms) but leaves a queue of one game frame: still 58-59 fps. Not used.
  - **xbox_d3d8 (`8137e77`, local, perf only):** `d3d8_combiners_get_shader` hashed the whole ~1.4 KB combiner state byte by byte (FNV-1a) and memcmp'd it at every draw: 12.3% of the game thread in cast_bug (`tools/diag/hostsamp.py`, an external leaf sampler with a temporary `/MAP`). It now returns the last slot found when the state is the same (memcmp) and hashes 32-bit words; entries are matched on the whole state as before. cast_bug at 4 sub-frames: four replays 13.5 -> 10.0 ms a frame, 37.6 -> 43.9 fps.
- **Checks (16:9, 240 Hz, 1280x720 unless said; play.py 60 s):**
  - cast_bug: 4 -> 2 -> 1 within ~1 s of the load, 1 through the level, 4 again in the hub after a game over. Final build: 16.51-16.81 ms a frame at 1, 13.8-14.8 ms busy (15-16 before `8137e77`); play 58.6/s with the load, the game over and the hub.
  - avalanch: 4 -> 2 at the load; then 2 or 1 with its scenery (four runs: 2 throughout in two, between 2 and 1 in two, changes 13-28 s apart), 16.57-16.76 ms a frame at either; play 59.2-59.9/s. Before: 50-56.
  - Native 4K 16:9 (3840x2160, windowed): the upstairs room 4 throughout, 16.67 ms a frame, 13.6-13.9 ms busy, no present at another gap; avalanch 2 throughout, 59.9/s; cast_bug 1, 16.6 ms a frame (final build 16.61-16.62, 14.1-14.5 ms busy).
  - The 25 main levels (pass-1 settings, 30 s play each, `adsum.py "m*"`): 21 stay at 4 throughout at 59.4-60.0/s; snow_m (135 frames), fire_fly (765 frames over three drops) and western (1,455) drop to 2 at spots of 965-1,105 commands with busy over a frame, and rise back; avalanch as above. No crash.
  - The display at changes (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_DISPLAYLOG=1`, sampled): nothing out of the ordinary at the changes; at 2 sub-frames 3-10 presents in 10 s shown a refresh late and the next early (3/1 pairs), at 1 in cast_bug 3/5 pairs (it runs at the edge there).
  - Off, identical (`id.sh`: drive.py 150 s, bridge off and host-only, exe copies, no ini; HEAD against `d1d205c` against `e595f95`, and against the final with `8137e77`): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]` lines, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, the host device lines, the first 8 host dumps byte-identical; the final exe (with `8137e77`) against `e595f95` the same.
- **Save times (`e595f95`).** XAPI's GetTimeZoneInformation (`sub_000ECD9E`, via XQueryValue `sub_000EDC0F`, public wrapper `0xED13A`) reads `XC_MAX_OS` (0xFF, the EEPROM's 0x60-byte user section): bias +4, names +8/+0xC, standard/daylight dates +0x18/+0x1C (month, week of the month, day of week, hour), biases +0x28/+0x2C, "DST off" bit 1 of the flags at +0x58. The runtime zero-filled it (UTC). Its own `XC_TIMEZONE_BIAS` answer (-300, labelled Eastern but UTC+5 by the sign) was never read. Now `XC_MAX_OS` and indices 0-6 answer Windows' zone (`GetTimeZoneInformation`; DST off from `GetDynamicTimeZoneInformation`); names are initials (AEST/AEDT). `[TIME] the game's time zone: bias -600 min, standard AEST (+0, month 4 week 1 day 0 3:00), daylight AEDT (-60, month 10 week 1 day 0 2:00)`.
  - Each slot's time is stored in `crash.sav` when saved. The step-73 save (made by the old exe at 11:47 local) still lists as 00:47 and loads; the console's current panel showed 17:09 at 06:09 UTC; a new save listed 17:11 after a restart, beside the old one, and loads.
  - Not checked: Windows' "adjust for daylight saving" off (the user's setting wasn't changed), another zone.
- **Known limits:** cast_bug at 1 sub-frame is as close to its budget as interpolation off (busy ~15 of 16.67 ms); avalanch's heaviest stretch goes to 1. At fewer sub-frames the queue holds more time (at 1, up to ~5 game frames when the CPU is ahead). A rise into a view too heavy for it shows ~0.5-0.75 s below 60 before the drop (then the hold doubles). The GPU's own time isn't measured: a GPU-bound case wouldn't drop (busy stays low); not seen here (RTX 4080 SUPER, native 4K).
- **A user ini:** `twoc\build\Release\crash_twoc.ini` (Oct 14 22:35) left alone; every run used exe copies in scratch, saves in scratch `sw/root`.
- **Files changed:** `twoc/src/{d3d_host.c,d3d_host.h,d3d_bridge.c,overlay.cpp,overlay.h}` (`d1d205c`), `twoc/src/recomp_manual.c` (`e595f95`), `xboxrecomp/src/d3d/d3d8_combiners.c` (`8137e77`, local); `twoc/tools/diag/{hostsamp.py,README.md,sweep/upst.py,sweep/adsum.py,sweep/README.md}`, this entry and "Start here tomorrow" (notes commit).

### 18.78 Step 75: an "Adaptive frame rate" setting, and where a 240 fps frame's time goes (scoping, nothing else changed). Esc, Display, "Adaptive frame rate: On / Off" (`adaptive_framerate`), On by default: Off draws every sub-frame whatever the cost, and in cast_bug the game itself then runs at 75% speed. At 4 sub-frames cast_bug takes 22-24 ms a frame against 16.68, all on the game thread: about half is the game and the mirror, half the four replays; the GPU idles at 40%. A render thread would split it into ~12 ms each: recommended, after a small stage of per-draw cuts. Off: identical.

Report: `step75_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\2353dab8-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commit: repo `e876c5c` (the setting); plus the notes commit.

- **Task:** 1. the Display setting "Adaptive frame rate: On / Off" (ini, live; Off = always 4, the `INTERP_ADAPT=0` path), with the default and why. 2. Scoping only: where the time goes in cast_bug's start, avalanch and the upstairs room at 4 sub-frames (game, recording, pairing, each replay, present), the budget and what has to fit. 3. Options with size, risk and fps: A a render thread (every shared state and D3D11 call site, the hand-over, the risks), B cheaper replays, C parallel work without a render thread, D anything else. 4. One path in stages, and an honest answer on a stable 240 in cast_bug's start. Setting off identical; commit item 1. The user doesn't like adaptive (240 -> 120 -> 60 is jarring); time is not a constraint.
- **The setting (`e876c5c`):** `adaptive_framerate = 0 | 1` (`RECOMP_FRAMERATE_ADAPTIVE`; step 74's `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_ADAPT` read as its old name in `twoc_settings_load`), shown under Frame rate while it is Display, applied at the next present (`d3dh_set_interp_adaptive`; `ia_frame` sees the change: back to the display's count, judged afresh, `[D3DB] host interpolation: adaptive frame rate off (the setting)`). The sub-frame trigger file also takes `adaptive 0|1`. Checked: live in cast_bug (On 4 -> 2 -> 1; Off 4 at once, 22.1 ms frames; On again 2 -> 1), the ini read, the overlay line; off identical (drive.py 150 s bridge off and host-only, exe copies: 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]`, exit 3, 8 of 8 host dumps byte-identical).
- **Default On, why:** the game runs one update a frame, so with Off a frame too heavy for 4 sub-frames slows the game itself (cast_bug 45 fps = 75% speed, 41 fps while running about), worse than fewer frames in between. Once the render thread lands it should never drop; the setting stays as a safety net, and Off can become the default if a sweep never sees a drop.
- **Where the time goes** (`twoc/tools/diag/step75_frame_profile.patch`, measurement only: `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_FPROF=FILE`, a CSV line a guest frame; `sweep/m.sh`, `sweep/fpsum.py`; `sweep/hs2.py` samples with what the game thread is doing; native 4K 16:9, HUD at the edges, adaptive off; ms a frame, mean):

  | | cast_bug start | cast_bug playing | avalanch start | avalanch playing | upstairs |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | commands | 1,655 | 1,602 | 1,521 | 1,392 | 979 |
  | game (guest code) | 4.60 | 5.05 | 5.06 | 5.04 | 3.55 |
  | mirror (d3d_bridge) | 5.02 | 5.18 | 3.99 | 3.89 | 3.20 |
  | recording | 1.87 | 1.99 | 1.11 | 1.27 | 0.73 |
  | pairing | 0.87 | 0.92 | 0.83 | 0.76 | 0.56 |
  | 4 replays (each) | 9.46 (2.41/2.39/2.38/2.28) | 10.72 (2.81/2.76/2.71/2.45) | 8.45 | 8.13 | 5.59 |
  | 4 presents | 0.14 | 0.19 | 0.34 | 0.20 | 0.21 |
  | frame (p90) | 21.98 (22.57), 45.5 fps | 24.16 (26.30), 41.4 | 19.82 (21.49), 50.5 | 19.77 (21.53), 50.6 | 16.68, busy 13.87 (14.70) |

  - The game's window is all CPU (no waits there); presents are 0.14-0.34 ms. The GPU is 36-42% busy at ~1,240 MHz, 46 W (its timestamps only follow the CPU's submission). Other threads: the NV2A ack thread spins a whole core (98%), NVIDIA's driver thread ~20%.
  - The game thread by phase (cast_bug start): game 26%, mirror 21% (`vs_fetch` 29%, `content_sig` 22%, `vp_vertices` 12%), recording 8% (`ic_key`, `ic_record`, `ic_obj`; memcpy 19%), pairing 3.7% (`ic_replaced`, qsorts), replay 40%: memcmp/memcpy 21% (mostly xbox_d3d8's sampler-cache scan in `d3d8_states_apply_sampler`, `d3d8_states_apply`'s descriptor compares, `d3d8_shaders_prepare_draw`'s fixed-function PS constants every draw), d3d_host 21% (`vs_bind` 10%: 192 registers compared every draw; `draw_exec`), xbox_d3d8 20%, the NVIDIA driver 18.5%, d3d11.dll 12%.
  - Identical work is small: render-target draws 0 (cast_bug) to ~10 a frame; draws "as they were" 1,121 of 1,237 a sub-frame at cast_bug's still start but 286 of 1,203 while playing. Off screen: 2.2-3.1% of cast_bug's 3D draws, 12-17.5% of avalanch's (50-56% of its vertices).
  - Budget 16.68 ms a game frame (4.17 a sub-frame); on one thread everything must fit in it, ~14-15 ms mean for p90 under 16.7: cast_bug playing has to lose ~10 ms (40%), avalanch ~30%.
- **Options (details, every shared state and D3D11 call site, in the report):**
  - **A, a render thread** (replays, presents, pairing, texture uploads, targets, AA, overlay, dumps, resizing on it; the game thread records into one of two slots, three for the interpolation tables, and hands over at its Swap, one deep): game thread 12.2 ms (p90 13.4), render thread 11.9 (p90 13.5) in cast_bug playing; ~3 ms spare: 240 in all three. Record-time `tex_get` becomes snapshots of missed guest textures; `vs_slot`'s shader creation moves; the bridge's `settings_apply` (aspect, draw distance) goes back to the game thread. ~1,000-1,500 lines in `d3d_host.c`, no xboxrecomp or guest change; risk medium-high (races, a second ~20 MB arena, latency +0-1 frame, adaptive measures two threads). Off: no thread.
  - **B, cheaper replays:** B1 xbox_d3d8 redundant state (sampler hash, skip unchanged binds, no fixed-function PS CB under a combiner) 2.5-3.5 ms; B2 `vs_bind` by read mask, constants in place, vertices once a frame 1-1.5 ms; B3 "compiled" passes 2-4 (bind pass 1's objects, re-upload only interpolated constants) 4-5 ms but medium-large and riskier; B4 culling per sub-frame 0.2 ms (cast_bug) to 1 ms (avalanch); B5 identical-work skipping ~0 while playing; B6 game thread: texture dirty tracking instead of `content_sig`, a vertex cache, recording/pairing 3-4 ms. All of B: 24.2 -> ~12.5-15 ms; without B3 ~16.5-18 (not stable).
  - **C:** classification and pairing on a worker 1.5-2.5 ms; D3D11 deferred contexts per sub-frame need xbox_d3d8's global state made per context (large, high risk) and leave the game thread loaded (~15-16 ms): not over A.
  - **D:** reprojection (warp by depth) would make 240 trivial but undoes object interpolation (steps 62-71); the NV2A ack thread's spin is worth an event later; the GPU has room.
- **Recommendation:** A, staged. 0: B1 + B2 (small, low risk; cast_bug ~24 -> ~20 ms). 1: A's groundwork single-threaded (slots, texture snapshots, the `vs_slot` split, the settings queue; identical output). 2: the render thread (Display only). 3: the 25-level sweep with adaptive On (it should never drop) and Off, then a playtest; decide the default. 4: B3/B4/B6 only if short. A stable 240 in cast_bug's start: very probably, with ~3 ms spare at p90 in its worse playing stretch; not for every frame (single frames around loads run 46-81 ms today), which adaptive keeps catching.
- **A user ini:** `twoc\build\Release\crash_twoc.ini` (Oct 14 22:35, fullscreen native 16:9, Frame rate: Display) left alone; every run used exe copies in scratch. The build folder's stale step-74 `crash_twoc.map` was removed with the measurement build's; the exe there is `e876c5c`'s.
- **Files changed:** `twoc/src/{d3d_host.c,d3d_host.h,overlay.cpp,settings.c,settings.h}` (`e876c5c`); `twoc/tools/diag/{step75_frame_profile.patch,README.md}`, `twoc/tools/diag/sweep/{m.sh,hs2.py,fpsum.py,wr.py,README.md}`, this entry and "Start here tomorrow" (notes commit).


### 18.79 Step 76: stage 0, cheaper replays (B1 + B2). Drawing each frame four times costs 37-45% less, with the same pixels: at 4 sub-frames cast_bug takes 21.0 ms a frame at its start (23.7 before) and 18.4 with the camera moving (24.1), avalanch 16.4 (18.6; 60 fps, p90 17.5 still over budget), upstairs 11.8 busy (13.5). Almost all of it is B2's (passes 2-4 at half of pass 1); B1 saves only 0.3-1.3 ms. A stable 240 in cast_bug still needs the render thread, which now has ~7-8 ms of work against the game thread's ~12.5. Off: identical.

Report: `step76_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\dfaad4ab-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: `xboxrecomp` `ef3a455` (B1) and `9dea071` (B2's xbox_d3d8 side), local, never pushed (the 18.63 rule refinement); twoc `98dfeb1` (B2), `ea415b3` (the check patches); plus the notes commit.

- **Task:** the plan's stage 0 only (18.78): B1 in xbox_d3d8 (sampler cache by hash, skip unchanged OM/RS/PSSetSamplers/IASetInputLayout/VS/PS sets, no fixed-function PS constants under a combiner shader, the combiner constants only when changed, no VB/IB rebinding after UP draws), B2 in twoc (`vs_bind` by the read masks, `ic_draw`'s registers in place, vertices and indices uploaded once a frame), each committed; measured before and after with the step 75 profile, standing still or with Crash's position written (no playing); REPLAY_CHECK, identity, sub-frames unchanged. Not the render thread.
- **B1 (`ef3a455`):** `g_d3d8_bound` records what the draws last bound (shaders, layout, VS/PS constant buffers, blend/depth/rasterizer, 4 samplers, VB/IB, topology) and skips binding the same object again; everything outside xbox_d3d8 that binds on the context puts back what it found (aa.c, the screenshot blit, ImGui's backend, the gamma command list with RestoreContextState). Samplers by an FNV hash of the description (256 slots), and a stage whose description is its last draw's keeps its object. `d3d8_shaders_prepare_draw` takes the combiners' pixel shader when one is set, else builds the fixed-function one (it built that at every draw only to be replaced). Pixel constants built on the stack, uploaded when they differ. UP draws: rings bound at 0, drawn with a base vertex/start index (vertices at a multiple of the stride); DrawPrimitive/DrawIndexedPrimitive bind the stream source themselves. The A8 smoke test sets each path's token and binds through the record.
- **B2 (`98dfeb1` + `9dea071`):** `vs_bind` compares the microcode's direct reads plus c58/c59, 64-bit inline compares; programs reading relative to a0.x compare all 192 (a0 may be negative). `ic_draw` writes c96-c99, the camera-position registers and the bones into the recording itself, each kept and put back after the draw (the 3 KB copy only where a camera-position register is among them). `d3d8_DrawPrimitiveUPKept`/`d3d8_DrawIndexedPrimitiveUPKept` with a caller-held `D3D8UpKept` (offsets and the rings' generation, bumped at every discard): a recorded draw uploads at its first pass and later passes draw from there; rings 64 MB / 8 MB (were 16 / 2; cast_bug records ~18.6 MB a frame).
- **Verification:** `twoc/tools/diag/step76_old_way_check.patch` + `step76_old_way_check_xboxrecomp.patch` (`d3d8_tmp_old`: every bind and upload made, the fixed-function pixel shader first, 192 compares, the 3 KB copy, uploads each pass). REPLAY_CHECK draws each frame at once the old way and replays it the new way; `TMP_ICHECK=1` draws every interpolated sub-frame both ways from the same targets. B1: 0 of 5,603 / 5,537 / 3,851 frames differ (cast_bug gliding, avalanch, upstairs). B2's first version: 4,533 of 7,960 differ (3-26 pixels, up to 5 levels): **xbox_d3d8's translation always reads c58/c59** (the viewport undo in `d3d8_vsh.c`'s epilogue), which the microcode masks lack; fixed before the commit. Final: 0 of 5,515 / 5,519 / 3,873 frames and 0 of 18,796 / 19,208 / 14,428 sub-frames (~615 program draws moved a sub-frame in cast_bug). Identity (drive.py 150 s, bridge off and host-only, step 75's exe against the final one): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]`, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, 8 of 8 host dumps byte-identical. xbox_d3d8's four smoke tests pass.
- **Measured** (step 75's profile patch, native 4K 16:9, HUD at the edges, adaptive off; three exes interleaved, two runs each; `sweep/meas.sh`, `sweep/tab.py`; "gliding" = `sweep/glide.py` moving Crash over z -3..7 at 3 units/s by writing his position, standing in for step 75's play):

  | ms a game frame | cast_bug start | cast_bug gliding | avalanch start | upstairs |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
