### 18.80 Step 77: frame-rate targets. Esc, Display, "Frame rate" is now Game (60), Match display, 90, 120, 144, 165, 180 or 240 fps (`framerate = 60 | display | N`). Each frame shown is drawn at its own moment between two game frames, so a display that isn't a multiple of 59.94 Hz (the Legion Go's 144: 2 or 3 frames each game frame, 2.4 on average) moves evenly; the game still runs 59.94 updates a second. A target is shown evenly when it is the display's refresh or a whole fraction of it, or with variable refresh; otherwise it falls back to the highest even rate below it (90 on 240 Hz: 80), and the overlay says which and why. The adaptive rate drops to whatever lower rate fits (240 -> 120, 80 or 60; 180 -> 90 -> 60). Game (60) and the bridge off: identical.

Report: `step77_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\7f3c0c0f-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: `ed00f29` (vrrprobe), `4f9029e` (item 1), `1cb3eb7` (item 2), `0e93919` (item 3), plus the notes commit (check patches, harness, README rows). No xboxrecomp change.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`): time-based sub-frames; the target list with an evenness line and a sensible fallback, VRR detection on Windows and what is known of the Legion Go's panel; the adaptive rate on the new steps; tests at 144 (the user sets the display), 120 and 90 on 240; Game (60) and bridge off identical; no playing (standing, or Crash's position written); each item committed.
- **The user's monitor offers 60, 120, 180 and 240 Hz at 4K, no 144** (EnumDisplaySettings). The user set 180 for the "144" test. 180 is a whole multiple, so the 2.4-presents clock was checked through the limiter (144 with `RECOMP_VRR=1` on the 180 Hz display: the clock exact, the display uneven as it must be without VRR), and 90 on 180 (1.5 presents a game frame) stood in for an uneven count under vsync. **A 144 Hz display under vsync is still unmeasured**: the Legion Go itself, or any 144 Hz monitor.
- **Item 1, time-based sub-frames (`4f9029e`):** game frame n ends at slot S(n) = n x span (span = slots a second / 59.94; slots are refreshes under vsync, the limiter's ticks with variable refresh). Presents go on a grid of whole slots `step` apart that runs on across game frames; each is drawn at t = (r - S(n-1)) / span; t = 1 is the frame as recorded (a plain pass). The game is held in its Swap until its last present, so it still ticks at 59.94. On a whole multiple the span is whole (within 0.5%, as the pacing always took it) and this is the old k sub-frames exactly. A frame with no plain pass (most at 144) now prices the adaptive rate from an interpolated pass (it measured nothing before). `fp_frame`, `fp_costs`, `present_slots` (DXGI intervals over 4 split by presenting again), `pace(mid, slots)`.
  - Check (`tools/diag/step77_old_counts_check.patch`, the old k's t, intervals and count compared every frame): 0 of 4,800 / 3,600 / 4,200 / 6,600 frames differ (upstairs at 4, 2 and 1 sub-frames, cast_bug adaptive 240 -> 120 -> 60 as before). Its first run "found" 4,199 of 4,200: the check expected the last present's unused t0 to be 1.
- **Item 2, targets (`1cb3eb7`):** `d3dh_frame_plan`: the target capped at the display; m0 the whole fraction R/m within 0.5% of it (even); else variable refresh (below) runs it from the limiter at the target; else the highest R/m at or below the target and above 59.94 (none: the lowest above). `g_fp.note` is the overlay's line ("Even: 120.00 fps, each frame held 2 refreshes of this 239.99 Hz display." / "90 fps isn't even on this 239.99 Hz display (no variable refresh: NVIDIA's driver has variable refresh off for this display): 80.00 fps instead, each frame held 3 refreshes."); the Pacing line under a plan says "display N Hz vsync for the frame rate below; the game 59.94 fps". The plan is made again on `WM_DISPLAYCHANGE`, at the end of a measurement, and when the window mode changes under a plan that counts on variable refresh. `settings.c` `twoc_framerate_parse/_text` (N > 60, capped at 1000; anything else 60).
  - **Presents held 3 refreshes or more now go mid-refresh** (step 45's hold, which sub-frames skip): at 80 fps on 240 Hz, 6-13% of frames were shown 2 or 4 refreshes after the one before, 4-6% after. Old configurations never present a sub-frame 3 or more refreshes apart, and their one-present frames already held.
  - **Variable refresh on Windows** (`vrr_read`, `vrr_claim`, `vrr_sample`): no Windows API says whether a panel is refreshing variably. DXGI's tearing support says the system allows it, not that the display does; the EDID's range-limits descriptor (with the continuous-frequency bit) and AMD's FreeSync data block say the display claims a range; NVIDIA's driver answers outright (`NvAPI_Disp_GetVRRInfo` through `nvapi64.dll`'s QueryInterface, ids from NVIDIA's MIT headers); AMD's ADL has a FreeSync query (not used). So: NVIDIA's "off" or no range claimed -> not counted on; a range or NVIDIA's "on" -> the plan runs from the limiter and DXGI's frame statistics decide over 120 frames whether frames reach the screen when presented (`[D3DB] display: variable refresh ...`), per window mode, for the run. `RECOMP_VRR=0 | 1 | measure` are test aids. `tools/diag/vrrprobe` is the same as a tool.
  - **This desktop:** MSI MAG321UX OLED, EDID range 48-240 Hz with the continuous bit, FreeSync block 48-240; NVIDIA: possible 0, enabled 0 (G-SYNC Compatible is presumably off in NVIDIA's control panel; not checked). vrrprobe: 90 fps lands 8.3 or 12.5 ms apart windowed, borderless and with tearing allowed. In the game, `RECOMP_VRR=measure` at 90: "117 of 120 frames shown on the refresh grid", re-planned to 80.
  - **The Legion Go** (Lenovo PSREF, Legion Go 8APU1): 8.8" 2560x1600, 144 Hz, no variable refresh listed; XDA: no VRR; gamescope PR #1573: its EDID lists fixed 60 and 144 Hz modes only. On it: Match display or 144 = 144 fps, 2.4 frames each game frame, even; 72 even (2 refreshes); 90 and 120 fall back to 72; 165, 180, 240 are capped to 144. AMD, so no driver answer: with no range in its EDID nothing is measured. The Legion Go 2 (TechRadar): a 144 Hz OLED with VRR; if its EDID claims the range, 90 and 120 would be measured there and run from the limiter if the frames follow (untested).
- **Item 3, the adaptive ladder (`0e93919`):** steps are every whole number of slots from the target's to the game's rate, kept only where cheaper than the step above (interpolated passes counted as two plain), then one present a game frame: 240 Hz 240/120/80/60 (80: a present every 3 refreshes, 1 interpolated and 1/3 plain pass a game frame against 120's 1 and 1), 180 Hz 180/90/60, 144 144/72/60. A drop goes to the fastest lower step whose predicted busy time fits 85% of a game frame (none: the slowest), still only if it saves half the overrun; a rise to the fastest that fits.

### 18.81 Step 78: the render thread's groundwork, still on one thread (stage 1 of 18.78's plan A). A recorded frame now lives in a slot that the game side hands to the replay side at the guest's Swap; textures are recorded as copies of the guest bytes and uploaded by the replay side; vertex programs are parsed on the game side and created on the replay side; live settings are queued and applied at each side's frame boundary; step 77's frame plan is one replay-side unit; every D3D11 call site is tagged by side, and `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_THREADCHECK=1` ends the process if one runs on the wrong side. Output identical; cost +0.18 ms a game frame at cast_bug's start, +0.01 gliding. No thread yet.

Report: `step78_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\ee1724bb-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: `f6cec98` (item 1), `c5e1f7a` (2), `df691f1` (3), `6ad1968` (4), `652f6a9` (5), `e908dc1` (6), `a33808c` (the slots share their memory: the cost fix); plus the notes commit (check patches, harness, README rows). No xboxrecomp change.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`): stage 1 of 18.78's plan, groundwork on one thread with identical output: 1 frame slots, 2 textures as game-side table plus snapshots, 3 the `vs_slot` split, 4 the settings queue, 5 step 77's plan as a replay-side unit with what it reads from the game side, 6 thread tags and a debug check, and the final ownership list. Verify with REPLAY_CHECK, the sub-frame check, identity (Game and bridge off), Display at 240/120/90 on 240 Hz, MSAA/FXAA/SMAA, 16:9, the overlay held and running, a live resize, H2 Oh No and the pause still, the loading screens; the cost in cast_bug under 0.3 ms. Each item committed. No playing (standing, or Crash's position written).
- **Item 1, frame slots (`f6cec98`, `a33808c`):** `fslot_t` holds a frame's commands, arena, render-target list, texture uploads, the game side's settings generation, and what the replay side's plan reads (swap, Swap time, recorded or not, for interpolation or not, the game side's time on it). Two slots: the game side records into `g_rs`, `slot_handover` (every Swap, under `g_lock`) passes it to the replay side (`g_ps`) and switches `g_rs` to the other one; `slot_free` at the replay's end. The interpolation tables are three sets (`g_ir` recorded, `g_ic` replayed, `g_ip` the frame before; `ic_end` makes the replayed set the frame before, the handover empties the third). On one thread the slot is replayed inside the same Swap, so the other is always free (a `[D3DB] host frame slots` line would say otherwise; never seen).
  - **The cost fix (`a33808c`):** with two arenas in turn every frame was recorded into memory last touched two frames before: in cast_bug the recording and the first pass each cost ~0.25 ms more (busy +0.73 / +0.62 ms). The slots now give their chunks and command array back to a shared pool at `slot_free`, and the slot recorded next takes them first: on one thread the same memory every frame, as with one arena.
- **Item 2, textures (`c5e1f7a`):** the cache in two halves. Game side: `gtex_t` (description, live, last use) in `tex_cache_t.g`, `tex_lookup` decides at record time in the old order (same probe, same LRU clock, same eviction) whether the texture is held or must be (re)made, and in which entry; a miss copies the guest bytes and a P8 palette into the arena (`tex_snap`, a `texup_t` with the command and stage it comes before). Replay side: `slot_prepare` before the first pass makes the textures from the copies (`tex_make`) in record order, an upload before its stage's pin, and pins each draw's textures (`tslot` -> `g_tc.h`). Upload failures go back through `bad`/`nbad` (interlocked). `d3dh_texture_touch` flips the game side's table. `rc_maybe_target` reads `g_gt_data` (the targets' Data, one word each, kept by `rt_find`/`rt_release`) and, for a render thread, the targets of a frame handed over but not yet replayed. Draws made at once (frames not recorded) look up and upload in one go (`tex_get`). REPLAY_CHECK's draws made at once and TEXCHECK use a cache of their own (`g_tcc`), so REPLAY_CHECK compares the old path with the new. A recorded draw's texture fetched at the replay that isn't a render target there is read from guest memory as before and counted (`[D3DB] host recorded texture uploads: ...; ... read from guest memory at the replay N`): 0 in every run.
  - Ordering against guest rewrites is today's: a miss copies the bytes at the moment the old path uploaded them, and each draw is pinned to the entry as it stood when it was recorded.
  - Copies in steady play: ~0 a frame (the cost table's `snap` 0.000 ms); 270-400 a run (3.5-5.6 MB), at loads.
- **Item 3, vertex programs (`df691f1`):** `vs_slot` parses only (inputs, read masks, a0.x bases; never changes an entry once counted, `MemoryBarrier` then `g_vs_n`); `vs_make` creates the host shader at the program's first draw on the replay side (`g_vsh`); `vs_bind` and the record dump only look up (`vs_find`). 12 shaders in cast_bug, 0 fails.
- **Item 4, settings (`6ad1968`):** a change the frames depend on is queued (`sq_push`; `g_sq` with a generation per side). The host side (`sq_apply_host`: fullscreen, anisotropy, anti-aliasing, adaptive, the bridge's `settings_apply_host`: the host picture's size and the pacing, computed from the settings alone) drains between frames: after each held overlay frame and at the present's end; the game side (`sq_apply_game`: the frame-rate setting, which decides whether frames are recorded, and the bridge's `settings_apply_game`: `d3db_wide` and the draw distance its translation reads, plus `d3dh_record_wide` for the HUD anchoring) after the hold and at the Swap's end. HUD, audio, pads and controls still apply at once (record-time and interlocked, or thread-safe). A slot carries the game side's generation it was recorded under (`set_gen`); the host side's must have reached it. Test aid: the sub-frame trigger file takes `set KEY VALUE` lines (aspect, render, aa, aniso, distance, framerate, adaptive, fullscreen, pacing; `sweep/st.py`), applied as the overlay's at the present's end.
- **Item 5, the frame plan (`652f6a9`):** `d3dh_present` is now: the trigger file; `slot_handover`; `replay_frame(g_ps)` (uploads and pins, `ic_queue`, `ia_frame`, `fp_frame`, `ic_begin`, the passes, the presents, the overlay's hold); the game side's settings; the frame-rate switch (`ic_apply`, game side, then the replay side's queue); `replay_tail` (a new plan on a mode change or a measurement, the overlay's frame applied, the host side's settings); the game side's settings. What each piece reads from the game side:
  - `d3dh_frame_plan` (made by the bridge's host part, at the start, and from `replay_tail`): the display, the window mode, the setting. Nothing of the game's; `game_hz` is the CreateDevice interval's 59.94.
  - `fp_frame` (time-based sub-frames): only `g_fp`/`g_fc` and the step `ia_k` gives.
  - `ia_frame` (the adaptive ladder, busy): whether the frame was recorded for interpolation (the slot's `interp`, `g_interp` as the game side took it), and the game side's time on the frame (the slot's `game_ms`, from its last Swap's return to the handover). Busy is still `dt - pace` on one thread (identical decisions) and is also split into the game side's and the replay side's, logged: upstairs at 240 7.4 + 4.4 ms, cast_bug at the 60 step 12.2 + 2.4; with a render thread the larger of the two decides.
  - `ic_begin` and the passes: the slot's tables and commands; `g_iprev_ok`, which `ic_apply` (game side) clears on a frame-rate switch: with a thread that switch must happen with it drained.
  - `present_slots`/`present_one`/`pace`: the slot's swap number and time (dumps, logs).
- **Item 6, tags and THREADCHECK (`e908dc1`):** `REPLAY_SIDE()` in 64 functions (63 at `e908dc1`, `slot_free` since), `GAME_SIDE()` in 15; each entry point sets its thread's side (`t_side`, thread-local): recording `SIDE_GAME`, the handover `SIDE_GAME`, the replay and presents `SIDE_REPLAY`, draws made at once while frames aren't recorded and the checks/probes that draw at once `SIDE_NOW` (both). `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_THREADCHECK=1`: a replay-side function while the game side records, or a game-side one while the replay side replays, writes `[D3DB] THREADCHECK: <function>, the <x> side's, ran on the <y> side (thread N)` and ends the process with exit code 120. Negative test: the sub-frame check build (it fetches the old path's textures while recording) ends at once: "tex_get, the replay side's, ran on the game side". It never fired in any other run of this step.
- **The final ownership list** (the render thread's, and what stays with the game):
  - Replay side (the render thread; D3D11, DXGI, xbox_d3d8 or their objects): MSAA (`ms_samples`, `ms_surface`, `ms_resolve`, `ms_fill`, `target_drawn`), render targets (`rt_resolve`, `rt_ms_release`, `rt_ms_make`, `rt_release`, `rt_find`, `bind_target`, `freed_exec`), `clear_exec`, `copy_exec`, the host textures (`tex_release`, `tex_upload`, `tex_make`, `tex_get`, `slot_prepare`), draws (`rescale_rhw_map`, `apply_ff`, `vs_make`, `vs_bind`, `draw_exec`), the replay (`replay_pass`, `replay_done`, `replay_run`, `slot_free`, `rc_forget`), interpolation (`ic_pair`, `ic_begin`, `ic_sub`, `ic_draw`, `ic_end`), the plan (`d3dh_frame_plan`, `fp_frame`, `ia_frame`, `pace`, `present_mid_refresh`), the frame (`back_tex`, `frame_tex` with aa.c, `present_one`, `present_slots`, `replay_frame`, `replay_tail`), the visible chain and picture (`ic_queue`, `flip_fit`, `recreate_flip`, `d3dh_set_pacing`, `bb_rebuild`, `set_antialiasing`, `d3dh_resize`, `blit_init`, `shot_take`, `blit_draw`, `copy_unscaled`), the overlay (`ovl_begin`, `ovl_draw` with overlay.cpp, `overlay_pause`), the host side's settings (`sq_apply_host`), dumps and checks (`dump_texture`, `dump_targets`, `replay_check_snap`, `replay_check`, and the probes `d3dh_ps_probe`, `d3dh_vs_probe`, `d3dh_texture_check`, at once).
  - Game side (the game thread): the recording (`rec_draw`, `rc_push`, `ra_alloc`, `rc_target`, `rc_maybe_target`, `ic_record`, `ic_obj`, `hud_anchor`, the overlay pass), the texture table (`tex_lookup`, `tex_snap`, `d3dh_texture_touch`), programs (`vs_slot`, `d3dh_vs_inputs`, `d3dh_vs_consts`), the handover (`slot_handover`), the frame-rate switch (`ic_apply`), its settings (`sq_apply_game`, the bridge's `settings_apply_game`).
  - Unchanged: `d3dh_create` (before any thread), the window thread, the pacing and why-notes' own locks.
  - **Left for stage 2:** `g_st` counters are written by both sides (per side or interlocked); `g_lock` serialises everything still; the overlay's hold must move to the render thread with the game waiting in its handover, and the game side's settings drained when it is let go; the frame-rate switch (`ic_apply`) only with the thread drained; the replay-time texture fallback (a maybe-target that isn't one at the replay reads guest memory: 0 so far; make it a copy at record time or a synchronous fetch); `rc_maybe_target` doesn't subtract a pending frame's freed ranges (it errs to "maybe", which lands in that fallback).
- **Verified** (final code; THREADCHECK on where marked):
  - REPLAY_CHECK (THREADCHECK): 0 of 5,136 / 5,203 / 3,908 / 5,052 frames differ (cast_bug gliding, avalanch, upstairs, H2 Oh No with its pause still). REPLAY_CHECK records from the title on, so it covers the menu, the intro, the loading screens, the hub and the level loads.
  - The sub-frame check (`step78_subframe_check.patch`, every pass, plain and interpolated, drawn with the old path's textures and the new path's from the same targets; Display at 4 sub-frames, adaptive off): 0 of 19,909 / 21,401 / 16,934 / 19,947 passes differ.
  - Identity (`sweep/id.sh`, drive.py 150 s, bridge off and host-only, the exe before step 78 against the final one): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]`, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, the host device and pacing lines the same (but the dump folder's name), 8 of 8 host dumps byte-identical.

### 18.82 Step 79: the render thread (stage 2 of 18.78's plan A). With Frame rate above 60 and Esc, Display, "Render thread: Auto / On / Off" on, the frames between game frames are replayed and presented on a thread of their own: the game thread records each frame and hands it over at the guest's Swap, never more than one frame ahead. cast_bug (start and gliding) and avalanch now hold 240 fps at native 4K and adaptive never steps down (on one thread they drop to 60 and 120); with adaptive off cast_bug keeps the game at 60.0 fps (53.5 on one thread). The game thread takes ~12.1-12.3 ms a game frame there (p90 ~12.8-12.9), the render thread ~7.5-7.8 (p90 ~8.5-9.1), and 99.9-100% of frames are shown one refresh after the one before (the thread's presents held to mid-refresh). Threaded output equals single-threaded output (REPLAY_CHECK and the sub-frame check with the thread in a sync mode: 0 differ). The setting is Off by default; Auto is on with 4 or more cores. Frame rate: Game (60), the bridge off and Off: identical.

Report: `step79_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\8774a98e-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/`, identity runs in `id/`. Commits: `266b9f2` (item 2, first part: counters per side, the recording lock), `9e7f289` (item 1, with the rest of item 2), `abc3316` (item 3), `e146ba5` (the watchdog's one block an episode; a comment), `3493f10` (the thread's sub-frames presented at mid-refresh); plus the notes commit (check patches, harness, README rows). No xboxrecomp change.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`): stage 2, the thread itself, for Frame rate: Display (any target above 60) only; Game keeps no thread. 1 the thread (everything tagged replay-side on it; the game records, hands over a slot, queues settings, goes on; hand-over depth 1); 2 18.81's leftovers (`g_st` per side or interlocked; `g_lock` split; the overlay's hold on the thread with the game waiting in its Swap; the frame-rate switch with the thread drained; the replay-time texture fallback made safe; adaptive busy as the larger side's); 3 the setting (Auto / On / Off, `render_thread`, `RECOMP_RENDER_THREAD`; Auto by cores, the number explained with handhelds in mind; default Off until the sweep); 4 verify (threaded = single-threaded in a mode where the thread works each slot while the game waits, with REPLAY_CHECK and the sub-frame check; THREADCHECK; stress: live changes, resize, the overlay, the game's pause, level loads and loading screens, quitting while rendering, a deadlock watchdog that logs where each thread is; 30 minutes cycling levels; the first 10 main levels; ms a game frame on each thread and the evenness at 240 Hz in cast_bug start and gliding, avalanch, upstairs, and whether adaptive still steps down); 5 Game, the bridge off and Off identical to step 78. Keep the display awake, no other games, no playing (standing, or Crash's position written), each item committed.
- **Item 1, the thread (`9e7f289`):** `rt_main` (the "twoc render thread"), created at the first engagement. The game thread's Swap with the thread on (`present_thread`, under `g_rec` only): once the thread has taken the frame handed over before (`g_pend` empty), the frame just recorded is handed over (`slot_fill`: swap, time, the game side's time on it, the settings generation and, when it changed, a copy of the game side's settings; `hold` when the overlay holds the game), the thread woken, and the game waits until the slot it records into next is free (the thread has drawn that frame's last pass: freed before the last present's wait), until the overlay lets it go if it stopped at this frame, and in the sync check until the frame is done; then it takes the interpolation table set that neither the handed-over frame nor the thread's frame before uses (`g_ip` under `g_ho`), applies the game side's settings (`sq_drain(0)`) and goes on. The render thread takes each frame: the trigger file's test aids, the host side's part of the settings that frame was recorded under (`sq_apply_slot`: a frame is always replayed under the settings it was recorded for; the overlay's live preview while it holds the game still drains between overlay frames), then `replay_frame` and `replay_tail` as the Swap ran them (uploads and pins, the plan, the adaptive measure, pairing, the passes, the presents and pace, the overlay, a new plan on a mode change). Lock order `g_rec` → `g_lock` → `g_ho`; the game never takes `g_lock` while the thread runs, the thread never takes `g_rec`.
  - Engaged at the end of a one-thread Swap with nothing recorded (`rt_engage`: the setting allows it, Frame rate: Display; `g_thr`), left at a threaded Swap with the thread drained (`rt_leave`: nothing handed over, its last frame done; the host side's queued settings applied there, then the frame-rate setting taken as one thread does). Live both ways (the setting, the frame rate).
  - The first run hung at guest swap 5 and the watchdog (below) said where: the slot is free before the frame's last present, so "my next slot is free" didn't mean the thread had taken the frame handed over before, and the next hand-over overwrote it. The game now waits for the take first: that is the depth-1 rule as asked.
  - `RECOMP_RENDER_THREAD=sync` (a check): the game waits at each hand-over until the thread has finished the frame, so the two never overlap and the game thread may draw at once (REPLAY_CHECK, the sub-frame check's old path). It also engages with any recorded frames (REPLAY_CHECK turns interpolation off).
  - **Deadlock watchdog:** each thread marks its stage (the game: running, waiting for its slot, held by the overlay, waiting for the sync, draining; the thread: waiting for a frame, taking one, replaying under the host lock, the pace wait, presenting, holding under the overlay, after the presents). A watchdog thread trips when the game waits or the thread stays in one stage over `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_RT_WATCHDOG` s (5; 0 off), the overlay's hold excluded, and logs one block: what each thread is doing, for how long, at which guest swap, its instruction pointer and the code addresses near the top of its stack (module+offset; the thread suspended only while its context and stack are copied). It only logs.
  - THREADCHECK knows the thread: replay-side code on any thread but the render thread fails while it is on (but a draw made at once in the sync check), game-side code on it fails.
- **Item 2, the leftovers:** counters per side (`266b9f2`: `g_stg` the game side's, added up in `d3dh_stats` without a lock); `g_rec` the recording lock (`266b9f2`; the game's entry points take only it while the thread runs, both on one thread); the overlay's hold on the thread with the game waiting in its Swap: the game looks at Esc at its Swap (`g_ovl_open` and the pause setting, `g_ovl_pauses`) and marks the frame; the thread holds at that frame, presents overlay frames (its live changes applied between them), and lets the game go when it closes or the pause is turned off; a frame handed over before Esc goes on interpolated (the overlay opens at the next); the frame-rate switch with the thread drained (`rt_leave`); the texture fallback (`PIN_TARGET`): with the thread a texture that may be a render target is also looked up and copied at record time (once while its guest bytes don't change), and drawn from that copy only if it isn't a target at the replay: never guest memory on the thread (0 such draws in every run); `rc_maybe_target` with the thread looks at the frame handed over last (`g_rprev`, its targets kept until the game records into that slot again); adaptive busy: with the thread, the larger of the game side's time on the frame and the thread's on the frame before (from the take to its last present, less the pace waits), and a drop must save at least half the overrun on the larger side (none when the game side is the slower: no sub-frame count changes its time).
- **Item 3, the setting (`abc3316`):** Esc, Display, under Frame rate (when above 60), "Render thread: Auto / On / Off", from the next frame, saved as `render_thread` (only when not Off, the default for now: switch `key_text` to write `off` when Auto becomes the default). The line under it: "on, beside the game's thread (this PC: 8 cores)", "off, the game's thread draws every frame", or why Auto keeps it off. Checked: grabs at Auto, Off, On; the combo clicked in the held overlay: the thread started after the close, `render_thread = on` written, read back at the next start.
  - **Auto: 4 or more physical cores** (`RT_AUTO_CORES`; logical processors don't count). The process keeps about 2.7 cores busy with the thread on in cast_bug: the game thread ~12 of 16.7 ms (0.73), the render thread ~8.5 (0.5), the NV2A ack thread's spin (a whole core, 18.78), NVIDIA's driver thread ~0.2, the APU and timer threads ~0.2. With 4 cores that fits beside Windows; with 2 (4 SMT threads) the render thread would take turns with the game thread and the spin, and the hand-over's waits would only add latency. Handhelds: Steam Deck 4 cores/8 threads: on; ROG Ally Z1 6 cores and Z1 Extreme / Legion Go 8 cores: on; a 2-core laptop: off.

### 18.83 Step 80: the Legion Go package. `twoc\tools\package\make_package.ps1` (or `make_package.bat`) makes `Desktop\Crash\LegionGoPackage`, a folder that runs the game on another Windows PC: the exe with the Visual C++ runtime beside it, the disc files without saves, `Play Crash.bat`, a 72 fps launcher, `Collect logs.bat` (logs, settings, notes, a system summary and vrrprobe's display tests in one zip), a `crash_twoc.ini` for the Legion Go (16:10 at 1280x800 in a window, Match display, render thread Auto, HUD at the edges, MSAA off, anisotropy 4x) and a README with the set-up and the test list. 990,546,780 bytes (945 MiB), 2,279 files. Tested from two other folders whose paths have spaces and parentheses, launched like a double-click: it boots, reads its ini, renders 16:10 with the render thread on, and closes with its console. No game code changed.

Report: `step80_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\0e9a6755-...\scratchpad`), the last test run's log and collected zip in `evidence\`, the test driver `pt.ps1`. Commits: `980efbf` (the package tooling, vrrprobe's mode list), `00cfd26` (PACKAGE.txt names the commits the exe's sources come from); plus the notes commit.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`): a package script making the folder (the exe and every file it needs, checked with dumpbin; the game files without UDATA; `Play Crash.bat`; an ini for the Legion Go; `Collect logs.bat` with a system summary and vrrprobe built in; a README); the exe running from any folder, tested from another folder and a path with spaces; the Legion Go's set-up (144 Hz, power mode, plugged in or battery) and a test list (Match display, 72, 90 falling back to 72, 144; the hub, cast_bug's start, avalanch; Render thread Auto against Off; what to note; bringing the log back) in the README. Commit the tooling, not the package.
- **What the exe needs** (`dumpbin /dependents`): `VCRUNTIME140.dll` and `VCRUNTIME140_1.dll`, shipped app-local from VS's redist (14.44.35112; no `MSVCP140`: the C++ parts use no STL DLL); the rest is Windows 10/11: the UCRT (`api-ms-win-crt-*`), `d3d11`, `D3DCOMPILER_47`, `XINPUT1_4`, `MFPlat`/`MFReadWrite` (Media Foundation: absent only on N editions), `dbghelp`, `winmm`, `setupapi`, `dwmapi` and the usual system DLLs. SDL3 is linked statically (18.73). At run time it also loads `xaudio2_9` (not in the import table; loaded at run time) and, on NVIDIA only, `nvapi64`. The script refuses an import it doesn't know and writes the list into `PACKAGE.txt`. In the test runs every module outside System32/DriverStore was the package's own three files: the runtime loads from the folder, ahead of System32's copy.
- **Any folder.** `game_files\default.xbe` and `game_files` (the D: root) are relative to the working directory (`main.c`), as are the saves (`game_files\UDATA`, made when missing), `game_files\TDATA` and `xbox_kernel.log`; the ini is next to the exe (`GetModuleFileName`, `settings.c`); the partition images go to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\xboxrecomp` (5.2 GB on paper, sparse). The exe holds no absolute path but SDL's assert file names. `Play Crash.bat` `cd /d "%~dp0"`s, sets what `run_twoc.ps1 -HostOnly -FastTitle` sets (the user's own desktop launcher) with no watchdog, moves the run before's logs into `logs\` named by their time, and starts the exe redirected to `run_twoc.err.log`/`.out.log`; the console stays only if the exit code isn't 0.
- **Tests** (`pt.ps1`: start the .bat by ShellExecute with the working directory `C:\`, grab the window with wgcgrab, list the game's modules, then WM_CLOSE): from `scratchpad\pkg test\Legion Go (copy) A` (three runs) and a fresh copy of the final package in `scratchpad\pkg test B\Crash Legion Go` (one run, 75 s): the ini's 11 keys read (`[CFG]`), 1280x800 at 16:10, the render thread on from guest swap 2 (Auto, 8 cores), the plan Match display at 239.99 (4 frames a game frame), the menu 5.8 s in, the intro loaded and drawn (with `RECOMP_DEV_AUTOSTART` from the test's environment), the console gone with the game (exit 0). The second run moved the first's three logs into `logs\`. The 72 fps launcher: `RECOMP_FRAMERATE=72 from the command line wins`; on this 240 Hz monitor 72 isn't even, so 60 (on the Legion Go's 144 it is half). `Collect logs.bat`: a 0.1 MB zip with the logs, `logs\`, the ini, the notes, `PACKAGE.txt`, `system.txt` and `vrrprobe.txt` (this monitor: fixed refresh, 90 fps lands 8.3/12.5 ms apart, as in 18.80).
- **vrrprobe** (`980efbf`) also prints the current mode, its rotation and scale, and every mode (sizes and rates). The Legion Go's panel is portrait underneath (1600x2560 shown rotated): the log will say what Windows reports, and DWM's rotation may cost flip-model presents there. The first Legion Go has no variable refresh as far as the notes know (18.80 names the Legion Go 2 for that); the probe's two 90 fps runs will say.
- **Not reachable from a new game:** cast_bug is warp room 6's first pad and avalanch warp room 4's (the pad table `0x19A7F0`); the package has no saves, as asked, so the README tests the hub, the hub's upstairs room (the central lift; the heaviest room of 18.82's set) and Arctic Antics, and does cast_bug and avalanch only with a save that opens them. `make_package.ps1 -Save DIR` copies a UDATA folder in, only when asked (the sweep's save: `7e905e6d-...\scratchpad\root\game_files\UDATA`).
- **For handheld use:** only Esc opens the settings overlay; the README suggests a keyboard or Windows' On-Screen Keyboard (the touch screen and the touchpad work as a mouse in it). A controller chord for the overlay would be the fix.
- **Reading the logs back:** the render thread's interval line ends with "the render thread X ms a frame for a frame", its own wait for a frame (0.00: it always had one), not its cost.
- **Traps:** Claude Code's environment sets `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath=1`, so cmd won't run an exe or .bat from the current folder by its bare name ("'crash_twoc.exe' is not recognized"; also why `cmd /c vrrprobe_build.bat` failed): the launcher names the exe by `%~dp0`. Windows PowerShell 5.1's `Compress-Archive` and .NET Framework's `ZipFile.CreateFromDirectory` write backslashes into zip entry names: the collector adds entries one by one with `/`. A 5.1 script must stay ASCII (no BOM: read as the ANSI code page); `.bat` files are written with CRLF by the script whatever git checked out.
- **The user's things:** the repo's `crash_twoc.ini`, saves and `game_files` untouched (the package copies; every run used copies in scratch, deleted after); the Desktop package never ran (no `logs\`, UDATA or TDATA in it). vrrprobe took the screen 8 times for 6 s (4 collector runs).

- **Item 1 (`f80677e`, `twoc/src/saves.{c,h}`):** the write is seen at the guest's own kernel calls: NtCreateFile (`0x15AB34`), NtOpenFile (`0x15AAE8`) and NtClose (`0x15AAB0`) tapped in `recomp_manual.c` (`sv_*`, the calls passed on unchanged). XAPI opens saves as `\??\U:\<save>\...` (the `[PATH]` line shows the resolved `...\UDATA\56550003\...`); the title's own `*.xbx` beside the save folders don't count. A save opens the folder (share 3), `SaveMeta.xbx` and `crash.sav` to write with **share 0**, then re-reads both. Since a copy's handle would fail a share-0 open, the worker announces a copy before it checks that the game has no save file open or opening, and the game announces an open before checking for a copy: the worker backs off (1 s) and throws away a copy the game opened a file during; a share-0 open waits for a copy under way (two files, 12.5 KB). The backup runs 2 s after the last close; the start's backup runs in WinMain before the entry point (0.5-9 ms; ~55 ms with a restore's flushed writes). A backup is a folder with the two files and `backup.txt` (`why: at start | after a save | by hand | before a restore`); `_2` for two in a second; nothing is made until there is a save. Pruning deletes only folders named that way and only their three files. Restore: the marker `UDATA_backups\restore at next start.txt` (the backup's name), done at the next start after that start's backup (temp file + MoveFileEx, flushed); a failure: a message box, the save untouched, the marker deleted. `RECOMP_SAVE_BACKUPS=0` off; `RECOMP_DEV_SAVE_COPY_DELAY_MS` (test) stretches each copy. Overlay: Saves tab (no scrolling table: a scrolling table is a child window the pad's navigation skips), LB/RB over 4 tabs.

### 18.87 Step 84: the game thread's work a frame, cheaper, with the same output. At cast_bug's start (the render thread on, native 4K at 240 Hz) the game thread went from 12.19 ms a frame to 6.69 (avalanch 9.98 -> 6.04, upstairs 7.22 -> 4.16); the render thread is unchanged (7.3 ms there). Texture signatures are ~10x faster over the same bytes; program draws' vertices go through a vertex cache (on by default after its checks and a 25-level sweep: every draw compared with the old conversion, 0 differ) whose hits the recording keeps by reference; the per-draw repeats (a clock read around every wrapped call, the program walk and hash, the interpolation keys, mallocs, copies of constants and vertices) are gone; Release builds use link-time code generation (the game's own code ~13% faster). The NV2A ack thread can wait instead of spinning (half a core back) but then slows the game's kicks by 0.3-0.6 ms: left off. Projected Legion Go game thread at cast_bug's start: 14.7-16.7 ms (was 26.8-30.5, measured 27-30), where its render thread (18.9-20.6 ms at 144 Hz) becomes the limit.

- **a, content_sig (`64d3879`):** the same bytes as before (whole up to 256 KB, else 4 KB + 64 x 64 B + 4 KB), hashed with four 64-bit lanes (xxHash64's round and avalanche) instead of byte-wise FNV-1a; a signature is only compared with the one before. No dirty tracking is possible without reading the bytes: guest memory is file-mapped views (no write watch), aliased, written by the recompiled code and the kernel's file reads without hooks (page protection would break the kernel's reads). Checks: `step84_sig_check.patch` (old FNV beside the new, agreement on "changed") 0 disagree of 1.52 M / 0.66 M / 0.63 M signatures and 0.48 M in H2 Oh No with its pause; none of those runs ever saw a texture change in place (animated textures are new headers here), so `twoc/tools/diag/sigtest.c` tests it directly: 189,000 random changes (byte, word, run; 4 B-1.4 MB, the sampled form included), both saw the same 180,220 (the rest outside the samples for both), 0 disagree.
- **b, the vertex cache (`5e655ac`, `80f47de`, `c39ede8`):** the converter now runs a register at a time (vs_fetch's arithmetic, format decided once, plain loads): no gain alone, it is the miss path. An entry is keyed by the draw's layout (each register's declaration, default rule, the stream address at vertex lo, stride, count) and keeps a copy of the bytes the conversion reads (each stream's span from vertex lo's lowest attribute byte to the last vertex's highest) and the vertices converted from that copy. **Guest writes are detected by comparing those spans with guest memory at every lookup** (memcmp, byte for byte): a hit is exactly what converting now would give. The gaps a conversion counted are counted again at each hit (the log's counters unchanged). 4,096 sets x 4 ways, LRU; emptied past 96 MB (cast_bug holds ~27 MB); hits are recorded by reference: vertices live in a block shared with the recordings (`d3dh_hold_t`), a recorded draw holds a reference until its frame is replayed (`replay_done`, a dropped frame, `rc_forget`), an entry converted again while one is held takes a new block. Hit rate at cast_bug 99.6% (the first, direct-mapped table 89%: collisions). `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_VCACHE=0` off, `=check` / `=check0` compare every draw with the old conversion (with / without the cache), `RECOMP_DEV_VCACHE_MB` a test limit. Checks: check mode 0 of 5.86 M / 1.50 M / 0.40 M differ (cache off too); `step84_hold_check.patch` (released blocks never freed, a replay from one counted) with the thread really on: 30.5 M replayed draws from blocks, 0 from a released one; the sweep (`sweep/sv.sh`): the 25 main levels from their pads, 40 s standing each, hold check + `=check` + THREADCHECK: all loaded, 0 differ (0.42-1.75 M draws a level), 0 early; seashell, H2 Oh No, fire_r, coral, volcano change ~0.5 M program draws' vertices a run (coral 36% of lookups) and still match. With a 4 MB limit (emptied 15,102 times in cast_bug): 0 differ, 0 early. Then on by default (`c39ede8`).
- **c, repeats and copies (`a9595da`, `d7965f0`), all exact:** `d3db_leave` reads QPC only around calls the mirror works on (before: every wrapped call, `SetVertexShaderConstant`'s included: -0.38 ms at cast_bug); the program's walk and FNV kept per object with the dwords it read (reused while they compare equal); `texel_patch` scans only with a linear texture bound; the interpolation key's pixel-shader part (56 states byte-wise, twice a draw) kept for the last two shaders; scratch buffers for index lists and uncached vertices instead of malloc/free; a program draw's constants (3 KB) and uncached vertices built in the frame's arena (`d3dh_rec_alloc`) and kept there by `rec_draw` (`ra_keep`); `ic_record`'s camera-position scan over each program's own register list with the tolerance once a draw, `ic_obj`'s kept states as a switch (`step84_ic_check.patch`: 0 of 7.6 M scans and 13.7 M keys differ).
- **d, the build:** a snapshot (items a-c, cache off) built five ways (`sweep/cfg84.sh`, `sweep/pg.sh`), two rounds, game thread ms (render thread) at cast_bug / avalanch / upstairs: `/O2 /Ob2` as now 9.19 (7.17) / 7.41 / 5.28; `/arch:AVX2` 9.19 (7.52) / 7.31 / 5.23; link-time (`/GL`, `/LTCG`) 8.60 (7.03) / 6.90 / 5.45 (its two upstairs runs 5.85 and 5.04); both 8.50 / 6.79 / 4.90; PGO on link-time 8.10 (6.51) / 6.59 (5.96) / 4.79 (4.04). The level was already `/O2` everywhere and `/fp:precise` stays (no fast-math tried). **Adopted: link-time code generation** (`1e89a39`, CMake `TWOC_LTCG` ON: the game's own code 4.64 -> 4.02 ms, exe 17.9 -> 13.7 MB, build time unchanged at ~1 min). AVX2 not (no gain; would need a fallback; both handhelds have it). PGO not (another 0.5 ms on each thread, but an instrumented build trained ~5 min and relinked for every release, its data not committable: a package-script option if wanted).
- **e, the NV2A ack thread (`xboxrecomp` `e2d7a1b`, local, off):** `RECOMP_NV2A_ACK_WAIT=N` spins while it acknowledges something and 2 ms after (`RECOMP_NV2A_ACK_SPIN`), then waits N us a pass on a high-resolution timer; off, the loop is unchanged (the 18.29 rule: with it on only the latency of GET updates moves, not their order). Spinning it takes 98-99% of a core; waiting 100 or 250 us, 40-56%, but the game kicks ~150 times a frame and waits on the kick after a quiet spell: the game thread +0.38 / +0.26 / +0.27-0.61 ms (p90 +0.5). Left off. On the Legion Go the freed half core might buy clock on the shared budget: `RECOMP_NV2A_ACK_WAIT=100` is the test. A proper fix wakes the thread from D3D's kick (a wrapper) instead of a timer.

### 18.89 Step 86: Vulkan stage 0 (DXVK, Proton, the plan; a probe committed), and the settings menu by F1 or Start held, with a startup message. DXVK 3.1.1 runs everything with the same pixels (the first 8 host dumps byte-identical to D3D11's); the game thread is unchanged, the render thread costs ~+10% of a core (its "ms a frame" doubles, but that is waiting inside DXVK's Present), and 240 Hz evenness drops to 93-99% in DXVK's estimated statistics. Our own per-draw translation and interpolation are ~80% of the render thread's busy time on D3D11 (the runtime and driver ~15%), so the recommendation is a native Vulkan backend staged behind a backend-neutral first stage: bake each recorded draw once a game frame. DXC compiles all 44 of the game's HLSL shaders to SPIR-V (`-HV 2018`). Stage 1, `RECOMP_VULKAN=probe`, is in. F1 opens and closes the settings overlay (Esc only closes it); a controller's Start held 1 s does too, a tap reaching the game at its release; "Press F1 to open the settings menu" shows for 8 s at the start (`startup_message`). Bridge off and the default run: identical (the dumps), two new log lines.

Likely problems: under Steam the .bat's variables aren't set (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE=1 RECOMP_VBLANK=1 RECOMP_AC97_READY=1 %command%`, or defaults in the exe); Steam Input's virtual pad versus SDL's HIDAPI through winebus (double or generic pads); the frame plan's `QueryDisplayConfig` / DWM timing under gamescope; DXVK's estimated statistics; the overlay's size at 96 DPI.
- **DXC (1.3):** `scratchpad/hlsldump/` is a stand-in d3dcompiler_47.dll (it writes every `D3DCompile` source, then forwards to System32's). 44 distinct sources over the three places, 8 more levels, every AA mode, the overlay and the counter: all compile with `dxc -spirv -T {vs,ps}_6_0 -HV 2018 -fspv-target-env=vulkan1.1 -O3` (DXC validates). With HLSL 2021 the 16 vertex programs and SMAA fail only on vector ternaries (`select()`). 15-55 ms a shader with dxc.exe's start.
- **The plan (1.3, in the report):** xbox_d3d8 is 8,098 lines on D3D11 (571 naming it); d3d_host.c ~26 functions (swap chain, present, MSAA, copies, dumps, overlay); aa.c; ImGui's DX11 backend; d3d_bridge.c none. The interface: the D3D8 subset the replay calls (24 methods), or better a "baked draw" both backends take, plus a host surface (swap chain, present with timing, targets, MSAA, copies, readback). Stages: 1 the probe (done); 2 bake draws on D3D11 (backend-neutral; REPLAY_CHECK and the sub-frame check); 3 a Vulkan draw device off-screen (DXC at run time with a SPIR-V cache, VMA), checked against D3D11 by readback; 4 the present path (present_wait / present_timing, interval N, fullscreen, MSAA, copies, dumps); 5 FXAA/SMAA and `imgui_impl_vulkan`; 6 the render thread and interpolation, measured on both machines, then an opt-in. Stages 3-6 ~6-9k lines. **Needs from the user:** the Legion Go's DXVK-test runs against the package's own, and a probe run there (the next package).
- **Stage 1 (`17740ff`):** `twoc/src/vk_probe.{c,h}`, `RECOMP_VULKAN=probe`: volk loads vulkan-1.dll at run time (the exe's 31 DLLs unchanged), an instance (1.3 if offered), the D3D11 adapter's GPU by LUID (`d3d_host.c` passes it after the device line), a device with present_id + present_wait when both features are there, a window of its own (no activation; closing it ends the probe, not the game), a FIFO swap chain, cleared frames on its own thread, `[VK]` lines: the loader, each GPU (driver, API, LUID match), 28 extensions a backend would use (has / lacks), the surface's present modes, the swap chain, and every 10 s the frames presented and present_wait's gaps. Here: RTX 4080 SUPER, Vulkan 1.4.351, all but GOOGLE_display_timing (present_timing, present_wait2, descriptor_buffer, shader_object, extended_dynamic_state3, external_memory_win32 among them), ~230 frames a second, gaps 4.56 ms median (rough through DWM); the game kept 60 / 240 beside it. Vendored: `twoc/third_party/vulkan` (Vulkan-Headers 1.4.363, the C headers), `twoc/third_party/volk` (1.4.350).

### 18.90 Step 87: Vulkan stage 2, baked draws. A frame drawn more than once (its sub-frames) is translated at its first pass, each draw captured by xbox_d3d8 as it is made, and its later passes draw from the captures with only the interpolated registers (or fixed-function matrices) written and only what differs bound; on by default (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_BAKE=0` off). At native 4K, 240 Hz, the render thread's ms a game frame: cast_bug's start 6.89 -> 4.68, avalanch 6.30 -> 4.11, upstairs 4.18 -> 2.65 (-32 to -37%; 1.04-1.08 -> 0.68-0.71 us a draw and pass), the game thread unchanged; at 120 fps (2 frames a game frame) cast_bug -13%. Projected Legion Go render thread at cast_bug's start, 144 Hz: 18.9-20.6 -> ~15.4-16.8 ms, about its projected game thread. REPLAY_CHECK and the sub-frame check: 0 differ everywhere asked; the 25-level check-mode sweep 24 clean, western not (the check's own floor there, with the bake off too). Frame rate: Game and the bridge off: identical.

Added during the step: anything skipped or failed goes under "NOT DONE" at the report's top with the command that would finish it.
- **xbox_d3d8 (`7dc1f6e`, local, perf only):** `d3d8_BakeCapture(b)` before an UP draw; at the draw, after its translation, `bake_fill` copies what `g_d3d8_bound` holds (VS, layout, PS, blend, depth-stencil + reference, rasterizer, 4 samplers, VS cb 0/1, PS cb), the viewport (as `dev_SetViewport` bound it), topology, stride, index format, count / first / base vertex, the rings' generation, and `d3d8_shaders_bake` the fixed-function transform and lighting blocks and the pixel block as uploaded (built in `g_ff_xf`/`g_ff_lt` now and copied into the mapped buffers; identical blocks share one address in a per-frame bake memory). `d3d8_DrawBaked(b, srv[4], vs_consts, fresh, ff_m)` binds what differs, all four texture views and the viewport (as the ordinary calls do), and draws; S_FALSE when `d3d8_bake_gen` or the rings moved since (the caller draws it the ordinary way). A moved fixed-function draw's matrices go through `ff_xf_matrices` (the ordinary path's code, moved into a function). xbox_d3d8's D3D8 state is left as it was; the held copies stay true. `d3d8_bake_gen` is bumped by the VSH cache's eviction, the combiners' LRU eviction, the fixed-function pixel shaders' eviction, a full sampler or state cache. Blend / depth-stencil / rasterizer objects are cached by description (64 each) instead of released and made again at every change (the runtime returns the same object for a description). **The constants ring:** with D3D11.1 `ConstantBufferOffsetting`, a captured program draw writes `g_vsh_constants` into a 32 MB ring (the block before when nothing changed) bound with `VSSetConstantBuffers1`; a baked draw with its registers as captured binds that block, a moved one writes a new one (`g_d3d8_bound.vs_cb1_first` kept). `d3d8_BakeReset` (a capturing frame's start): the bake memory reused, and the vertex, index and constant rings start over at their next write when they may not hold the frame (the last frame's bytes, a quarter more, 1 MB): a mid-frame wrap had sent ~6% of later passes' draws in cast_bug the ordinary way (177,000 per 10 s; after: 138). `tests/d3d8_smoke/test_a8.c` stubs.
- **twoc (`bc7a146`, `7f20eb4`, `224a7b9`):** `replay_pass` captures at a frame's first pass when it gets more than one (`g_bk_plan`: its sub-frames, or `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY=N`) and draws the later ones from the captures (`draw_cmd`, `draw_baked`); textures fetched every pass as before; `ic_draw` says whether it moved a draw; a draw captured at a moved sub-frame gives its registers / matrices again at the others (`bake_t.moved`: the check found the t = 1 pass reusing the first sub-frame's ring block, a third of compared passes off, fixed in `7f20eb4`). `[D3DB] host baked draws: N captured at first passes, N drawn from their capture, N drawn the ordinary way in later passes` each interval, and a start-up line. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY_CHECK` keeps `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY=N`, each pass from the frame's start (so baked passes compare with the frame drawn at once). On by default (`224a7b9`).

At 144 Hz (k = 2.4) 5.03 -> 4.10 ms, x0.815; applied to its 18.9-20.6 ms (18.84): ~15.4-16.8 ms, where its game thread was projected after step 84 (14.7-16.7, 18.87). A projection: the next package's test e (bake off) against a measures it.
- **The baked-draw interface (for a second backend):** per recorded draw, captured at the end of its translation at a frame's first pass: (1) a **pipeline key**: vertex shader (a program's microcode hash, or the fixed-function one), vertex input (the program's input mask + texture-coordinate field, or the FVF; one stream, the draw's stride), pixel shader (the combiner state, or the fixed-function signature), blend / depth-stencil / rasterizer descriptions, topology (fans and quads already lists), the target's format and samples; (2) **dynamic state**: viewport (in host pixels), stencil reference (blend factor 1, sample mask all ones); (3) **resources**: four texture views, fetched every pass (a target resolved and bound by its draw), and four samplers (filter, U/V address, anisotropy); (4) **constants**: the program's 192 registers (given by the pass: the recording's, or a copy with c96-c99 / the camera-position registers / bones from c124 / HUD c96 rewritten), or the fixed-function transform block (its matrices made again when moved) and lighting block; the pixel block; identical blocks share one address; (5) **geometry**: first vertex / index, count, base vertex, index format in the frame's rings, valid while their generation holds; (6) **validity**: a generation bumped by any cache release a capture may point to. On D3D11 that is `D3D8Baked` with object pointers from xbox_d3d8's caches. On Vulkan: (1) a cached VkPipeline per key (or extended dynamic state 3), (2) dynamic state, (3) a descriptor set or push descriptors per texture / sampler quadruple, (4) uniform blocks in a per-frame ring with dynamic offsets (unmoved blocks written once a game frame, moved ones per pass), (5) the frame's vertex and index buffers with offsets.
- **The next Legion Go package (not built):** `make_package.ps1 -Force` from a clean tree, no save, should carry (1) step 87's exe (F1, Start held, the startup message, baked draws on; PACKAGE.txt names xboxrecomp `7dc1f6e` on `e2d7a1b`; no new imports); (2) `Desktop\Crash\DXVK-test\crash_twoc.exe` replaced by it (it holds step 85's `1e89a39`) and its README's line 75 ("F1 is not in this exe yet: Esc ...") made F1 / Start held; (3) `Play Crash (Vulkan probe).bat` (`RECOMP_VULKAN=probe`, one optional minute in the hub; committed in `files/`, `97b630a`); (4) `Play Crash (bake off).bat` (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_BAKE=0`, the first test's e, round 1: cast_bug's start against a; committed); the README's and Test notes' texts for both (committed). Reading the zip: the `host baked draws` lines (the ordinary-way count near 0), the render thread's ms (the interpolation line's "replay side") against 18.9-20.6, e against a, the probe's `[VK]` lines.

### 18.95 Step 92: ambient occlusion, an option (Off / SSAO / GTAO), off by default. No xbox_d3d8 change: with it on the back buffer gets a depth of the host's own that a shader can read; the pass goes in inside the world flush (0x8929A) at its first back-buffer draw that doesn't write depth, so blended things, the stencil shadow, particles, fades and the HUD come after it; recorded among the frame's commands, so every sub-frame draws it under its own camera. GTAO is Intel's XeGTAO (MIT, vendored in `twoc/third_party/xegtao`), SSAO a hemisphere SSAO on XeGTAO's depth MIPs and denoiser. Verdict: it suits the game as a subtle option (tower bases, door arches, snow trees, pad pedestals grounded; no halos from particles or the HUD, no sub-frame flicker), but characters darken a little with GTAO and the hub floor's reflection shows faint AO through the floor; left out of High on purpose. GPU a pass at native 4K: GTAO 0.79-0.87 ms, SSAO 0.73, half resolution 0.24-0.25; at 1080p 0.25-0.42 / 0.25 / 0.12; no visible cost here at 240 Hz. Default run identical (231 opens, `[PATH]`, 8 of 8 dumps).

Report: `step92_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\cadb7a56-...\scratchpad`), images numbered in `report92\` (scene sets off / GTAO / SSAO side by side, AO-alone views, the menu), runs in `h\runs\`, exe copies in `sw\` (B before = the step-91 package exe, A1-A5 prototypes, F final, U the menu test with its ini), identity in `id\` (`idcmp_BF.txt`). Commits (twoc): `88f448a` (items 1-3, built on its own), `a18b58a` (item 4, the setting), `56890dc` (items 5-6 tools), plus the notes commit; xboxrecomp unchanged.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`; the asked-for `next_prompt_ao.txt` doesn't exist, this was the AO prompt): 1 the depth surfaces, readable? (an xbox_d3d8 change only if local and inert when off, else stop and ask); 2 run AO between the world flush (0x8929A) and the overlay (0x89679), and what to do with the sky, particles, water, glass, the stencil shadow, fog, the underwater pass, render-to-texture; 3 a GTAO prototype (XeGTAO if its licence allows), normals from depth, denoise, half resolution, MSAA, render scale, per sub-frame; 4 F1: Off / SSAO / GTAO + Strength, `ao`, `ao_strength`, live, off by default, only in High (or say why not); 5 GPU ms a pass at 4K and 1080p, render-thread ms, pacing in cast_bug, avalanch, upstairs at 240 Hz interpolated; 6 grabs off against on in the hub, Arctic Antics, cast_bug, H2 Oh No and a boss: halos, fog, sub-frame flicker, the HUD; say whether it looks good.
- **1. Depth.** Every depth surface is D24_UNORM_S8_UINT with BIND_DEPTH_STENCIL alone: xbox_d3d8's back buffer's (`d3d8_device.c`), `CreateDepthStencilSurface`'s (render targets' own, the stand-in back buffer of a live resize / render scale), twoc's MSAA `ms_surface`s; none readable. No xbox_d3d8 change (so no rule refinement): with AO on, `bb_rebuild` always makes the back buffer a target of its own (even at xbox_d3d8's size) and its depth with `ao_depth_surface`: R24G8_TYPELESS, DEPTH_STENCIL | SHADER_RESOURCE (multisampled with MSAA), xbox_d3d8's surface wrapper given a D24S8 DSV as `ms_surface` does, `g_bb.dsrv` an R24_UNORM_X8 view (TEXTURE2DMS with MSAA). The smallest xbox_d3d8 change would have been the bind flag and a typeless format in CreateDepthStencilSurface. Off (`g_ao_depth` 0): made as before. The projection is standard D3D (Arctic Antics: near 0.3, far ~50; P22 1.006, P32 -0.30), taken from `0x242398` at the world pass's start (widened at 16:9 already).
- **2. Placement.** A draw log of Arctic Antics (`sweep/dlsum.py`): the sky in its own flush (`0x88BC4`, at the far plane); the world flush: ~200 opaque draws, 20 alpha-tested ones writing depth, 43 blended without depth writes (a second, soft-edged pass over the alpha-tested ones, and the characters' second, blended layer: Crash is drawn opaque at #20 and blended again at #270), 38 stencil-shadow draws and its darkening; then the snow (512-vertex point sprites, additive), fades and full-screen quads in other flushes, the overlay last. So the occlusion goes in inside the world pass (earlier than "between world and overlay"): d3d_bridge.c's `d3db_list_flush` calls `d3dh_world_pass(1, projection)` / `(0)`; on the game side `ao_world_draw` counts the pass's opaque back-buffer draws (ZENABLE and ZWRITEENABLE, the fog of the last: FOGENABLE, FOGTABLEMODE, START, END, DENSITY) and at the first that doesn't write depth after 8 of them (`AO_MIN_DRAWS`), or at the pass's end, `ao_mark` records `RC_AO` (an `ao_mark_t`: projection, fog) among the frame's commands, or draws it at once when frames aren't recorded (the REPLAY_CHECK way: both). `replay_pass` draws it each pass (`ao_exec`), so every sub-frame and baked pass gets its own depth's AO; ao.c puts back every state it sets (shaders, layout, views, viewport, blend, depth, raster) and uses PS slots b8/t8-t10 only, so xbox_d3d8's and the baked draws' idea of what is bound holds. Render targets and frames without a world pass (the pause) get none; a world pass drawn into a target gets none. Counters: `[AO]` line "placed at a blended draw N, at the world pass's end N, world passes without one N" (Arctic: all at a blended draw).
- **3. The pass (`twoc/src/ao.c`, `ao.h`).** XeGTAO, commit `a5b1686`, MIT (Copyright 2016-2021 Intel): `XeGTAO.h` and `XeGTAO.hlsli` unchanged in `twoc/third_party/xegtao` (LICENSE.txt, README.twoc.txt), embedded by CMakeLists.txt as `xegtao_hlsl.h` and compiled by D3DCompile through an include handler (~220-260 ms at the first use); fxc can't swizzle a literal as DXC can (`2.xx`), so `hlsli_for_fxc` gives it a copy with those literals in parentheses. Entry points after vaGTAO.hlsl; `ao_constants` is GTAOUpdateConstants in C. Passes (at the back buffer's size, or half with `ao_half`): (1) a pixel shader copies the depth into an R32F texture (sample 0 with MSAA; the nearest of each 2x2 at half); (2) `XeGTAO_PrefilterDepths16x16` (view depth, 5 MIPs); (3) GTAO: `XeGTAO_MainPass` 3 slices x 3 steps ("High"), FP32 depths, normals from depth in place; or SSAO: 16 hemisphere points (golden-angle spiral, cosine) over the same normals, turned per pixel by XeGTAO's Hilbert R2 noise, read from the MIP their distance asks for, Crytek's range check; both writing XeGTAO's working term and edges; (4) `XeGTAO_Denoise` 2 passes (one left grain: XeGTAO expects TAA); (5) a full-screen multiply (blend ZERO / SRC_COLOR, alpha kept) on the back buffer's view, MSAA's every sample, a bilateral upsample by depth at half size, nothing at depth 1, faded by the strength and by the fog (its table mode from view depth). Noise index 0 (no TAA): the same pattern every frame and sub-frame. Radius 0.5 game units (XeGTAO's default; the hub sweep: 0.3 too faint in levels, 0.8 darkens characters and the hub floor more). Test aids: `RECOMP_AO_DEBUG=1` (the occlusion alone, grey: what shows in colour is drawn after it), `RECOMP_AO_GPU=1` (D3D11 timestamps a pass, read back without waiting, averaged in the `[AO]` line every 10 s), `RECOMP_AO_RADIUS`, `RECOMP_AO_DENOISE`, trigger file `set aoradius|aodenoise|aohalf`. `sweep/aofxc.py` compiles every entry with the SDK's fxc offline.
- **4. The setting.** F1, Display, after Anti-aliasing: "Ambient occlusion" Off / SSAO / GTAO, and while on "Strength" 0-100% and "Half resolution", each with its (?) text, "Now: off" if it couldn't be set up. `ao = off | ssao | gtao`, `ao_strength` (100 unwritten), `ao_half` (0 unwritten); `RECOMP_AO`, `_STRENGTH`, `_HALF`; live through the settings queue's host side (`set_ao`, which makes the back buffer again the first time it is turned on; turned off, the readable depth stays until the next start); trigger file `set ao|aostrength|aohalf`. In no preset (the presets were removed in 18.96): High left without it on purpose (it changes the look, darkens characters a little; adding it is a `g_presets` line). Checked: an ini with gtao / 60 / 1 read back (`[CFG]` lines, "gtao, strength 60%", working textures 1280x720 at 2560x1440), the menu (grab), SSAO by a click, live, and the ini written on close (ao = ssao, 60, 1).
- **5. Cost** (`sweep/ms92.sh` + `ms92sum.py`; RTX 4080, 240 Hz, render thread on, Frame rate: Display, adaptive on, 16:9, HUD at the edges, standing; off / GTAO / SSAO / off live). GPU ms a pass (one a sub-frame, 4 a game frame here): native 4K GTAO 0.79-0.87 (depth 0.03, prefilter 0.07, occlusion 0.55, denoise 0.11, apply 0.05), SSAO 0.73 (1.39-1.50 before it read the MIPs), half GTAO 0.25, half SSAO 0.24; 1080p GTAO 0.25-0.42, SSAO 0.25, half 0.12. In cast_bug's start, avalanch and upstairs, at 4K and 1080p, every mode: 60.0 updates a second, ~240 presents a second, 99.9-100% of frames shown 1 refresh apart, no adaptive step, the render thread's ms within 0.07 of off (cast_bug 4K 4.73 off, 4.76 GTAO, 4.77 SSAO). Legion Go (estimated, not measured): ~8-10x this GPU's time, ~2.5-4 ms a pass at 1920x1200 x 2.4 passes at 144 Hz: half resolution there.
- **6. Checks** (`sweep/aocmp.sh`, `aoset.py`, `scenes.sh`, `flk.sh`, `flkan.py`): the hub, Arctic Antics, cast_bug, H2 Oh No, Drain Damage, avalanch, off / GTAO / SSAO from one standing run each (live switches, full-size sub-frame dumps); MSAA 4x full and half; the AO-alone view in the hub, H2 Oh No, at a level start (the HUD in full colour over the grey AO: clean) and the pause (the menu clean; the still is the frame captured when it opened, with its AO, as a screenshot); sub-frames of a glide in cast_bug (AO alone): smooth from sub-frame to sub-frame, no alternating change. H2 Oh No: the AO fades into its fog almost completely. Halos: none from particles, snow or the HUD (drawn after); SSAO leaves a light one on the hub floor around Crash's legs, GTAO hardly any. The hub floor is a blended layer over a reflection of the room that writes depth, so AO of the reflection shows faintly through it (a grainy patch at radius 0.8+). Identity (step-91 package exe B against F, `id.sh`/`idcmp.sh`): 231 opens in order, 356 `[PATH]`, 8 of 8 dumps, exit 3, bridge off and host-only; only timing-dependent APU lines differ.
- **Traps:** heredocs ate backslashes twice more (a `\n` in an ao.c string and a Windows path; asserts and a syntax error caught them: Write-tool scripts only). `shutil.copy2` keeps a file's old time: restoring sources that way left MSBuild's newer objects in place and the exe silently the old one (touch after a restore; check the exe's size). Start in the pad file is `buttons=0x10` (`pad:start` does nothing). The AO's `[AO]` line needs 10 s of one mode; `ms92sum.py` counts "off" only after a mode has been on (the load's intervals are lighter).

### 18.97 Step 94: the Legion Go zip of step 93's package, D3D11 against DXVK (read only, two sessions). Three runs, plugged in, Legion Space Performance, 1920x1200 at 144 Hz, cast_bug's start. No crash, watchdog, stall or display recovery; one audio underrun at boot each. **D3D11 holds 60 updates and 144 shown: game thread 11.7-12.1 ms, render thread 8.9-9.2.** **DXVK's +4.2 ms on the render thread is ~2 ms real** (its draw calls 1.86-2.07 against 1.43-1.49 µs, and our pairing slower beside its threads) **and ~2 ms waiting moved into its Present.** It also costs ~1 ms on the game thread from contention, so its adaptive rate drops to 72 where D3D11's doesn't. **The render thread doesn't rise with temperature.** About 2.5 minutes in, the CPU clock fell 3,640 -> 3,132 MHz while the temperature fell 83 -> 66 C: a firmware power limit, not heat (Windows' passive limit 100% throughout); 60 / 144 still held. **By 18.89's rule our own per-draw work, not AMD's D3D11 driver, is the bigger cost there.** The driver-heavy baked draws are 1.4-1.55x the desktop's, about the game's own code (1.5x); our translation is 1.9-2.1x and the mirror 2.2x. A native Vulkan backend would save a guessed 1.2-2.5 ms of the render thread (not the limiting thread), so it isn't worth building for the handheld now. On AMD the swap chain goes onto a hardware plane even in a window, 0.2 s after the first present (no stall), so step 90's short swap chain doesn't keep DWM composing there; the deferral is the remaining prevention, unproven for D3D11 fullscreen starts. Fixed: events.txt's dates said UTC but were local; they now say local with the UTC time beside them.

Reports: `step94_report.md` (items 1-2, the zip unzipped in `lg\`, `iv.txt`, `split.py` / `split.txt`) in the session scratch folder `C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\1a67c6ba-...\scratchpad`, and `step94b_report.md` (items 3-7; `item6.py` / `item6.txt`, `ev_test.py` and `evt\`) in `...\ce6d799b-...\scratchpad`. Commits: the collect_logs fix and the notes, one commit.

### 18.98 Step 95: cheaper per-draw work on both threads, measured; PGO as a package option; the Legion Go package rebuilt. Item 1 measured first: the draws' D3D11 calls are timed (one draw in 32), first-pass repeats counted, and a "CPU clock down" power line. **98% of cast_bug's first-pass draws repeat the frame before's state** (avalanch 97%, upstairs 75% once the count read only the lights in use), so cut 3 went ahead. Desktop, native 4K at 240 Hz (4 frames a game frame), standing, ms a game frame, the start build against all four cuts with PGO: **render thread cast_bug 4.58 -> 2.92 (-36%), avalanch 4.02 -> 3.40 (-15%), upstairs 2.68 -> 2.30 (-14%); game thread 6.71 -> 6.35, 6.07 -> 5.85, 4.14 -> 3.96 (-4 to -5%).** Cut 3 is most of it: a first pass's draw from the frame before's capture, and its vertices and indices from where the rings still held them (1.52 -> 0.45 us a draw). Cut 2 halved the bytes recorded (8.2 -> 4.2 MB a game frame) but left the desktop's game thread as it was. Cut 4 took pairing 0.82 -> 0.56 ms, the same pairs. PGO adds 1.4-4.7% on the game thread and 4-5% on the render thread, with identical output: the package is built with it, while the script's default stays without (it needs this PC's display and ~13 minutes). On the desktop, NVIDIA's D3D11 calls inside our draws plus Present are 28-31% of the render thread before the cuts and 21-30% after. Every check is 0 differ: REPLAY_CHECK, the sub-frame check (first passes too), the pair check against the old pairing, and identity. Legion Go projection at cast_bug's start, 144 Hz: render thread 9.2 -> ~6.0-6.9 ms, game thread 12.0 -> ~11.2-11.7.

- **Item 1 (`c4f2b18`, xboxrecomp `19a4df7`, sampling halved in a follow-up):**
  - xbox_d3d8's COM macros for the context calls the draws use (Map, Unmap, Draw*, the IA / VS / PS / RS / OM sets, UpdateSubresource, copies, clears, resolves, and VSSetConstantBuffers1 at its site) go through inline wrappers that time each call while `d3d8_ctx_timing` is set.
  - twoc times one draw in 32 of each kind (drawn once, a first pass's captured draw, drawn from its capture, a later pass's drawn the ordinary way, and with cut 3 a first pass from the frame before's capture). One QPC call (~16 ns here, measured at the start) is taken off each timed call; the other 31 give the kind's untimed us a draw. Present is timed at every present.
  - `[D3DB] host D3D11 in the draws`: each kind's draws, us a draw, D3D11 us and calls in it, and the share; the draws' ms a game frame and the D3D11 ms in them; Present's ms.
  - `[D3DB] host first passes repeating the game frame before`: every 64th capturing frame keeps 64-bit hashes of its first-pass draws, the state alone and all of it, and the next game frame looks its own up.
  - Cost at 240 Hz: at one in 16 and every 32nd, the render thread +0.15-0.29 ms a game frame (the start build against item 1's, two rounds). Halved, 0.06 ms less again (cast_bug 3.06 -> 3.00, avalanch 3.50 -> 3.44 against cut 4); fewer passes at 144 Hz cost less.
  - The power log: `CPU clock down` when the MHz stay more than 10% under the run's level (the highest 60-s average, from 30 s in) for 10 readings in a row, and `CPU clock back up` once within 5% again. `RECOMP_DEV_CLOCK_FAKE=N` is a test aid; both lines fired with it (+120 s, +142 s, 20 s down).
  - Measured, desktop NVIDIA, before the cuts (N1), a game frame:

    | place | first pass, us a draw (D3D11 share) | drawn from a capture | the draws' D3D11 ms + Present | of the render thread |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | cast_bug | 1.52 (54-61%) | 0.10 (48%) | 1.19 + 0.17 | 28% |
    | avalanch | 1.36 (25%) | 0.14 (45%) | 1.15 + 0.17 | 31% |
    | upstairs | 1.35 (25%) | 0.12 (49%) | 0.63 + 0.17 | 28% |

    cast_bug's first-pass share is high because it uploaded 10.4 MB of vertices a game frame through the rings' Map / Unmap (a temporary ring diagnostic).
  - Repeats (the state; everything too): cast_bug 98.2-98.3% (0.4%). Avalanch and upstairs read 51% and 56% at first: the state hash read the unused light slots, whatever the bridge's buffer held there. Fixed in cut 3: 97.1% (67.5%) and 74.7% (48.6%).
- **Cut 2 (`c23945a`, xboxrecomp `caed643`):**
  - A temporary diagnostic first: only 15-17% of program draws have all 192 registers equal to the draw before's, but most change only 1-3 of 12 blocks of 16. SetVertexShaderConstant's "fresh" mask missed changes in 0.1-7% of draws, so blocks are compared, not trusted to it.
  - `d3dh_vprog_t.blk[12]`. The bridge builds the 192 registers on its stack (not in the arena). `rec_consts` copies a block only when memcmp says it differs from the block the program draw before in this frame recorded (`fslot_t.kprev`, forgotten whenever the slot's arena starts over), and shares that one otherwise.
  - The replay side reads by block: `VREG` / `vk_gather`. `vs_bind` uploads a run of adjacent blocks at a time. `draw_baked` hands the blocks to xbox_d3d8, whose `d3d8_DrawBaked` takes 12 pointers into the constants ring.
  - `ic_draw` changes registers in place in the shared blocks and puts each back after its draw, as before. The bones are read and written a register at a time.
  - `[D3DB] host recorded constants`: 1.73 / 1.65 / 1.83 of the 12 blocks copied a program draw. Recorded 8.2 -> 4.2 MB a game frame at cast_bug.
  - Timing: game thread 6.72 -> 6.70, 6.11 -> 6.15, 4.11 -> 4.14 (no change on the desktop); render thread 4.86 -> 4.71, 4.31 -> 4.31, 2.84 -> 2.87.
- **Cut 3 (`49b540b`, xboxrecomp `6d115bd`):**
  - **The state key.** Each first-pass draw gets a 128-bit key: xxHash64's round on four lanes over the primitive, stride, FVF or program, render and stage states, pixel shader, the lights in use and the material, viewport and pillarbox, target, the textures' formats and kinds, anisotropy and the frame's settings generation. It is trusted as the texture cache trusts its signatures.
  - The first, word-at-a-time version cost 3.4% of the render thread's samples. Register lanes over 32-byte stripes brought that to 1.4%.
  - **A key the capturing frame before had** is drawn from that capture by `d3d8_DrawBakedAgain`:
    - its own vertices and indices are put in the rings as the UP draws put them;
    - its constants are set as an ordinary draw sets them (`vs_consts_up`, split out of `vs_bind`, then the capturing draw's ring step), so the ring holds the same bytes and is written as rarely. Writing all 192 every draw had cost +1.3% of samples;
    - its fixed-function matrices are given (the transform block made again; the lighting and pixel blocks copied into this frame's memory);
    - its textures are fetched as every pass does;
    - it is captured into this frame's entry for the later passes.
  - The bake memory alternates between two frames.
  - **A second table keys geometry:** the vertex-cache block (held by the table, so its address stays its bytes), counts, stride, primitive and the index bytes. A first-pass draw with the frame before's geometry is drawn from where the rings still hold it. With one table by state alone only 7% matched, because many draws share a state, each with its own mesh.
  - `d3d8_BakeReset` now uses a slowly falling peak of the frames' uploads (1/256 a frame). With little uploaded, the last frame alone had let a ring fill up mid-frame, about once in 10 s.
  - Pre-transformed draws and HUD draws moved to the screen's edges take the ordinary way. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_BAKE_AGAIN=0` turns it off.
  - cast_bug: 98% of first passes come from the frame before's capture, 99% of those with its geometry; a first-pass draw 1.52 -> 0.45 us. Avalanch and upstairs: 100% / 26% and 100% / 19%; their fixed-function vertices are copied each frame, not cached blocks.
  - Timing: render thread 4.71 -> 3.31, 4.31 -> 3.76, 2.87 -> 2.57; game thread 6.70 -> 6.57, 6.15 -> 6.06, 4.14 -> 4.11.
- **Cut 4 (`97de3ca`):**
  - `ig_build` groups a set's objects by key in a hash table, each group in draw order. `ig_set` keeps the grouping while the set's objects and stamp are unchanged, so this frame's grouping is the frame before's at the next frame.
  - `ic_kind_before` and the no-match path look it up. The leftovers are grouped by loose key the same way.
  - `ic_replaced` sorts (x, index) pairs. qsort makes the same comparisons on the same values, so the order is the same, ties included.
  - `ic_match_keys` takes the groupings. A key's matching reads and marks only its own objects, so key order is free, and inside a key both sides are in draw order as before.
  - Pairing 0.82 -> 0.56, 0.77 -> 0.54, 0.49 -> 0.36 ms a game frame; render thread 3.31 -> 3.05, 3.76 -> 3.54, 2.57 -> 2.41.
