### 18.99 Step 96: Sea Shell stuck at 60 / 60 with Match display. The cut test matched draws by which texture they used, and Sea Shell's water and caustics are flipbooks (a texture each frame): deep enough into the level under half the draws matched and every frame was shown as it is. The test now matches by the textures' shape (step 68's loose key), over the larger of the two frames' draws: Sea Shell 89-99.8% matched where the old key gave 35-50%. Not a latch: the interval line's "draws matched" averaged interpolated frames only and read 0.0% with none. Found on the way: a render-thread race that compared a frame with the next one half recorded (a 1-2% "match" every 5-10 s standing still), fixed. The overlay's Now line, the Detailed counter and the log now say when frames are shown as they are and why. Off and Game (60): identical.

Report: `step96_report.md` in the session scratch folder (`C--Users-User-Desktop-Desktop-Stuff-Claude-Stuff-Crash-TWOC-decomp\7e24d100-...\scratchpad`), runs in `sw/runs/` (the user's replay `user1`, checks `c1`-`c6`), identity in `id/`. Commits: `1538ab1` (items 1-2: the key and the fraction), `f692af6` (the race), `370c3b0` (item 3), plus the notes commit. No xboxrecomp change.

- **Task** (`twoc/reference/next_prompt.txt`, the user's playtest `twoc/reference/playtest/sea_shell_60_60.log`: Sea Shell, room 2 pad 2, Match display at 240 Hz, adaptive off, render thread on): 1. find the cause (the cut test, its match key, what a held frame is compared against) and say whether the hypothesis (texture identity in the key; a latch) is right; 2. the smallest safe fix (texture shape in the key; a held frame still updates the comparison), step 61's cut checks again; 3. the overlay's Now line and the Detailed counter to say when and why frames are held; 4. reproduce (Sea Shell, H2 Oh No, Coral) with sub-frame dumps of the water; 5. default run and Game (60) identical, commit.
- **The playtest log (verified):** interpolation never switched off. Sea Shell loaded at ~+285 s; "draws matched" 89.4% (+291) -> 78.1 -> 70.3 -> 60.0 -> 56.0 -> 52.2 (+341: 21 frames interpolated, 584 held for matches) and then 0 interpolated for minutes; "paired across a texture change" 25 -> 58 -> 109 -> 162 -> 183 -> 223 a frame, commands 283 -> 508. The brief returns (+431: 38 frames at 50.8%, +601-611) were the scene sitting at the 50% line, not the settings changes. Held frames were not reported any other way: the interval line's figure is the interpolated frames' mean (`d3d_bridge.c`), printed 0.0 with none.
- **Cause (item 1):** `ic_begin` counted a frame's draws whose kind (`ic_key(d, 0)`: program or FVF, pixel-shader states, **each texture's header**, target, primitive, counts) appeared in the frame before's sorted list, and held the frame under half. A flipbook's frames are textures of their own (18.71: H2 Oh No's caustics, `0x00C6F000`-`0x00C81000`), so every draw of the water and the caustic-lit floor and rocks was unmatched every frame. The hypothesis's first half is right. The latch is not: `ic_end` runs for held frames too (since step 61), so a held frame already became the frame before. The "0.0%" was the stats line.
  - Measured with `tools/diag/step96_match_diag.patch` (the match by the exact key, by shape, by shape without the target, without the counts, each judged frame): the user replayed Sea Shell to the same stretch with it (`user1`, the user's own play from a scratch exe copy; they asked to play it themselves): 84 of 129 sampled frames under half by the exact key (35.3-50%, mean 51.9%), by shape 89.2-99.8% (mean 99.0), 0 under half. Counts and targets don't matter (without them 98-100%). Unattended (`c1`-`c3`, `swim.py` through Sea Shell, `play.py` in H2 Oh No and Coral): exact-key means 72.6% / 82.8% / 75.5%, by shape 98.8 / 98.6 / 99.0.
- **The fix (`1538ab1`):**
  - `ic_key` makes both keys in one pass (the exact one for the objects, the loose one, textures by format, size, levels and cube, as step 68's object fallback); the cut test's list (`g_ikey`) takes the loose key, and the objects take it from there instead of a second `ic_key` call. The object keys and pairing are unchanged.
  - The fraction is of the larger of the two frames' draw counts. By shape alone, the first frame of the loading screen (12 draws, 6 alike, after the hub's 353) read exactly half and was drawn in between: the only frame decided differently from the old rule in 41,246 judged frames (`c1`-`c3`, once a run). Now a frame that lost or gained most of its draws is a cut.
  - The cut checks (step 61's, `c4`, the final build with the per-frame log; 14,295 frames: the intro 150 s, the hub, the load, Sea Shell's start, its pause): **no frame the old rule held is interpolated**; the intro's 7 camera steps and 14 turns, the level starts ("its camera is new"), the loads and the pause opening are held as before; 16 frames held for matches, every one a collapse of the draw count (logos, loads, fades, the pause opening 271 -> 6 draws), which the old rule had drawn in between (no visible difference with a still camera).
- **The race (`f692af6`), found in the replay:** every log with the render thread (the playtest's too) had frames shown as they are standing still with 1-2% matched, one every 5-10 s (`ss1` 16 over its run, `user1` 5). With the render thread, `replay_frame` freed the frame's slot (`replay_done` -> `slot_free`) before `ic_end` made its table set the frame before (`g_ip`). The game thread, waiting for that slot in `present_thread`, picks the set that is neither its handed-over frame's nor `g_ip` for its next frame: `g_ip` was still the frame before last's, so it took this frame's set, emptied it and recorded into it, and the next frame was compared with that (and could have been paired with it). `ic_end` now comes first in all three places (held frames: before `replay_run`). Since: 0 in ~55,000 judged frames (`c1`-`c4`). On one thread the handover follows the replay, so it never happened there (by the code; not run).
- **Item 3 (`370c3b0`):** at each counter sample (0.5 s) `meas_held` looks at the frames judged since: most shown as they are for matches -> "too few draws matched, N% of M" (their mean fraction and draws); for camera steps or turns -> "the camera cut (moved or turned too far in one frame)"; no frame judged (the overlay holding the game) keeps the last. The overlay's Frame rate line: "Now: 59.94 fps, each game frame shown as it is: too few draws matched, 42% of 480."; the Detailed counter: "Shown as it is: ..."; the log: `[D3DB] host interpolation: game frames shown as they are from guest swap N (...)` / `... interpolated again from guest swap N` at each change (200 at most). The interval line's held count now carries its frames' figure: `matches N (draws matched x% of y)` (`lgiv.py`'s pattern is ahead of it). Checked with the cut fraction raised to 0.995 (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_CUT=2,30,0.995`, `c5`, 2560x1440): the counter and the overlay as above with 99% of 264, the change lines in the log; as it is (`c6`): 60 / 240 and no held line.
- **The water, interpolating (sub-frame dumps, 3 swaps at a time; `interp_blendscan.py`, `interp_oddscan.py`, strips looked at):** Sea Shell 9 swaps (each sub-frame between its game frames, e.g. 1.10 / 0.74 / 0.38 of 1.51; odd pixels 4-4,667 of 921,600 with the camera moving), H2 Oh No 6 (odd 3-17), Coral 6 (odd 29-1,673; the 1,673 at a 0.72-degree turn, 4.58 / 5.76 / 4.60 of 10.13). No tearing or doubling: the caustics change once a game frame (the flipbook, at 60 Hz as before) and everything else moves in between.
- **Identity** (`sweep/id.sh B F3`, drive.py 150 s, bridge off and host-only, step 95's build against the final): 231 CdRom opens in order, 356 `[PATH]`, exit 3, no `[CRASH]`, the host device lines (the dump path aside), the first 8 host dumps byte-identical. That run is Game (60), which reaches none of the changed code (`ic_record` runs only while interpolating; the held lines need judged frames).
- **Not checked:** 120/144 Hz and the Legion Go; the user hasn't replayed with the final build (their replay was the fix without the race fix and the larger-count fraction); the cost of the second key on draws that aren't objects (a few hashes a draw, the objects' second `ic_key` call gone; not measured).
- **How it was run:** the first unattended runs were stopped on the user's request; they replayed Sea Shell themselves with a diagnostic exe copy in scratch (their ini copied beside it, their own ini and saves untouched), then allowed the automated checks. A long command line pasted into cmd.exe wrapped and ran in three pieces: give relative paths (from the scratch `root`: `..\D1\crash_twoc.exe 2> ..\runs\user1.err.log`) or a `.cmd` file.
- **The user's things:** `twoc\build\Release\crash_twoc.ini` untouched (copied once to the scratch test folder); every run from exe copies in scratch with its own root; no other games run; display settings untouched. The build folder's exe is the final build.
- **Files changed:**
  - `twoc/src/d3d_host.c` (`1538ab1`, `f692af6`), `twoc/src/{d3d_host.c,d3d_host.h,d3d_bridge.c,overlay.cpp,overlay.h}` (`370c3b0`);
  - `tools/diag/{step96_match_diag.patch,README.md}`, `tools/diag/sweep/{v96.sh,swim.py,cutsum.py,README.md}` (notes commit);
  - the 18.64 and 18.82 pointers, this entry and "Start here tomorrow".

### 18.102 Step 99: the display's card from the display driver, where the threads run, a [MEM] line, the robustness sweep and leak hunt (one leak fixed: the frame slots' arenas), Render thread Auto the default, both packages rebuilt. **No crash, hang or broken picture anywhere; 59.9-60.0 updates a second in every level at 240 Hz Match display with the render thread at native 4K.**

- **Part 1 (`69fbfe5`): the display's card.** gpu.c takes the window's monitor (`GetMonitorInfoW` `szDevice`) through gdi32's `D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromGdiDisplayName` to its adapter LUID and matches that in the adapter list. DXGI's `EnumOutputs` is used only when D3DKMT has no answer, with a `[GPU]` line when they disagree. About and `[GPU]` say `<card> (Windows copies each frame)` when it isn't the rendering card. Tested: the normal case (RTX 4080, "the same card"); the reverse (the Radeon iGPU chosen, through Restart now in the package stand-in: "NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4080 SUPER (Windows copies each frame)"). **Not testable here:** the Optimus direction (display on the Intel), the Nitro 5's case.
- **Part 2 (`9a93ed3`): where the threads run.** The game thread at each guest swap and the render thread at each frame it takes call `GetCurrentProcessorNumberEx` and count it, mapped through the CPU sets' `EfficiencyClass`. A `[D3DB] host cores:` line each 10 s gives each thread's P / E shares and its two most used processors. On a hybrid CPU only, it also gives each kind's average effective MHz (PDH `Processor Information(*)` per instance, on the power log's thread, +0.38 ms a second there). This Ryzen 7 7800X3D: "one kind of core". `RECOMP_DEV_CPU_CLASSES=N` (a test aid) simulates a hybrid split. Logging only, nothing pinned.
- **Part 3 (`53fab9e`, xboxrecomp `ee60b0e` local: `d3d8_MemStats`): the `[MEM]` line,** one sample every 10 s on the power log's thread (that thread 2.06 -> 2.18 ms a second, nothing per frame). Fields:
  - The process: private / working set / commit, handles, threads, GDI, USER.
  - The rendering card: `QueryVideoMemoryInfo` local and non-local, use and budget.
  - Caches: the vertex cache (MB, 96 MB cap, times emptied), the texture table (of 1024), the frame slots' arenas (MB, chunks), command arrays, texture copy lists, interpolation tables, baked draws and drawn-again tables, xbox_d3d8's bake and the rings' peaks and restarts, render targets, AO textures.
  - The guest heap, contiguous arena, pool and VM zone.
  - Where it goes: the power log's file too; Collect logs' summary has the first and last lines. `sweep/memfit.py` fits slopes per hour.
  - **What empties what:** the vertex cache replaces the least recently used way of its set and empties entirely at 96 MB, not at a level change (the old level's entries age out). The texture table replaces the least recently used entry when its probe run is full (1024 entries). The arenas are a pool shared by the frame slots, kept and reused, never freed.
- **Part 4, pass A** (Match display at 240 Hz, render thread Auto, baking and vertex cache on, 16:9, native 4K, HUD at the edges, adaptive off, fullscreen, THREADCHECK):
  - Coverage: the 25 main levels, five bosses, room 6, the pause pages, game over and Continue, both endings.
  - Health: 0 `[CRASH]` / watchdog / THREADCHECK / frame-slot lines.
  - Rates: clean minimum 59.9-60.0 updates, presents 238.8-240, shown one refresh apart 99.5-100%.
  - Thread times: game side 0.9-6.6 ms (cast_bug 6.6, avalanch 5.9), replay side 0.6-4.2. Private 681-869 MB.
  - Against step 73 (cast_bug 37-42 fps, avalanch 50-56 at 1280x720): both 60.0 now.

Crash's object is `[0x17B0DC]` (position `+0x6C`; the same as `0x8F5120` in the levels tried). Writing his position moves him and the camera; he doesn't die below the floor (hangs there) but over water he falls and dies. In Medieval Madness the ball overwrites his position (find the ball: `findpos.py`).
- **Item 4 (fixed, `6274760`).**
  - Static: `sub_000B0E10` draws the flying-pickup list (`0x4670C0`, 32 entries of 0x40: type at `+0x3C`; added by `sub_000B0890(x, y, type, ...)`; `sub_000B0A90` moves them): types 2 / 5 an extra life's head (to x 0.33 then to the lives head at 0.49, `0x1A41A4` / `0x1A417C`), 3 a bonus round's head (to 0.575, then 0.66, the bonus lives), 0 / 1 a bonus round's fruit from its counter (-0.8, -0.8: `0x1A4188` / `0x1A41A0`) to the level's (-0.78, 0.77). The panel calls it at `0xB462D`, its last call before the panel camera: the return address `0xB4632` was just outside `PANEL_HUD_FROM..TO` (`0xB2568`-`0xB462D`, d3d_bridge.c), so its draws weren't the HUD's and kept their 4:3 places (the user's photo: fruit at x -0.81 of 4:3 = the bonus counter's unshifted place; the head short of the counter). The table's `0xB3354` / `0xB33B7` are the ordinary fruit flights (`0x560840`, `0x562F60` lists), which were right.
  - The fix: `PANEL_HUD_TO` 0xB4633 and `{ 0xB4632, HUD_FLY }`: moved by where it is on the ramp, so at the end of a flight as its counter, at any margin.
  - Checked (Arctic Antics, 16:9, edges, Match display): the wumpa counter's word `0x4678C8` poked to 100 makes the game spawn a type-5 head (`0xB47C5`: count >= 100 -> -100 and `sub_000B0890`); grabs every 0.1 s (`sweep/xl.sh`): before, the head stopped at ~0.65 of the width with the counter at 0.82; after, it pauses left of the counter (the 0.33 point is on the ramp: further left than its 4:3 offset) and lands on it, margin 0 and 5%. The bonus round wasn't reached unattended (the same site by the code).
- **Item 5 (found; partly fixed, `f79d0db`).**
  - Gold Rush's late point (fruit 16, 254.84 4.28 265.13), Crash glided ±2 along z (`tx.sh` / `t5b.sh`, sub-frame dumps of 30 swaps): a brick decal on the wall ahead vanishes in some in-between sub-frames and is back on every recorded one (`patch.py`: contrast with the wall ~10.6 on all 30 recorded frames, 4 or less in sub-frames 1-3 of ~1 swap in 3): a 60 Hz flicker at 240 Hz. Standing still: never. Baking off: the same. At the start of the level: none seen. Game (60): no sub-frames (the draw-log's dumps there were taken with the glide running in real time, so not comparable).
  - Cause (`INTERP_REC`, `recls.py`; the draw log): the walls are fixed-function draws (class `IC_FF_PROJ`: corrected as view x `vfix` and projection + `dp`), the decals 4-vertex program draws (`IC_VP`: WVP x C, C = P^-1 vfix Pi), every draw LESSEQUAL with no bias. In the game's own frames the decal and its wall reach bit-identical depths (xbox_d3d8 composes `(W V) P` in float as the game composed the decal's WVP), so the decal wins; the two correction paths round differently, and the error grows with the coordinates (worse later in a level). Not the precision of the game's maths.
  - The part fixed: `g_isub`'s c, vfix and dp were floats (each corrected matrix rounded twice); now double: sub-frames with the decal gone 20 of 30 swaps -> 9 (the build before, rerun: 20).
  - The rest (not done): only identical arithmetic on both paths removes it: the camera correction applied in clip space after the transform (oPos x C) in both of xbox_d3d8's shader paths, the pre-correction clip bit-identical to the game frame's for every draw; it touches xbox_d3d8's generated shaders, a constant block and the baked draws' capture (a local xboxrecomp change, the 18.32 rule). A depth bias for decal-like draws in sub-frames would hide it more cheaply, with its own risks.
  - Not reached: Smokey and the Bandicoot (a race: needs throttle), Medieval Madness's late point (the ball overwrote the teleport). Whether the user's "ground shakes" is the same thing is open (kit 5 / 5b).
- **Item 1 (not reproduced; kit).** Five runs in Drain Damage at the playtest's settings: standing 150 s (the boss's rings never reach the start platform), walking and jumping forward (an invisible wall at z 27.5), teleported over the water four times (deaths, respawns: Crash visible every time). The boss's own kill wasn't reached. The playtest log: water_b loaded at +1598 s, won at +1721 s; the 50 s with 0 interpolated objects and ~130 overlay draws (+1641-1691) were the pause menu (Start at ~+1621, no world camera, pause stills), not the invisible life, which the log can't place.

The title animation finishes, the main menu opens (129 s in since 18.29; banner `[GAME] MAIN MENU OPEN`), and a real controller drives it into the next level. **The 18.26 level-load deadlock is fixed (18.27, `6f51e4b`):** `NtSuspendThread`/`NtResumeThread` are cooperative, so a suspended thread parks only at a kernel-call boundary and never holds a host lock. **The 18.32 hub-load deadlock is fixed too (18.33, `800f27b`):** a suspended thread also parks only once it owns no guest critical section. **The intro-load heap exhaustion is fixed (18.28, `145f8ef`):** a hand-written `NtFreeVirtualMemory` returns released blocks to the guest heap. On, the replay side runs on a thread of its own (`twoc render thread`): the game thread records each frame and hands it over at the guest's Swap, at most one frame ahead (it waits for the thread to take the frame before and to free its next slot); the thread applies the host side's part of the settings each frame was recorded under, then uploads, pairs, replays and presents it (each present held to mid-refresh), and runs the overlay, whose hold keeps the game waiting in its Swap. cast_bug and avalanch hold 240 at native 4K and adaptive never steps down (one thread: 60 and 120); the game thread ~12.2 ms a game frame there, the render thread ~7.5-7.8. `RECOMP_RENDER_THREAD=sync` makes the game wait for each frame (REPLAY_CHECK and the sub-frame check: 0 differ); a deadlock watchdog logs where each thread is after 5 s stuck (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_RT_WATCHDOG`); THREADCHECK knows the thread. Frame rate: Game, the bridge off and Off: identical. **Since 18.81: the render thread's groundwork (stage 1 of 18.78's plan A), on one thread, identical output.** A recorded frame is a slot the game side hands to the replay side at the Swap (two slots sharing one pool of memory; three interpolation table sets); textures are recorded as copies of the guest bytes and made by the replay side before the first pass, from a game-side table of what the host holds; vertex programs are parsed on the game side and created on the replay side; live settings are queued and applied at each side's frame boundary; step 77's plan, the adaptive ladder and the presents are one replay-side unit (busy split into the game side's and the replay side's); every D3D11 call site is tagged by side and `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_THREADCHECK=1` ends the process (exit 120) when one runs on the wrong side. REPLAY_CHECK and the new sub-frame check (`step78_subframe_check.patch`): 0 differ; identity holds; cost +0.18 ms a game frame at cast_bug's start, +0.01 gliding. The sub-frame trigger file takes `set KEY VALUE` lines (live settings, `sweep/st.py`). **Since 18.80: frame-rate targets.** Esc, Display, "Frame rate": Game (60) (the default), Match display, 90, 120, 144, 165, 180 or 240 fps, live, saved as `framerate = 60 | display | N` (`RECOMP_FRAMERATE`). Each frame shown is drawn at its own moment between game frames (game frames on a clock of refreshes, 2.4 of them at 144 Hz), so any display faster than 59.94 Hz interpolates evenly; the game still ticks 59.94 a second. A target the display shows evenly (its refresh or a whole fraction) runs on its vsync; another needs variable refresh (the EDID's range, NVIDIA's driver, then 120 frames measured) or falls back to the highest even rate below (90 on 240 Hz: 80); the overlay says which and why. Adaptive: down to whatever lower rate fits (240/120/80/60, 180/90/60, 144/72/60). This desktop's monitor has no 144 mode and NVIDIA reports variable refresh off; a 144 Hz display under vsync (the Legion Go) is still unmeasured. Off: identical. **Since 18.77: fewer sub-frames where frames are heavy, and save times in local time.** With Frame rate: Display each game frame gets 4, 2 or 1 sub-frames: when game frames run late (30 frames 3% over, the work the cause) it drops to the next count, and after 2 s or more with room for the next count it goes back up (a rise that fails doubles the wait, to 32 s); each change is logged and the overlay's Frame rate line shows "2 of 4 frames each game frame". cast_bug plays at 1 and avalanch at 2 (sometimes 1) at 60 fps; light levels stay at 4. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_ADAPT=0` keeps 4 (a test switch). The Load Game list's save times are the PC's local time (XAPI reads Windows' time zone and daylight saving through `XC_MAX_OS`); saves made before keep the UTC time they were stored with. On a 2, 3 or 4 x 59.94 Hz display paced by its vsync each game frame is drawn k times (4 at 239.98 Hz) at interval 1 inside the game's Swap, the camera moved partway from the frame before; the game still runs 59.94 updates a second; cuts are shown as they are. **Since 18.65 objects move too:** draws are matched with the frame before's by kind and guest mesh, and a matched object's world and its bones are interpolated (over 1.5 units it snaps; `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_INTERP_OBJECTS=0` is camera only), so Crash runs smoothly under the smooth camera; particles, animated textures and H2 Oh No's underwater layer still move at 60 Hz. Off: identical. **Since 18.66** a mirror image (the hub floor's Crash) is never paired with its model, menu letters that change colour or line show the change at once instead of sliding, auto pacing reads the monitor's mode rate at the start (not DWM's, which follows what presents on a variable-refresh display), a vsync stall gives the limiter 10 s instead of the rest of the run, and `[D3DB] host interpolation off, one frame each game frame: <why>` says why when the setting is on but can't run (the overlay's help line too). **Since 18.63: record and replay** (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_REPLAY`, a switch for the frame-interpolation stages; off by default) and a host draw call ~5x cheaper. **Since 18.61: anti-aliasing.** Esc, Display, "Anti-aliasing": Off (the default), FXAA, SMAA, MSAA 2x, 4x or 8x, live, saved as `antialiasing` (`RECOMP_ANTIALIASING`). FXAA and SMAA (`twoc/src/aa.c`; SMAA vendored in `twoc/third_party/smaa`) filter the finished frame at the present, HUD and text included (a little softer). MSAA draws the back buffer and the screen-sized render targets multisampled (`d3d_host.c` makes the surfaces itself: xbox_d3d8 views multisampled textures as TEXTURE2D, which D3D11 refuses; xbox_d3d8 unchanged) and resolves them before every CopyRects, every draw sampling a target, every dump and the present. All modes hold 60 fps at native 4K here. Off: identical. **Since 18.60: controls, keyboard play, and a menu that can leave the game running.** The Esc overlay has three tabs (Display, Audio, Controls). "Pause the game while this menu is open" (`pause`, on by default) turned off keeps the game running and drawing behind the menu with its sound on, so a change shows live, while the game gets an idle pad until the menu closes (a button still held then waits for its release). Controls: each Xbox input (A, B, X, Y, Black, White, triggers, Start, Back, sticks, stick clicks, d-pad) with the PC controller's input that presses it; click, press the new button, swapped with whatever had it; reset to defaults; the default is the old mapping exactly and isn't applied at all. The keyboard plays too (`keyboard`, on by default; the bridge window's own key messages, so only while it has the focus): W A S D or the arrows move at full stick, Space/K jump, J/E spin, L/Left Shift crouch, slide and body slam, Enter/P pause, every Xbox input rebindable to two keys the same way; with the keyboard on, port 0 counts as connected, so no controller is needed. Saved as `pad_*` and `key_*` where they differ from the defaults (`twoc/src/controls.c`). **Since 18.59: 3D sounds are placed left-right.** The VP's HRTF filter is on by default (Esc, Audio, "3D sound placement (HRTF)", live; `hrtf = 0 | 1`, `RECOMP_HRTF`): an enemy or crate to one side is heard on that side, in stereo and 5.1, and the music/effects balance is as before. Off: the old mix, every 3D sound centred. The user's xemu centres them with its HRTF on or off (its mix sends the left ear to every submix). **Since 18.58: music and effects volume, and 5.1.** The overlay's Audio section has Music (stream voices: music, ambience, speech) and Effects (buffer voices) sliders, live, saved as `music_volume`/`effects_volume`, and a Speakers choice (`speakers = stereo | 5.1`, from the next start): 5.1 opens six channels when the default device has the speakers and tells the game it has surround speakers (`XC_AUDIO`), so 3D effects pan front to rear; the user's default device (Razer Nommo V2, Dolby Atmos) takes stereo only, so it falls back there and the overlay says so. Left-right placement of 3D sounds: since 18.59. Unset: as before. **Since 18.57: fullscreen and a settings overlay.** `run_twoc.ps1 -HostOnly -Fullscreen` starts borderless over the window's monitor (the picture letterboxed); Alt+Enter switches at any time. Esc on the game window opens a Dear ImGui settings overlay (`twoc/src/overlay.cpp`, `twoc/third_party/imgui`) that holds the game (no update, draw or pad read; its audio session muted) until Esc again, and applies fullscreen, widescreen, render size, frame pacing (auto, vsync, limiter, limiter+vsync), anisotropic filtering and the game's volume at once. They are saved to `twoc\build\Release\crash_twoc.ini`, read at the next start into the `RECOMP_*` switches the launcher left unset (run_twoc.ps1 switches win; `-Widescreen:$false` etc. to switch off). The controller is gated around the overlay (`overlay_gate` in the XPP poll). No file and no Esc: as before. Music and effects apart: since 18.58. **Since 18.56: 16:9 and a render size.** `run_twoc.ps1 -HostOnly -Widescreen` plays in 16:9 (`RECOMP_WIDESCREEN=1`: a `sub_000956B0` wrapper widens every screen projection and its culling; the host renders 854x480, full-width 2D stretched, other 2D pillarboxed; the HUD and menus are 3D and stay 4:3 in the middle), and `-RenderSize 1920x1080` or `native` (`RECOMP_RENDER_SIZE`) renders at that size in a window in real pixels, with or without widescreen; screen copies and 640x480 targets follow the host's size. Both off: identical. **Since 18.55:** pausing no longer crashes in DSOUND's interrupt path (a handled APU trap was delivered twice), the pause still shows the level in every level (render targets are released with their memory), and shiny materials draw (Bamboozled's ball rings, the pause screen's crystal and gems: the host texture cache no longer unbinds stages mid-draw). **Since 18.52:** Aku Aku and the "!" box no longer show holes while Crash idles (0 x NaN in the morph program), the water's reflection pass draws its cube map, Wa-Wa shows in Drain Damage, and Crash's stencil shadow darkens the ground as in xemu (an unbound texture stage reads opaque black, `2a3960a`); `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_DRAWLOG_TRIGGER=FILE` logs the next N swaps of a scene reached by hand (`tools/diag/drawlog.py`). **Since 18.51 levels render like xemu's:** the fog is real (it was a constant ~84% fog-colour wash on the terrain: the "flat shading"), pixel shaders without a final combiner get D3D's default one (fog, V1 + R0 with specular), DrawVertices is mirrored (box-break shards) and program point sprites are drawn as quads (the snow). **Since 18.50 animations are right:** the lifter's `frndint` rounds by the x87 control word (`twoc/tools/lift_frndint.py`, run by `lift.ps1`), so the game's `floor()` floors and the animation key lookup `sub_00090BE0` no longer picks the wrong key or block (Crash's idle-pose snaps, the enemies, the floating hub pads). **Since 18.45 the game is heard:** the APU interrupt reaches DSOUND (`twoc` thread + DSOUND's IRQL made a lock), the title music, logo and intro voices, hub ambience and hub music play at their own pitch and match the source files (`tools/diag/srcmatch.py`/`timeline.py` on a `loopcap.exe` capture). Frame rate: `twoc/tools/diag/fpsrun.py` + `fps.py` read the game's frame counter `0x25A660` from outside the process. **Option B: S1, S2 and S3 done (18.32-18.34):** 24 D3D wrappers in `recomp_manual.c` (`sub_X` → lifted `sub_X_gen`) feed `twoc/src/d3d_bridge.c` (guest side) and `twoc/src/d3d_host.c` (host side). All of it is inert unless switched on. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE=1` opens a second window, "Crash TWOC - D3D bridge (host)", with an `xbox_d3d8` device. It mirrors the game's back-buffer clears and, since 18.34, its DrawVerticesUP draws (state translated from XDK to PC values, textures cached and uploaded): **the splash's Universal logo fades in and out on that window.** Since 18.35 (S4 part 1) it also mirrors render-to-texture, pixel shaders (re-encoded for `xbox_d3d8`'s combiners) and P8 textures on those UP draws. **Since 18.36 it mirrors fixed-function indexed draws (FVF `0x152`) with their transforms, material and lights: the LOADING text, the title animation, the menu and name entry, and the hub's world show on that window.** **Since 18.37 it also mirrors immediate mode (Begin/SetVertexData/End: the menu's particles, the hub's portal glows) and CopyRects (back buffer to texture, the hub).** **Since 18.38 (S5 part 1) it also mirrors vertex programs** (UP and indexed; xbox_d3d8's translator fixed locally, `a0b306d`): **Crash's model and the menu's logo prop show on that window.** **Since 18.39 (S5 part 2) cube maps upload, and program draws that sample the hub's CopyRects copy are mirrored with their texel coordinates scaled; no draw is skipped on any path from splash to hub.** `run_twoc.ps1 -Bridge` runs the mirror (18.39). **Since 18.40 (S7 part 1), `run_twoc.ps1 -HostOnly` runs the mirror alone with the executor off, at 60 fps (a 59.94 Hz limiter at the host present), splash to hub; the heap is flat over repeated level loads (worker TIBs are freed and the runtime heap is guarded across threads).** **Since 18.42, with WOMBAT, every warp room is open and 23 levels have loaded; the heap has a pool arena and a VM zone (bump 84.1%, at least 10 MB contiguous free at every load), and voice stops no longer stall the game.** Every path is counted on a `[D3DB] host draws` line. With `-DumpDir` it writes `hostNNN.bmp` next to the executor's `shotNNN.bmp`. `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_TINT=1` clears to the frame index as a check, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_POINT=1` point-samples as the executor does, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_DUMP_EVERY=N` adds host dumps, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_SKIPTEX=1` (18.36) writes the textures of the draws the mirror skips, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_DRAWLOG=A-B` (18.37, `drive.py --drawlog`) logs every draw of guest swaps A-B with its screen box and dumps each of those swaps, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_VSDUMP`/`_VSPROBE` (18.38, `--vsdump`/`--vsprobe`, checked by `tools/diag/vscheck.py`) dump each vertex program and probe where the host draws its vertices, `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_VP_COPY=1` also mirrors the copy-sampling program draws, and `RECOMP_D3D_CENSUS=1` is 18.32's census. `twoc/tools/diag/drive.py` runs splash → hub unattended (`--bridge`, `--tint`, `--point`, `--census`, `--dump=DIR`, `--dump-every=N`), `census.py` summarises a census, `fbcompare.py` compares host dumps with executor dumps, and `splashcheck.py` is S3's per-frame splash check. To regenerate: `twoc\analyze.ps1` (disasm with the seeds), then `twoc\lift.ps1` (it now also runs `twoc\tools\wrap_callers.py`, without which callers bypass the wrappers), then `cmake --build twoc\build --config Release --parallel`.

**Resolved (18.25): the `*(0x427A58)` "3D listener" gap.** It was never a listener: it is the sound manager's `IDirectSound*`, null only because DirectSoundCreate failed with DSERR_NODRIVER (no AC'97 codec reported). With `RECOMP_AC97_READY` it is populated and the 18.13 guard is inert. Remaining audio limits: no DSP, so the effects image (reverb) does not run; the GP handshake is acknowledged, not executed. *(18.74: scoped. Crash's only effect is the I3DL2 reverb, five presets by level and stretch of level, fed by every 3D voice; the image's XTalk and the game's HEADPHONES option are unmodelled too. Recommended: run the image on the MIT dsp56300 emulator, starting offline.)* *(18.75: stage 0 done: the real program's reverb is 24-41 dB under the mix, inaudible in play; XTalk is the identity; stage 1 not recommended. Our DSOUND also writes the late lines off, because it reads the state back from the empty GP window.)*

**Also known, accepted gap (bypassed for gamepads by 18.23's hand-written open/close, and for gamepad hot-plug by 18.73's hand-written XGetDeviceChanges; still true for any other USB device).** The real XPP arming path is gated behind real OHCI hardware at *two* levels, both confirmed by exhaustive full-binary xref checks (no write anywhere in the binary, same method used for the DSOUND listener gap): (1) `[0x1534B0]`/`[0x153434]`, the root-hub-status-change interrupt gate (ISR `sub_001596F4`, traced in 18.16), and (2) the host-controller-context table at `0x153404`/`0x153408`/`0x15340C` that `sub_001547DA` looks a port up in before `sub_000EB5A0` can arm a slot (traced in 18.17, confirmed with a real controller connected — `xbox_InputIsConnected(0)=1` but `sub_000EB5A0(0)` still returns `slot[0]=0`). Both are the same underlying gap: this recomp's USB/host-controller emulation (14.4/15.1) doesn't walk far enough to populate either. 18.16's fix (a gated one-shot call into the game's own real insertion handler) is correct and reachable but cannot get past gap (2) on its own; building real OHCI host-controller-registration and root-hub-status emulation would be the faithful fix for both, not yet attempted. **18.18 scoped a lighter-weight alternative (fabricate a fake context object to satisfy gap (2)'s lookup) and found it isn't lighter-weight at all**: a match falls straight into a real two-phase blocking USB SETUP-packet transfer (`sub_001554A1`) needing 2-3 fabricated sub-structs, two more untraced callees, and a `KeWaitForSingleObject` wait this recomp can't ever complete (fails fast, doesn't hang, but still fails) — realistically the same size/category as option 1, not a quick data fix.

**Also known, now well-scoped — and confirmed to need a full swap, not incremental bridging.** *(Corrected by 18.31: the all-or-nothing constraint only applies to replacing the native device. A mirror bridge that calls the lifted body first and never writes guest memory is incremental. See item 1 of "Next step".)* The NV2A executor (`nv2a_pgraph_d3d11.c`, driven by `RECOMP_PB_EXEC=1`) is a hard-coded 2D-only renderer built for a different title's menus (Burnout 3 — hard-coded texture names, a fixed 5-dword pre-transformed vertex layout, `NV097_SET_TRANSFORM_PROGRAM`/`_CONSTANT` and vertex-array-format uploads all silently ignored) — this is why 91% of triangles are "fully off-surface" (18.14): real 3D vertices get reinterpreted as already-in-screen-space pixels. **18.19 found the toolkit already has a full, working, tested fix for this sitting unused**: `xbox_d3d8`'s `d3d8_vsh.c` is a complete, already-wired-up NV2A vertex-microcode-to-HLSL translator (validated by `tests/d3d8_smoke`), reachable only via 15.1's *original*, not-yet-taken plan (replace the game's ~91-99 D3D calls at their API entry points, rather than running over the push-buffer executor). That route turns out cheaper than 15.1 feared — Crash's D3D library is a normal, cleanly-bounded library, not the harder LTCG case `d3d8ltcg-device-context.md` warns about — and would fix the vertex-program gap as a side effect, with no new toolkit code. **18.20 tried the incremental version of this plan (bridge `CreateDevice`, leave the other ~90 entry points on the old push-buffer path) and confirmed by direct observation that it cannot work**: `CreateDevice` succeeded (real `xbox_d3d8` device, vertex-shader translator initialized), but the very next unbridged D3D call crashed immediately, because a host-shaped `xbox_d3d8` device object and the native, push-buffer-shaped object every other D3D function still expects cannot coexist at the same address, and unbridged code dereferences raw offsets directly rather than through an accessor a handle layer could intercept. **Option B is therefore an all-or-nothing swap**: `CreateDevice` plus the entire wrapper cluster (all ~91-99 entry points) have to be bridged together in one batch before any of it can be tested, not incrementally a few functions at a time. **18.21 then did the scoping pass this implies, over all 99 entries via the disassembler's own full xref database rather than by hand**: they split cleanly into 4 cost tiers -- CreateDevice itself (done once, 18.20); 4 shared-plumbing entries covering 33% of call sites that gate one object/refcount-model design decision; 36 confirmed (+48 probable) simple single-push-buffer-opcode writers that become ~1 line of C each once an NV097 method-number table is built (extending the 21 constants `nv2a_pgraph_d3d11.c` already has); and 10 large entries (30-85-function closures each, CreateDevice-class effort) that are the real cost driver despite being only 7% of call sites. Real-size estimate: roughly **10-15x what CreateDevice alone took in 18.20**, concentrated in those 10 functions and the object-model decision, not spread evenly across all 99.

**Next step.**
0. **S7 (18.40): part 1 done** (`1ee8de2`, `44d29e9`). `run_twoc.ps1 -HostOnly` = `-Bridge` with the executor off. Menu and hub 60.0/s; menu idle timeout 30 s, as the game counts it (1800 updates); 14 level loads with the heap flat at 39,773 KiB (`cycle.py`); natural demo ends return to the menu; bridge off identical.
   - ~~The first attract demo crashes on the normal title path~~ **fixed in 18.41 (`3648de8`)**: our hand-written `sub_000EB270` never popped its return address, and the stack leak handed `sub_00087BF0` a garbage level-pool size.
   - **Playable (18.41):** host-only, the hub's earth warp room (the only open wing) leads to Tornado Alley (pad 2) and Arctic Antics (pad 1). Both load and play at 60 fps; pause → WARP ROOM returns to the hub. The spawn is the Load/Save pad (it opens the LOAD/SAVE menu; EXIT with d-pad down + A).
   - **Every area (18.42):** start a new game named WOMBAT (25/25 crystals, 106%). The warp-room table `0x19A7F0` and the pad paths `0x19C118` give every pad's level and position; scratch `visit.py`/`newgame.py` (step 40) drive them. 23 of the 25 main levels have been loaded host-only.
   - ~~Heap: 96% of the bump range~~ **fixed in 18.42 (`e05685c`)**: it was no-split slack (16.5 MB held by small DSOUND pool blocks), not level data. A pool arena and a VM zone keep bump used at 84.1% with ≥ 10 MB contiguous free at every load; no level needs more than the heap has.
   - ~~The voice-off waits~~ **fixed in 18.42 (`33feacc`)**: `sub_0013ADB8` is wrapped to deliver what the APU's idle-voice interrupt would. 0 five-second force-offs over 12 levels; the hub and level changes no longer stall.
   - **Long sessions (18.42):** the runtime's kernel-call counter wrapped past 2³¹ after ~30 min and then logged every call (~7 fps). Saturated in the `xboxrecomp/` clone (`fe75987`, local), and the voice-off fix removed the spins that got it there.
   - **The bosses (18.43, `edfa555`):** Drain Damage and the final boss no longer end early. A never-taken `je` in the boss-script interpreter made its "hit" condition always fire; `twoc/tools/lift_flag_join.py` (run by `lift.ps1`) fixes that class of join. Re-lift with `twoc\lift.ps1` after any lifter change.
   - **Step 42 (18.44):** the mixed-setter flag joins are lifted (`264ae78`, 14 branches; none ran in the hub or two levels). The flying pads are **game side**: `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_MOTIONLOG` (`85d3114`, read by `tools/diag/motionlog.py`) shows the game submits the pieces' transforms in the air (program `F1CEC538`, 77 vertices), and the mirror draws exactly that. Audio step 0 is in (`a591bb2` + `xboxrecomp` `853b2ee`), still silent: the APU monitor discards the VP's output.
   - **Step 43 (18.45): audio step 1 done.** Monitor (`xboxrecomp` `31b7165`), VP resampler and starved-voice hang (`4b439c7`), the APU interrupt and DSOUND's IRQL lock (`a8bd0c6`, `063f04a`): host-only, splash to hub, sound every second, matching `ThemeL.wav`, `LOGOS.wav`, `INTRO1.wav`, `HUB_BG.wav` and (under the ambience) the hub's `ThemeL.wav`. Present pacing locked to the display (`03d755b`). Side finding: the timer thread's stack is inside the game's 3 MB pool at `0x5EB840` (worker slices at `0x780000`).
   - **Step 43 follow-up (18.46):** a playtest felt slower; not reproduced. HEAD and the step 42 build measure the same in the hub, the intro, loads and Arctic Antics with the window visible (`tools/diag/perfmon.py`/`perfsum.py`). The vsync wait is bounded (`dd841bc`), `[D3DB] host swap gaps` counts stutter in the log, `RECOMP_APU_IRQ=0` switches the interrupt off. Next playtest: send the log (or two, with and without `$env:RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_HZ = '59.94'` / `$env:RECOMP_APU_IRQ = '0'`), and say where it stutters.
   - **Step 44 (18.47): the playtest stutter is fixed.** A 40-110 ms hitch every 2 s and a 140-146 ms one every 10 s (the user's CapFrameX): `run_twoc.ps1`'s `Start-Process` redirection opens the logs write-through, and unbuffered stderr went out a byte per WriteFile, so the periodic stat lines cost 60-140 ms. `1c81230` clears the write-through at startup (a `[LOG]` line says so) and writes each periodic block in one call; `xboxrecomp` `d8ce164` does the same for the `[KERNEL] summary`. Menu and Arctic Antics (attract demo) through `run_twoc.ps1` in Windows Terminal: p99 30 → 17.7 ms, 0 gaps over 50 ms; the boot's first swap at 1.0 s (was 12.7 s). Next: the user's CapFrameX capture to confirm; then the 0.8 s / 1.4 s level-load freezes.
   - **Step 45 (18.48): motion jitter and jittery music.** Game ticks are 1 per frame (6,211 of 6,211). The presents sat on the vblank edge, and DWM showed some 5 then 3 refreshes apart: `d2ef367` presents mid-refresh (frames off the grid 5.5-7.3% → 2.2%, 0-2% in levels; the menu's 10 Hz remainder is DWM's own), with a `[D3DB] host frames shown this interval` line. The music: the APU frame thread skipped the voice processor on every front-end trap (13-23% of level frames silent); `xboxrecomp` `1551271` renders through traps (level dropouts ~930 → ~90 a minute, the music continuous). *(`1551271` approved in 18.49.)* Next: the user's CapFrameX A/B (`RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE_PRESENT_PHASE=0` for the old timing), the mix balance, the front end's halted spells.
   - **Step 46 (18.49): worker stacks, the mix, the clicks.** The worker stack slices moved above the image (`ed9fe5a`); nothing live sat under the old one, and the floating pads are unchanged (game side). The mix follows the real GP, measured against xemu's own run of the same attract demo: speaker bins at unity, the 3D (HRTF submix) bins at -11 dB, the sends dropped (`0a6f40b`); music minus rest -11.7 → -3.3 dB (xemu -2.0). No frames while the front end is halted (`13da2d0`) and the last sample of a segment or loop played (`2c4e0dc`): demo dropouts 13 → 0, clicks 81 → 6; hub sliding 3 → 0 and 49 → 27 (footstep onsets). The new CapFrameX capture still has 20 pairs of 20.8/12.5 ms in 60 s (presents even; DWM's). **Loopback here goes through a compressor: compare ratios, not levels** (`musicfit.py`). Next: a playtest of the mix and the clicks (idle poses; bouncy boxes with the music off), then reverb (needs the DSP) or the S5 items. *(Playtest done: mix and clicks fine, all three APU changes confirmed; the idle poses were step 47.)*
   - **Step 47 (18.50): the model and pad glitches are fixed (`edcf64b`).** `frndint` was lifted as `rint()` (always nearest) and ignored the control word, so the CRT's `floor()` rounded to nearest. The shared animation key lookup `sub_00090BE0` (block = floor((t − 1)/32), key = floor(local) − 1) then picked the next block in the second half of every 32-frame block (local time negative, key byte 255: the curves read garbage) and a key mask one key ahead at any fraction ≥ 0.5. Before: wrong keys on 91% of the demo's character-animation frames, garbage on 18%; after: 0 of 360k lookups. The hub grabs show no floating pads or numbers. `step47_animtap.patch` has a hardware write watchpoint tap (`TMP_WATCH`) for "who writes this dword". Next: a playtest (Crash, Coco, enemies, pads, the Death Tornado Spin disappearance); `padfloat.py` doesn't measure the pads (18.50). *(Playtest done: every animation glitch is gone.)*
   - **Step 48 (18.51): rendering gaps, part 1.** The flat look was the fog: `xbox_d3d8`'s combiner shader used the fog colour's alpha as the fog factor (~84% fog colour on the terrain), and its fixed-function shader never fogged (depth sign). Fixed locally (`b6f1a2b`), with the combiner output clamp (`d0f76c4`) and D3D's default final combiner in the bridge (`fe2fac5`). Gamma is the identity (`gammaramp.py`), no fixed-function draw is lit. DrawVertices is mirrored (box-break shards) and the snow's point sprites are quads (`28c75be`). Compared with xemu's attract demo frame for frame (`refmatch.py`): difference 33.5 -> 20.7. Next: a playtest (Arctic Antics, the hub, other levels' fog and particles), then the water's dot-product modes, the blob shadow, the pause still. *(Playtest done: the Aku Aku/box glitch was step 49.)*
   - **Step 49 (18.52): rendering gaps, part 2.** Aku Aku's and the box's holes: `xbox_d3d8`'s vertex programs gave 0 x inf/NaN = NaN where the NV2A gives 0, and the morph program scales a garbage stream 1 by `c[122].x = 0` for unmorphed meshes (`7642510`; the user confirmed the fix in game). The water: the dot-product texture modes in the combiner shader, float4 texcoords (`00a94df`, `dcdc1a1`). Wa-Wa: his copy-sampling programs use every temp; `texel_patch` reuses one dead after the oTn writer (`dcdc1a1`). Crash's shadow: a stencil shadow whose MODULATE(TEXTURE) passes read an unbound stage as 0 (`2a3960a`). Bridge off identical. Next: a playtest (water levels and their reflections, Wa-Wa, Crash's shadow in other levels), then the pause still, load times.
   - **Step 50 (18.53): H2 Oh No and the pause still.** One bug: fixed-function draws sampled linear textures over 0..1, and the NV2A addresses them in texels. H2 Oh No's underwater pass draws its 640x480 linear target over the screen with coordinates 0..639, and the pause screen draws the CopyRects copy the same way; both showed one texel's colour. `ff_texel_scale` (`e423d47`) scales the coordinate set on the fixed-function paths. The menu vortex isn't a bug: its texture animation starts ~14 s into the title, before `-FastTitle` opens the menu. The draw log shows P8 palettes (`0979a6e`). Bridge off identical. Next: an xemu capture of H2 Oh No's start (needs someone to play there; no demo plays `water_r`), then the playtest's water levels against xemu.
   - **Step 51 (18.54): H2 Oh No matches xemu; 16:9 scoped.** H2 Oh No's water colour cycles (fog colour, ~9.5 s); one phase of the cycle matches the user's xemu screenshot to 1.7 levels, so nothing to fix. 16:9: the camera aspect at `0x242664` (0.75) sets the projection width and the frustum culling (`sub_000956B0`); widened from outside (`tools/diag/wide.py`), five places show full geometry at the edges. **Next: 16:9 implementation, the 18.54 plan:** a `sub_000956B0` wrapper scaling the aspect, a 16:9 host back buffer/window, full-width 2D stretched and the rest pillarboxed, back-buffer-sized copies; behind `RECOMP_WIDESCREEN`, off = identical.
   - **Step 52 (18.55): Sea Shell's pause crash, the pause still, the black shiny materials.** The crash (`0xFFFFFFBE` in `sub_0013AAF6`) was our APU interrupt thread running DSOUND's ISR again on a trap it had handled (the model re-raises FETINTSTS at the ISR's own write-back; the pause freed the voice in between): `718b2a3` clears a handled trap's status. The 31 kHz `[APU-OUT]` line was the crash's own interval. The black pause still: 16 render-target slots never given back; `32c35ba` releases a target with its memory (LRU at 32). Bamboozled's black ball rings, the pause crystal and gems: the host texture cache keyed by header alone thrashed between a stage's 1-level and another's 7-level copy, and each replacement unbound the stages already bound; `c97e232` keys on header and levels and fetches before binding. Draw log: copies and pixel-shader hashes (`8b5da10`). **The black first visit isn't reproduced** (5 runs): ask the user to retest on `8b5da10`. **Next: the retest, then the 16:9 plan (18.54).** *(16:9 done in 18.56; the retest answers didn't come back, so it is still open.)*

     - Cost: ~16 µs a draw, ~6% of wall time in the hub (rough). S7 wants a vertex cache for program draws.
   - **S4 part 2 (18.37, `ae718cb`): immediate mode and CopyRects are mirrored.** Splash to hub: 338,292 draws mirrored, 37,735 of them immediate, 3,300 copies, 0 failures; mirror time in the hub down to ~2.8% of the interval (rough). The menu's particles and the hub's portal glows show. The menu's planets are drawn but stay outside the view. Bridge off identical.
     - SetVertexData2f/4f only store (`D3DB_DATA_CALL`, `d3db_vertex_data`); End builds the FVF's vertices and draws them. The game's only caller is `0x38300` (DIFFUSE as R, G, B, A floats; TEXCOORD0 as u, v).
     - CopyRects: the whole back buffer into a 640x480 linear A8R8G8B8 texture, from the intro's LOADING screen on. The host keeps the copy as R8G8B8A8 for that Data. **Only vertex-program draws sample it**, so it matters from S5 on. Linear textures are addressed in texels on the NV2A, not 0-1: check what those draws pass.
     - "www.t-tales.com" is complete: 15 glyphs, FVF 0x152, all mirrored. Its uneven letters are the game's per-letter scale wave. 18.36's "TT logo glyph missing" was a misreading.
     - Texture signatures are remembered per guest frame (a 256 KB atlas hashed per draw had doubled the mirror's cost); a guest lock drops them.
   - **Fixed-function indexed draws (18.36, `0bf16db`): the LOADING text, the title animation, the menu, name entry and the hub's world now show on the real window.** 264,498 indexed draws mirrored splash to hub, 0 failures; bridge off identical.
   - **What's still missing** (`[D3DB] host draws`; `--skiptex` shows what each kind draws):
     - ~~Vertex programs~~ mirrored since 18.38; ~~those that sample the hub's CopyRects copy~~ since 18.39. No vertex-program immediates from splash to hub.
     - ~~Draws with a cube texture~~ mirrored since 18.39, but their dot-product texture modes aren't drawn (above).
     - Nothing is counted as not mirrored from splash to hub since 18.39.
   - **Fixed-function facts to build on (18.36):**
     - Lights: array at device `+0x390` (0x90 bytes each, D3DLIGHT8 first), enabled list from `+0x398` linked at `+0x8C`. Transforms at `+0x750` (XDK order), material at `+0x9F0`.
     - **Lighting is untested against the game:** no fixed-function draw from splash to hub has LIGHTING on (census `ff` key). `xbox_d3d8` lights a draw whose colours all come from the material; a vertex-colour source (the menu's setting: ambient and emissive from COLOR1) is lit on the CPU in the bridge (`cpu_light`). Check both against the first level that lights fixed-function geometry (the `[D3DB] host fixed function` line counts them). *(18.51: none does; in Arctic Antics LIGHTING is only on for XYZRHW quads, where D3D ignores it. The levels light in their vertex programs.)*
     - `xbox_d3d8` gaps worked around: lighting ignores COLORVERTEX and the material sources; DrawIndexedPrimitiveUP doesn't convert fan/quad indices and ignores MinVertexIndex; texture matrices ignore TEXTURETRANSFORMFLAGS; `fvf_texcoord_size` misreads the texture-coordinate formats; normals are always normalized.
     - Cost: the mirror is 5-6% of wall time in the hub (was 0.3-0.4%), mostly two immutable D3D11 buffers per indexed draw inside `xbox_d3d8`. Needs a ring buffer or a vertex-buffer cache by S7. *(18.37: ~2.8% in the hub, 9.9-10.7 µs a draw, after texture signatures became per frame; much of the 5-6% was hashing textures of up to 256 KB at every draw. Rough: not measured back to back. The per-draw buffers remain.)*
   - **S4 facts to build on (18.35):**
     - A host render target per guest Data: level 0 plus a same-size depth buffer; a texture with that Data samples it.
     - Two `xbox_d3d8` gaps are worked around in `twoc/`:
       - XYZRHW is mapped through the back buffer's size, so pre-transformed vertices are rescaled to the viewport;
       - MIPFILTER NONE still mips, so such a stage gets level 0 alone.
     - `translate_ps` re-encodes the XDK combiner def for `xbox_d3d8` (its field order differs in eight ways, listed in 18.35).
     - Blue-to-alpha has no route there (it's counted). It's the first candidate for a local `xbox_d3d8` fix under the 18.32 rule once a mirrored draw needs it; shader 5's use of it is harmless.
     - P8 is expanded through the stage palette (device `+0xA88`) on upload.
     - Checks:
       - `pscheck.py` (`--psprobe`): the title's 6 shaders, 24/24 exact;
       - `rtcheck.py` (`--rtlog`, `--dump-rt`): 100% within 8 in trilinear;
       - `--texcheck`: the 19 P8 textures decode correctly.
     - The executor isn't a reference for render targets or the title/menu: it rasterises swizzled targets as linear rows, and 3D as screen space.
     - Real-window grabs: `tools/diag/wgcgrab.cpp` (Windows Graphics Capture). It works while other windows cover the bridge window and takes no focus. With `drive.py --exe=` (a renamed copy) its frame counter prints -1 (18.36).
     - No texture op above 11 is used from splash to hub.
   - S3 facts to build on (18.34):
     - The state translator (`translate_rs`/`translate_tss`, `xl_*` in `d3d_bridge.c`) covers every state `xbox_d3d8` reads. Texture ops above 11 follow the XDK order from memory, not verified: each use is counted as a "texop" gap, so check the first one seen.
     - `xbox_d3d8` reads a texture argument of 0 as "default", so plain `D3DTA_DIFFUSE` on stage > 0 has no route yet (counted).
     - `xbox_d3d8` recreates 4 sampler states on every draw, and the texture signature hashes whole textures up to 256 KB. Both are cheap at S3's counts; revisit for the hub.
     - The executor point-samples and ignores vertex colour, and its dumps are mid-frame: compare with `splashcheck.py`-style references, not pairing by time.
   - S2 facts to build on (18.33):
     - One bridge lock around every host call, and a window thread that owns the window and pumps its messages.
     - Presents go through a flip-model swap chain of the bridge's own, because blt-model presents never reach the screen on this machine. `xbox_d3d8` renders into its own swap chain on a hidden window, and each present copies its back buffer across.
     - Threads: the loading screens hold `0x4EA440` for all their D3D calls, and nothing interleaves with them. No D3DX draw ran from splash to hub.
     - The gamma ramp isn't mirrored. (Render-target clears are, since 18.35.)
   - **S7 needs (measured in 18.33):**
     - A vertex cache for program draws: every draw fetches and expands its vertices on the CPU (18.38).
     - ~~A ~59.94 Hz limiter at the host present~~ done (18.40, `1ee8de2`).
     - ~~A real `MmFreeContiguousMemory`~~ done (18.34, `dd4be13`): with the executor off the arena stays at 30.5 MB over 5 menu → demo cycles.
     - ~~**A general-heap leak of 1,200 KiB (640×480×4) per level load**~~ fixed in 18.40 (`44d29e9`): worker TIBs, never freed (18.34). With the executor off, menu → demo cycles then run the 48.5 MiB heap out at ~112 s: a 1,228,848-byte request fails, then a null function-pointer loop and a stack overflow. The game never frees the block through `NtFreeVirtualMemory`. Find what allocates it.
   - Census facts to plan around (18.32):
     - render-to-texture (six 128x128 targets) every frame from the title on, and CopyRects back buffer → texture in the hub. Both move up to S4. *(Corrected by 18.35: in the title and menu the targets are drawn once, at the title load, and again at each level load; the hub draws to them every frame.)*
     - streams 0+1 from the intro on;
     - DrawVertices and the `0xFEC00` fast path never ran;
     - no readback needed;
     - VS constants (~6800 a frame in the hub) and immediate data must be buffered in the wrappers.
   - xemu references: `twoc/reference/` (the user captures them; gitignored).
   - Earlier plan details, kept for reference:
   - **Design:**
     - Native D3D keeps running.
     - 15 wrappers (`sub_X` calls the lifted `sub_X_gen` first, then `xbox_d3d8`) render from host-side state. Everything else is read at draw time from D3D's own shadows (`D3D__RenderState` `0x10EE18`, `D3D__TextureState` `0x10EC18`, the device at `0x10C110`).
     - Guest memory keeps the native XDK objects as the only representation.
     - Gated by `RECOMP_D3D_BRIDGE=1`, so the default run is unchanged.
   - **Decisions (18.31; all three taken, 18.32):**
     1. Adopt the mirror design.
     2. `xbox_d3d8` gaps: work around in `twoc/` first; local `xbox_d3d8` commits only if a translator bug has no work-around.

2. **Frame rate. Executor perf work is dropped (18.31): Option B replaces the executor.** The intro runs ~47-50 fps and the hub ~10 fps on the executor.
   - Dropped: profiling the hub's 10 fps on the executor. S7 measures fps with the bridge instead.
   - Done (18.30): `note()`/`note_unhandled()` O(1) (`fe406e4`), identical output checked on 348 M lookups; intro 23.1 → 46.9 fps.
   - Done (18.29): the per-method `getenv` is cached (`694cb98`) and the per-kick `DMA_PUT` line is rate-limited (`5012b2d`). The intro cutscene went from 6.8 to 24.0 fps, and the title without `-FastTitle` from 226 s to 129 s.
   - **Proposal 3 (hand-written KickOff): not now.** The gain is capped at the game's ~5% share. It's also unsafe: the ack thread sets GET = PUT *before* it executes a segment, so running ahead could overwrite unexecuted pushbuffer. Fixing that is a behaviour change in `xboxrecomp/`.
   - **Watch:** `[VMSTAT]` (18.42): zone live, peak, largest free range, and "to the heap" (regions the zone couldn't hold; 0 so far). *(Before 18.42: the bump high-water mark was 93.5% from the intro on, most of it no-split slack.)*

A suspended thread parks only once it owns no guest critical section. `twoc/tools/diag/step31_suspend_stress.patch` forces the old timing, to re-check the fix after a change. It was also what stopped step 30's no-executor runs at the attract demo's load.

   Earlier XPP history, superseded: **(18.16/18.17/18.18) — needs a real decision, both routes scoped and both nontrivial.** Confirmed with a real controller connected (18.17): detection works (`xbox_InputIsConnected(0)=1`), but `sub_000EB5A0(0)` still returns `slot[0]=0x00000000` because `sub_00154249`'s own lookup (`sub_001547DA` against the host-controller-context table at `0x153404`-`0x15340C`) never finds a registered controller. 18.18 scoped the "fabricate a fake table entry" shortcut and found it requires 2-3 fabricated structs, two untraced functions (`sub_00154B69`, `sub_00155C28`), and a hand-written replacement for half of `sub_001554A1` (564 bytes) to route around a transfer-completion wait that can never succeed — not meaningfully smaller than real OHCI emulation. Choose: invest in one of the two (real OHCI emulation benefits every USB path; a hand-written pipe-object builder is narrower but still nontrivial), or set input aside for now.
   Earlier Option B history, superseded by 18.31: **NV2A vertex programs / Option B (18.19/18.20/18.21).** 18.20 confirmed by direct observation that bridging `CreateDevice` alone crashes immediately (a host-shaped `xbox_d3d8` device object and the native push-buffer-shaped object every other D3D function still expects cannot coexist at the same address) -- there is no safe, additive subset to bridge one at a time; the crashing bridge was reverted, repo re-verified clean. 18.21 then scoped the resulting all-or-nothing batch over all 99 external entry points using the disassembler's full xref database: they split into CreateDevice itself (done once already), 4 shared-plumbing entries gating one object/refcount-model decision (33% of call sites), ~84 entries that become mechanical (~1 line each) once an NV097 method-number lookup table is built, and **10 large entries needing CreateDevice-class individual tracing** -- the real cost driver. Estimate: **roughly 10-15x CreateDevice's own effort**, concentrated in those 10 functions and the object-model decision. **Next actual step here, if pursued: trace the 10 Tier D-big entries (list with addresses and current best guesses at identity in 18.21 / `step19_d3d_wrapper_cluster_scoping.md`) and resolve the Tier B object-model question -- both still open, neither started.** Otherwise, explicitly weigh setting this aside (it's now sized well enough to make that call on purpose rather than by default).
